  names where each version is actually pinned. Everything else in that population verified clean and
  is recorded rather than left implied: all six SHA-pin `# vX.Y.Z` tag comments resolve to exactly
  the pinned SHA against the GitHub API (control: a deliberately wrong tag returns a different SHA,
  so the check discriminates); `Set up Node 20` matches `node-version: '20'` and the channel server's
  `engines: ">=20"`; `Set up PHP 8.5` and the `setup-app` description match its own `php-version`
  pin; the `changelog-gate.yml` comment's PHP 8.5 is current; and `PHPUnit (MariaDB ${{
  matrix.mariadb }})` interpolates the matrix, which is the shape that cannot drift. Version strings
  in historical or measurement prose (`v0.12`, `v0.72.0`, the `0.7.1`/`0.8.3` lock measurement) are
  frozen-original records, not restatements of a live pin, and are untouched. **A second, different
  stale claim in the same two comment blocks, found by re-reading rather than by the version grep:**
  both the workflow header and `CLAUDE_TESTING.md` described the `Laravel Tests` workflow as running
  *"three checks"* and enumerated Pint / PHPStan / PHPUnit — omitting the `bin/check-doc-refs.php`
  doc-sync step, which has been a fourth check in the SQLite job since it was added. Both now say
  four and name it. Corrected in the same change because a comment being rewritten should not ship
  still miscounting the steps directly beneath it. **Still open, still gated:** the coordinated
  rename. DL-040's annotation carries the recommendation that it drop the
  version from the check name entirely rather than move it to `8.5`, so the branch-protection op is
  paid once instead of at every PHP upgrade — a recommendation, not a decision.
- **card#6101** (**DL-281**) — **`changelog-gate.yml` stops silently exempting a PR whose in-scope
  paths are all non-ASCII-named.** `core.quotepath` defaults ON, so `git diff --name-only` prints any
  path carrying a non-ASCII byte as a C-quoted string with a **leading `"`** — which defeats the `^`
  in the feature-PR step's scope predicate. Measured: a commit touching only `app/café.php` matched
  the predicate **0** times; the same diff read under `git -c core.quotepath=false` matched **1**
  (control: `app/plain.php` matched under both). The gate did not error — it printed *"OK: this PR
  changes no app/, bin/, or .github/workflows/ file"* and exited 0, a false claim about the diff.
  **⚠ THIS CHANGES WHAT CI REQUIRES:** the gate now fires on PRs it previously let through. It was
  user-gated before any code. **The reach was narrow, and this entry does not oversell the fix** —
  the miss needed *every* in-scope path in the PR to be non-ASCII-named; one ASCII `app/`, `bin/`, or
  `.github/workflows/` file anywhere in the diff always fired the gate. The flag is set at the
  **capture** in both steps rather than at either predicate, so a future predicate change *in this
  workflow* is safe by construction; the release step's `grep -qxF VERSION` was never exposed (an
  ASCII literal is never quoted) and its half of the change is deliberately untestable. **Sibling
  audit run, one instance:** three `git diff --name-only` call sites exist in this repo's automation
  and only the one is exposed — the release step's fixed-string `-x` match and
  `channel-server-supply-chain.yml`'s unanchored `grep -v '/node_modules/'` exclusion are both
  provably unaffected by quoting. Residual, stated rather than claimed closed: a path containing
  `"`, `\`, or a control byte is C-quoted regardless of this flag. `-z` closes that residual too and
  was rejected on cost — **DL-281 § Alternatives considered (a) owns that reasoning and what the
  rejected option would have bought; deliberately not restated here.**

## [0.73.0] - 2026-08-07

### Fixed
- **Docs stop restating the dev-PR gate model — and G-002's drifted recipe no longer targets a
  live database (card#5913).** `CLAUDE_CONVENTIONS.md` § Workflow, `VERSIONING.md`'s wait-for-CI
  release step, and `CLAUDE_TESTING.md`'s pre-merge sentence carried second copies of the gate model
  `CLAUDE.md` rule 5 owns; the copies had drifted (retired ask-before-open steps, the card#5575
  named-workflow list, a hand-tag instruction `auto-tag-version.yml` would race). Each is now a
  pointer to its owner. `CLAUDE_GOTCHAS.md` G-002's "run MariaDB locally" block — a drifted copy
  of the `CLAUDE_TESTING.md` throwaway-container recipe — pointed `phpunit` at
  `agent_webhook_bridge_dev`, the live dev install's DB, which `RefreshDatabase` would have
  dropped; it now points at the owner recipe with an explicit never-target-a-live-install warning.

### Added
- **CI asserts the WHOLE declared release-artifact set at release-PR time (card#5910, DL-279).**
  A new `release-artifacts-gate.yml` runs the toolkit's SHA-pinned `release-artifacts` action,
  which takes its members from `.release-pr.json`'s `artifacts` array — the declaration that until
  now had exactly one consumer, the release-PR body generator, which merely PRINTED it as a
  checklist. A release PR missing the **`CLAUDE.md § Recent releases` row** — the one declared
  member no gate read, so its absence could not fail any signal — now fails instead of shipping
  silently. Members come from the declaration, so a fourth is guarded by DECLARING it rather than
  by remembering to extend a script.
  **⚠ THIS CHANGES WHAT CI ACCEPTS** on release PRs; it was user-gated on card#5910 before any
  code. It **composes with** `changelog-gate.yml` rather than replacing it — that gate still owns
  the release-body size limit and the per-PR `[Unreleased]` entry — and `auto-tag-version.yml`
  stays fail-soft post-merge by design.
- **The silent-drop guard the docs have promised since v0.12 now exists (card#6025, DL-278).**
  The dispatcher WARNS when a classifier emits a `channel_push` ReactionTarget whose `targetId`
  equals no `Intent.subjectId` in the same `ClassifyResult` — a wake fired with no durable inbox
  backstop behind it, which until now was exactly the silent drop the operator could not see.
  Implemented per the **ratified card#2014 acceptance criterion**, translated to the PHP model
  (the pairing partner is the Intent, not the Python-era `log_intent` target — that handler is
  forensic-only and `bridge:inbox` never reads it).
  **No dispatch-outcome change: warn only.** Never a throw, never a delivery/drop flip. Thirteen
  places in the repo already presupposed this guard existed — eight operator-facing doc claims,
  two source docblocks and three test comments; none of them was true until now, and the two that
  ALSO overstated it (a warn *ensures* nothing, and does not *pair* anything — the classifier
  does) are corrected in the same change.
  **Default-ON**, via the already-parsed `surface.silent_drop_warnings` (default `true`) — a
  config key that had zero consumers until now. **Opt out per agent** with
  `surface: {silent_drop_warnings: false}`; no new config, no schema change.
  **Blast radius: no shipped classifier can warn.** Both `channel_push` construction sites in
  `app/` — `InboxOnlyClassifier::wakePush()` and the `route_intents` synthesis — build the target
  FROM the intent, so they are paired by construction; the nine agent configs on the reference
  install (5 prod, 4 dev) all take the shipped classifiers and the `true` default, and none can
  emit an unpaired push.
  **Warn volume, stated plainly:** once per agent+`target_id` **within one event**, and therefore
  once **per redelivery attempt** — a durable-handler throw after the check 5xxes and the
  redelivery warns again. There is deliberately **no cross-request dedup** (it would need new
  cross-request state); a busy stream re-emitting the same unpaired target warns once per
  delivery until the classifier is fixed or the agent opts out.
- **CI reads `docs/CHANGELOG.md` before a merge for the first time: `changelog-gate.yml`
  (card#5910, card#5971, card#5972, DL-276).** One workflow, two assertions. **(1)** A PR that
  moves `VERSION` must carry a matching `## [<VERSION>]` section, and that section must fit
  GitHub's 125,000-byte release-body limit. **(2)** A PR touching `app/` or `bin/` must be named
  in the `[Unreleased]` section — by its title's `card#`/`DL-` token, or, for a PR carrying no
  such token, by having changed the section at all. `dependabot/*`, `release/*`, `sync/*` and
  `revert-*` branches are exempt (the same set `pr-title-lint.yml` uses); docs-only and
  test-only PRs are exempt by the path scope rather than by a carve-out.
  **⚠ THIS CHANGES WHAT CI ACCEPTS** — it can red a PR that would previously have merged. It was
  user-gated on all three cards before any code.
  **Why:** `docs/CHANGELOG.md` had exactly one reader, `auto-tag-version.yml`, and only *after*
  the merge to `main`. Measured 2026-08-05 with a controlled instrument: **25 of 56 tokened
  commits reached v0.72.0 undocumented**, each absent from the whole file, against 100% coverage
  on the three prior releases. A missing release section published a generated placeholder over
  the gap rather than failing.

### Changed
- **The draft overlay stops taking a PR title's word for which card it may pin (card#5953).**
  **⚠ THIS CHANGES WHAT THE BRIDGE ACCEPTS** — a `block_reason` overlay that lands today stops
  landing. It was user-gated before any code, as option (a) on the card.
  DL-270 made the head branch outrank the PR title for `card#NNNN` and settled the residual —
  a title-only token the branch does not back — with a **card-side corroboration gate** in
  `KanbanMoveCardHandler`. The **draft overlay** shared the resolver, so it inherited the
  title-vs-branch *rule*, but its `kanban_block_reason` target carried no flag, so it did not
  inherit the *gate*: a draft PR whose title descriptively cited another card on the same mapped
  board still wrote the `"PR is in draft"` marker onto that card — **pinning it** against the
  branch-cut auto-promote (DL-178) on the strength of prose alone. DL-270 recorded that as a
  known-open gap rather than widening what the bridge accepts unasked; this closes it.
  A **SET** now refuses an uncorroborated title-only `card#` unless the card **tracks no PR yet
  or already tracks this PR** — the same predicate, extracted to one shared
  `App\Bridge\Writeback\CardTokenCorroboration` that both handlers call, never a second copy.
  The refusal logs and (with an `alert_channel`) signals as `card_token_uncorroborated` under the
  synthetic `draft_overlay` outcome. **A CLEAR is deliberately NOT gated:** clear-if-ours can only
  null a `block_reason` that exactly equals our own marker — a human's differing text is
  untouchable (bounded by the constant-sentinel ambiguity `docs/writeback.md` documents) — and
  gating it would strand any marker on a card that now tracks a different PR, including those set
  before this shipped, the guard permanently pinning the card it exists to protect. Accepted
  residual: a foreign PR's `ready_for_review` can clear the marker (releasing the DL-178 pin)
  that another PR's draft set. A corroborated overlay target's payload is **byte-identical** to
  before (the flag and the event `pr_number` ride together and only on the residual), so ordinary
  `<type>/<id>-slug` work is untouched.
- **`auto-tag-version.yml` can no longer leave a release tagged but unpublished (card#5972,
  DL-276).** v0.72.0's CHANGELOG section was 134,904 bytes against GitHub's 125,000 limit, so the
  publish step — the *last* step, after the tag had already been pushed — returned `HTTP 422:
  body is too long` and the Release was created by hand. The step now falls back to a
  line-truncated body ending in a pointer to `docs/CHANGELOG.md` at the tag. **Fail-soft on an
  *absent* section is unchanged and deliberate** (failing there would leave a merged release
  untagged); the loud half of that case is the PR-time gate above.
- **Both workflows extract CHANGELOG sections through one shared `bin/changelog-section.py`
  (DL-276)**, so the gate measures the body the publisher would actually ship. Byte-identical to
  the awk one-liner it replaces for any section that fits, so published notes do not change
  shape. The size limit is enforced in **bytes**: GitHub's 422 says "characters" and documents no
  unit, and byte length is `>=` both the code-point and UTF-16 counts, so it is conservative
  under every reading. Correction to card#5972's own figure — 134,904 is the section's **byte**
  count; its character count is 133,906.
- **The `bridge:check` event-consumer renderer drops its two unreachable `'unknown'` last-seen
  fallback arms (card#5555).** Output-neutral: `received_at` is `timestamp(3)` NOT NULL with a
  DB-side default and no app code ever writes it (not fillable — every row takes the default),
  so the reconciler's `MAX(received_at)` over a non-empty group is always a scalar timestamp
  string and neither arm could ever fire. Both were inherited by the DL-242
  stage-7a migration, which deliberately carried them unchanged (deleting a branch that never
  fires is output-neutral, which is exactly why a byte-identical stage must not do it) and filed
  the deletion as its own decision; taken now as option (a) on the card.

### Fixed
- **The two silences DL-273 shipped named — and left open as card#5961 — are closed, each at the
  surface where its own statement is true.** **(1) The CI PR-title lint grows a DL arm.** Its
  DL-234 warn step probed only the CARD stem, so `DL-<U+0663>239` warned on **neither** surface:
  the runtime near-miss probe's digit class is ASCII by ratification (DL-231, not reopened), and
  the card stem survives that only because that step names the non-ASCII-digit class **by name**
  to the author. A `looks_dl=`/`good_dl=` pair now sits in the SAME step at the SAME severity —
  a `::warning::`, exit 0 pinned (DL-234(c) settled the severity; the precedent for shipping a
  warn-leg widening on the record is DL-250's own `looks=` widening — exit code pinned to 0, no
  accept/reject change — while DL-250(e) retracted "it changes what a CI check reports" as a
  gating discriminator and issued no gating verdict either way) — with the DL arm's operator text naming that class by name. **No accept/reject
  change anywhere:** the require step, the correlation grammars and the runtime probe are
  untouched; only which non-correlating title/branch draws a CI annotation changes. Tied the way
  the card arm is: `good_dl` answer-set-to-answer-set against `DlTokenGrammar` in three
  renderings, and `looks_dl` against the RUNTIME probe over the **union** of both engines'
  separator domains, which is what makes it bidirectional (DL-250(4)) — measured, not argued: a
  separator removed from the shared `NearMissProbe::SEPARATORS` alone reds it, and so does one
  added to the YAML class alone. **Disclosed, not buried — the arm's reach is bounded by its own
  whole-subject predicate, and the bound is measured, not argued.** The step probes title+branch
  as ONE subject, and each arm greps only its own accept pattern — so suppression is
  whole-subject **per stem**: any parsed `DL-NNN` anywhere suppresses the DL arm for the whole
  subject, while a parsed card token does not (both directions asserted in PrTitleLintTest).
  Driven over the 400 most recent PRs (review measurement, 2026-08-06): `looks_dl` matches 169
  subjects and `good_dl` suppresses **every one** — in particular, every historical glued-`dlNNN`
  branch (`feat/dl189-…`, `docs/dl204-…`) sat beside a well-spelled `DL-NNN` in its PR title,
  so the arm fires **zero** times on that history. The branch-only glued miss DL-273 measured is
  therefore the RUNTIME half's to catch: a branch-create push reaches the classifier as a bare
  ref, where the DL-273 near-miss probe warns when no card token parses, and the new stamp-site
  warn (below) does when one does — the latter fires on 2 of the same 400
  (`card-5156b-dl232-…`, `feat/card-4322-dl199-…`). What the arm actually catches is a subject
  whose EVERY DL spelling is malformed — the non-ASCII-digit `DL-<U+0663>239` class above being
  the one no other surface names, and its reason to exist — plus one FALSE-POSITIVE shape both
  arms share: `dl` at a token boundary then a separator and **no** digit (`fix/dl-parser`;
  neither arm's `looks` needs a digit after the separator, so neither can tell that from a real
  miss). DL-250(e) ruled that shape a separate defect and left it surfaced-not-fixed; with a
  second arm now carrying it, the class is tracked as **card#6028** (12 of the same 400 draw the
  card arm's warning purely from it). **Both warn lines' operator text is corrected to per-token
  claims in the same change.** Because suppression is per-stem, the previous "so NO card will
  move" clause was false whenever the OTHER stem's token correlated: of the shipped card-arm
  line's 12 fires on the same corpus, **10** carried a parsing `DL-NNN` in the same subject — a
  card could move by DL resolution while the line said none would (the mirror shape on the new
  DL arm — a parsing card token beside a malformed DL — is exactly the lost-stamp subject below).
  Both lines now claim only what is true of the token itself ("it correlates to nothing: no card
  will move on this token"), the same what-is-true-HERE discipline the stamp-site warning
  states. **(2) A lost `dl_number` stamp stops being
  silent.** A subject
  like `feat/card-3410-slug-DL_272` moves card 3410 and stamps **no** `dl_number`, because
  `DlTokenGrammar::sole()` returns null on the malformed token. The near-miss probe is
  deliberately silent there and **stays** so — it sits behind the whole-subject guard (DL-234(e))
  and its own *"no move"* clause would be false about a subject whose card DOES move — so the
  signal is emitted at the STAMP site instead, as a **distinct** warning saying what is true
  there: the card moved, and it is unstamped. A lost STAMP and a lost MOVE are two signals at two
  sites; neither is the other reused, and the pin that reds if the near-miss half is ever closed
  by accident is still in place (updated, not deleted — this closure was by decision).
  **Log-only; zero change to moves, stamps or exit paths.** Proven failable by mutation, each
  applied-and-asserted, run, and restored from a snapshot: deleting the stamp-site emission reds
  **3** classifier legs (the two lost-stamp legs plus the DL-234(e) pin); dropping its
  `parse() === null` clause reds 3, two of them PRE-EXISTING DL-win legs; removing the CI arm's
  `if`/`echo` while leaving both regexes in place reds the four end-to-end legs (the DL
  shape-sweep, the non-ASCII-digit naming leg, the severity pin, and the cross-stem suppression
  leg — end-to-end since its assertions moved onto the arm's own message text) and **neither
  tie**, which is why the end-to-end legs exist at all; widening `good_dl` to accept the plural
  `DLs-239` reds the grammar tie; dropping `looks_dl`'s `s?` or its space-then-hash separator reds
  the probe tie; and making the DL arm `exit 1` **after** it warns — so the warning still prints
  and only the exit code moves — reds exactly one leg, the severity pin, which is the control that
  keeps a `::warning::` from silently becoming a merge gate.
- **Three false code-state claims in comments/docs corrected (card#5952; no behavior change).**
  Found by the card#5289 comment-truthfulness audit. **(1)** `CoordinationClassifier`'s
  no-self-wake docblock called the dependabot handler "the bridge's only card-CREATE path" —
  there are three (the dependabot handler, the DL-198 coord-card handler, the DL-217
  `board_create_card` tool). All three write through the one writeback client as the
  `identity_id` user, so the global-echo gate covers them all when it is configured; the
  `triaged` tag is an independent second layer only the dependabot and poll-backstop paths
  have. The docblock now states that structure (correction entry DL-277 — the stale claim
  originated in DL-168, true when written). **(2)** The `board_create_card` reserved-tag guard
  justified its casefold with "the kanban tag search is case-insensitive (`_ci` collation)" on
  seven surfaces (tool docblock + three inline comments, `docs/board-tools.md`, two test
  docblocks) — the MariaDB JSON column collates `utf8mb4_bin` (case-SENSITIVE, measured); the
  correct justification is defense-in-depth across driver collations plus bridge-side
  determinism, and all seven surfaces now state it. The guard's behavior was already correct — only its stated
  reason was wrong. **(3)** The reference channel server cited a README note by a title it never
  had ("One server per session" → "One server per UDS path").
- **`bridge:check` certifies the board-tools ssh transport even when the kanban writeback client
  cannot be constructed (card#5474, DL-275).** The board-tools client envelope skipped three
  slots on a construction failure; two of them — the offline pinned-line probe
  (`SshPinnedLineCheck`) and the DL-225 flipped-default advisory — read nothing from that client.
  A missing or unreadable writeback token therefore silently disarmed the ssh security
  certification: a forced-command `authorized_keys` line granting `pty` exits **1** with the
  token present and exited **0**, printing nothing about ssh, without it. The envelope now skips
  the board-**state** legs only (they do read the client). **⚠ This can change an exit code:** an
  install with an enabled ssh `board_tools` block, no resolvable writeback token, and a bad
  pinned line goes from exit 0 to exit **1** — surfacing a real failure that was previously
  hidden, not a new rule. Installs that construct a client are unaffected and their output is
  unchanged. Note that `SshLiveProbeCheck`, the heavier opt-in live round-trip, already ran
  outside this guard; the offline probe needing strictly less was the one gated on it.
- **`bridge:check` reads `shared-identities.json` once per run instead of up to three times
  (card#5546).** The registry derivation read it, and `SharedIdentitiesCheck` read it twice more —
  once raw for its DL-259 absent/unreadable/malformed/parsed discrimination and once through the
  loader for the count. The loader LOGS, so on a file that parses as an object but carries an entry
  with no numeric `github_user_id` the run emitted the same per-entry warning **twice**, and an
  operator tailing the log (or an alert counting the line) read one install fault as two. The read
  now happens once in the derivation and is published on `CheckContext` as a state object the check
  consumes; `AgentRegistry::loadSharedIdentities()` keeps its exact fail-soft list contract for the
  receiver. **Stdout and every exit code are byte-identical** — which is why the acceptance evidence
  is a log-count assertion watched to red against the pre-fix code, not a green golden suite. Two
  log-count deltas otherwise: on an install where *no* agent YAML parsed, an unreadable or malformed
  file now warns once where it warned not at all (the check has always reported that install; the
  read reaching it is what the report was missing).

### Security
- **league/commonmark bumped 2.8.3 → 2.9.0 for GHSA-29pj-957v-52mc (#496).** Routine dependency
  bumps in the same release: symfony/yaml 8.1.1 → 8.1.2 (#470), laravel/pao 1.1.2 → 1.1.3 (#471),
  laravel/pint 1.29.3 → 1.30.0 (#469, dev-only).

## [0.72.0] - 2026-08-05

**Minor — `bridge:check` becomes a machine-readable, self-accounting diagnostic as the DL-242 check registry lands stages 0–10; the writeback stops letting a PR title outrank the branch the PR is work on; and `board_my_cards` can finally carry a card's scope to the seat implementing it.** 65 PRs since v0.71.0 (#413–#484). **The receiver is untouched — zero changes under `app/Http/` or `app/Bridge/Adapters/`, so HMAC verification, dedup, envelope adaptation and what the receiver accepts or rejects are unchanged; no migration (zero files added or changed under `database/migrations/`) and no `.env.example` change.** The behavior that does move is concentrated in the `bridge:check` reporting vocabulary, the PR→card correlation precedence, and the board-tools projection — each called out below.

**⚠ UPGRADING — four changes alter behavior on an install that configured nothing:** **(1) card#5287, DL-270** — when a PR's title and its head branch disagree about `card#NNNN`, **the branch now wins**, and a disagreeing title token is refused and warned. Previously the title won *by position* in the matched string, so a descriptive citation of another card in a PR title could silently move that other card. **(2) card#5291 / card#5292, DL-251** — 21 `bridge:check` findings move onto the `unvalidated` severity under a stated rule. **(3) card#5585, DL-248** — every `bridge:check` run gains one inventory line and the `unvalidated` tally is reworded (33 golden fixtures regenerated). (2) and (3) change what a run PRINTS; **neither changes an exit code.** **(4)** a consumer that greps `CARD_TOKEN_PATTERN` will red — **the grammar did not change, only its name and location**; the section immediately below owns that one in full.

### ⚠ Upgrading — a consumer that greps `CARD_TOKEN_PATTERN` will red, and the grammar did NOT change
- **The card-token regex is byte-identical to v0.71.0; its NAME and LOCATION moved.** v0.71.0 carried
  `private const CARD_TOKEN_PATTERN` in `app/Bridge/Classifiers/GitHubPrCardMoveClassifier.php`; DL-250
  extracted it to **`app/Bridge/Support/CardTokenGrammar.php`** as `private const PATTERN`, and the
  classifier now calls `CardTokenGrammar::parse()`. The value
  (`/\bcard(?|[-#](\d+)|(\d{2,}))/i`) is **unchanged** — correlation behaviour is untouched.
- **Consequence, reported by a downstream operator before release rather than discovered after:** any
  external check that reads the grammar by grepping the classifier for `CARD_TOKEN_PATTERN` finds
  nothing on upgrade and — if it is built correctly — **fails closed**, reporting something like
  *"wrong file, or the bridge renamed it; refusing to certify against a source it cannot read."*
  **That red means RE-POINT the check, not "correlation changed."** The new home is the one named
  above.
- **No alias is retained, deliberately.** A back-compat `CARD_TOKEN_PATTERN` would re-create a second
  reference to the grammar in the classifier — precisely the restatement DL-250 removed, and the shape
  that let a stale copy drift in the first place. A fail-closed red on a relocated source is correct
  behaviour; it is announced here so it costs a lookup instead of an investigation.
- **This is a courtesy note, NOT a contract change — and the distinction is load-bearing.** That
  constant was `private` in v0.71.0 and always had been, so **no supported surface changed** and
  nothing here creates an expectation that internals hold still. It is written down only because a
  consumer had built a grep against it and a silent relocation would have cost them an investigation.
  **The supported way to read this grammar is the public seam `CardTokenGrammar::parse()`** — driving a
  corpus through it ties a consumer to *behaviour* rather than to a source string, and survives any
  future move. A check coupled to a private declaration in another repo will red on that repo's routine
  refactors; that is a property of the coupling, not a defect in the refactor.

### Added
- **card#5596, DL-266** — **A check must DECLARE its silence: the deliberate empty yield stops being inferred.** **Text output is byte-identical on every install whose silent paths are declared — which is all of them today (84/84 goldens green, no regeneration). No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change, no exit-code change.** DL-242 stage 8 made a check's silence visible and counted; it could not say what that silence MEANT — a check that looked and correctly had nothing to report and a check **falling off the end of its generator through a path its author did not intend** produced the identical `Silent` row. Stage 8 disclosed that bound as open and filed it; this closes it. **A deliberate silence is a `App\Bridge\Check\Silence::because('<reason>')` yielded on the path that produced it**, `CheckRunner::materialize()` is the ONLY strip site (so no renderer can be handed one to print — pinned by a test), and an execution yielding neither a finding nor a declaration is named in **`CheckInventory::undeclaredSilent()`** and disclosed at runtime on the existing `bridge:check internal:` channel. Same direction as the not-run accounting: the runner trusts a **declaration**, never an inference from an empty yield. **⚠ The card's own recommended shape — a second contract method, by analogy with `OptInCheck::wasRequested()` — was REJECTED with the reasons written on the class:** `wasRequested()` is a constructor-time fact about the INVOCATION, correct however many times `run()` is called, while *"was this silence deliberate"* is a fact about the EXIT PATH of ONE execution, so a method would need per-execution mutable state on an object `PerAgentCheck::runFor()` re-invokes per agent — **the exact shape stage 8 rejected for the not-run accounting** ("correctness depending on remembering to call it, which is the same shape as the bug it closes"). The card's third option, a `return;`-keyed static analyser, is **structurally blind**: the commonest silent path here has no `return` at all — a `foreach` over an empty collection — the same blindness DL-251's sweep had when it keyed on the sites that REPORT. **The idiom is a TRAILING UNCONDITIONAL yield, and that is what makes the tripwire survive:** a declaration states what a silence *would mean*, not "I am silent", so the runner IGNORES it when findings were also yielded — and **an early `return;` added later sits BEFORE the trailing declaration, skips it, and is recorded undeclared**, so a new silent path cannot inherit an old path's judgement. **No new `CheckDisposition` case and no new `Severity`** — declaredness rides ALONGSIDE the disposition exactly as `notRunReasons` rides alongside `NotRun`, so `--format=json`'s four `disposition` values, the exhaustive `match` and the exit contract are untouched **by construction**. **`undeclaredSilent()` is recorded per EXECUTION**, the one accessor on `CheckInventory` that is not a fold over `$dispositions`, and that **closes** the per-agent granularity bound rather than disclosing it: a per-agent check that reports for agent A and is undeclared-silent for agent B ends the run `Reported`, so the `unexplainedNotRun()` shape would have hidden precisely the execution that went unjudged (mutation-proven). **⚠ ONE DELIBERATE DEVIATION from the card's byte-neutrality clause: `inventory.undeclared_silent` is added to `--format=json`** — `unexplained_not_run` is already there, and a machine consumer blind to an internal defect the text consumer can see is this program's own recurring defect re-minted at the new surface; per [`docs/check-json-contract.md`](check-json-contract.md) §2 an added key does not bump `schema`, which **stays 1**. **The second `bridge:check internal:` warn IS reachable on a real install, unlike the first, and the stale docblock claiming otherwise was corrected in the same change** — every conditional slot records a not-run reason by design, so `unexplainedNotRun()` cannot fire on any real install, but **whether a given silent path executes is a fact about the OPERATOR'S install**. No fixture reaches it because every path the corpus exercises is declared, which is **asserted** (`CheckJsonContractTest` pins the list empty), not assumed. **What a declared silence does NOT establish, stated because an unstated bound reads as a guarantee: it says the author INTENDED the silence, never that it is CORRECT** — a floor on the unjudged paths, not a ceiling on the wrong ones. **The forced review over all 37 check bodies found NO wrong path, reported rather than rounded up** — the card predicted ≥1. What it did produce: a trailing declaration written on `ChannelSnapshotCheck` claiming `ChannelSnapshotProbe::probe()` can return nothing was **false** (every path returns ≥1 finding; an undeclared `server_path` is answered with `unvalidated`) and was removed with a comment saying why — **a declaration on a path that cannot be silent is a false statement about the code**, and it is the failure mode a bulk sweep produces, as a scripted docblock widen proved by over-reaching onto five private helpers (caught and reverted per-method: a bulk regex over docblocks needs a per-method control). `BoardToolsHttpProbeCheck`'s defensive `continue` is **deliberately left undeclared** — unreachable by construction, so the run SHOULD disclose rather than have a declaration vouch for an impossible state. **⚠ The mechanism broke 110 assertions across 22 test files, and the duplication that let it is its own finding (canon #7):** every per-check unit test ended in its **own copy** of `iterator_to_array($check->run($ctx), false)` — **43 sites across 30 files** — harmless while checks yielded only `Finding`s, wrong at all 43 at once the moment one could yield a `Silence`. **A filtered run is structurally incapable of seeing it**; the whole-suite run is what named it, the same measurement lesson stage 9 recorded. **Fixed at the primitive, and the tempting fix was rejected:** a test-local strip helper would have been a **second implementation of `CheckRunner::materialize()`**, whose entire safety argument is that it is the ONLY strip site — so `tests/Support/MaterializesChecks` drives each check through a **real `CheckRunner`** and returns what the runner built, making the tests measure the production path rather than an imitation of it. All 43 converted, including the ~14 still green (leaving them would leave the defect armed for the day a declaration reached one of their paths); the sweep script **refused** 3 it could not parse rather than mangling them. **Evidence:** the positive control is the only reason the green means anything — removing the trailing declaration from `AgentIdentityCollisionsCheck` reds **35 of 84 `CheckGoldenTest` cases** (49 pass), every one naming `agent.identity_collisions` verbatim in the diff, restored `cmp` byte-identical; three further single-arm mutations (one mutation, one named test, restore + `cmp` between each) each RED against their named test, pristine negative control green. **Whole suite 2003/2003 (5983 assertions)**, phpstan L7 0, pint clean, check-doc-refs clean. The audited population is every check body, **re-derived per pass by command with three controls and never quoted** — the first form of that derivation omitted `OptInCheck` and silently under-counted by two.
- **card#5229, DL-249** — **`bridge:check --format=json`: the run as a versioned, machine-readable document — and the event-consumer reconciliation becomes DATA rather than a sentence.** **Additive and opt-in: absent `--format=json`, output is byte-identical (33/33 goldens green, no regeneration). No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change, and no change to the exit code in either format.** The plan's stage-9 row named one deliverable and the stage owed **two**: the observed-vs-consumed reconciliation was computed as **locals inside `EventFollowsConsumerCheck` and thrown away**, so a generic renderer over the existing report would emit its prose as JSON strings and a consumer would still be parsing prose — which breaks toward a **false clean** (a parser that matches nothing reports nothing), and DL-236 had already reworded findings in this command once. `EventConsumerReconciler` / `EventConsumerReconciliation` / `EventConsumerScope` extract the derivation **whole**; the check becomes a **renderer only**. **One derivation behind two front doors** — a standalone `bridge:consumed-events` was considered and rejected because it has no inventory to carry the per-check `not-requested` half the resolved opt-in-probe decision owes, so it would have closed the card, left the plan row open, and cost a second write contract and a second guard for one known consumer (DL-223 `BoardToolDispatcher` precedent). **The document (`schema: 1`):** `schema · ok · checks[] · findings_outside_registry[] · inventory · event_consumers{error, scopes[]}` — per-check **dispositions** (incl. `not-requested` **by id**, which the text line only counts), findings with **agent attribution** (an attribution the text report has nowhere to put), the exact inventory, and the reconciliation with its **derived** `unconsumed` / `unlisted_actions` so no consumer re-implements the projection rule. **It is a WRITE CONTRACT, which is why the stage was gated** — versioned, guarded by `CheckJsonContractTest` asserting the **exact key set at every level** with `assertSame` against literals (a *contains* assertion greens on a renamed key, and a renamed key is the change that breaks a consumer silently), and owned by the new **[`docs/check-json-contract.md`](check-json-contract.md)** so the shape has exactly one written copy. **An added key is deliberately not a version bump** (consumers must ignore unknown keys); **`message` strings are excluded from the contract by name** — they are operator prose and a consumer keying on them re-creates the coupling this surface exists to break. **The four fail-soft envelopes in `handle()` now emit captured `Finding`s** (`findings_outside_registry`): two of them set the exit code, and omitting them would produce a document showing every check clean with `ok: false` and nothing naming the cause — a machine-readable false clean, this program's own defect re-minted at the new surface. **The reconciler's failure is a FIELD, not an exception channel:** an empty `scopes` with a null `error` is a clean install, with a non-null `error` it is a measurement that did not happen, and a fail-soft producer returning only its successes hands every consumer an empty list reading as "nothing to report". **The exit contract is untouched by construction:** only the RENDER arm is format-gated — the tally and the `fail`-only return run either way — so the document's `ok` and the exit code are literally one variable, asserted per install shape against the committed **text** capture. **An unknown `--format` fails closed** (exit 1, no report of either kind) rather than falling back to text. **⚠ Bounds stated in the contract doc rather than left to production:** under `set -e` an unhealthy install still exits non-zero before you read the document (read `ok`, not `$?`); the fail-closed `--format` diagnostic goes to **stdout**, so unparseable stdout is a state a consumer must handle (stated, not fixed — moving it changes how the command reports an error, which is gated); and the `warn` ↔ `unvalidated` assignment is still imprecise, so **absence of `unvalidated` is not evidence a leg was measured** until card#5291/#5292 land. **Evidence:** **whole suite 1861/1861 (5316 assertions)**, within it `CheckJsonContractTest` 13/13 · `EventConsumerReconcilerTest` 7/7 · `EventFollowsConsumerCheckTest` 19/19 · `CheckGoldenTest` 76/76; phpstan L7 0, pint clean, check-doc-refs clean; real-surface run against a live install (exit 0, one parseable document, 37 registered / 31 ran / 15 reported / 16 silent / 2 not-requested / 4 not-run); **10/10 stage mutations witnessed — M9 came back GREEN**, because dropping per-agent result retention left the whole suite passing (the document's `agent` attribution was asserted nowhere), closed by a named test and re-run RED. **Two test defects the whole-suite run exposed, both present in the first stage-9 commit and both invisible to a per-file run:** (a) `CheckJsonContractTest` leaked a **pinned `PATH`** into the rest of the process — `PinnedHost` saves the ambient environment in its **constructor**, so a second `build()` inside one test method saved the already-pinned value as ambient and restored the process to it, redding **15 tests across three unrelated suites** that shell out (`sh: 1: bash: not found`, exit 127); fixed structurally by tearing down at the start of a build, so nesting is unrepresentable. (b) The skipped-advisory severity test reached its fail-soft state via a throw from the check's own query, which stage 9 moved into the reconciler — behaviour intact (verified at source first), the failure is now handed in as a value, every assertion unchanged. **Neither is reachable by a `--filter` run: both are cross-test.** **One renderer defect the contract doc itself exposed, fixed before first ship:** `not_run_reason` was emitted **for checks that RAN** — a reason is attached to a whole slot before the run knows whether that slot will execute, so a per-agent check noted for the agents that aborted and then run for one that did not came back as `"disposition": "reported"` with a finding **and** *"every parsed agent aborted before this leg"*, which a consumer would read as *did not run*. Gated on the disposition (which `CheckInventory::notRunReasons()`/`unexplainedNotRun()` already do — the JSON renderer was the one reader of the raw map that did not); the pre-existing assertion **passed under the defect** because its subject had no reason recorded at all, so it is replaced by one that drives the renderer with an inventory where every id carries a reason. **Docs:** two false claims corrected — `JSON_PRETTY_PRINT`'s rationale cited a fixture directory **that does not exist**, and stage 7a's `@phpstan-impure` docblock claim was re-measured on an **unmodified `dev` checkout** and found already stale for two stages (removing the annotation leaves phpstan at 0 errors; the annotation is KEPT, since deleting it on an unidentified substitute is how the guard comes back dead) — recorded as the plan's § Disproved claims item 9. **card#5687** lands with it: the plan doc's header **enumerated which stages are built**, a moving value its own § Staged execution table owns, and it drifted through six merged PRs invisibly to grep (`CLAUDE.md`'s row was correct the whole time, so the search returns a good copy and a stale one) — cured by **deletion, not re-synchronisation**, on both surfaces.
- **card#5384, DL-245** — **`board_my_cards` gains an opt-in `include_description`, so a card can finally carry its scope to the seat that has to implement it.** **Absent, both keys are ABSENT rather than null — the default response stays byte-identical to the DL-217 shape. No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change.** The DL-217 projection returned title-and-metadata only, so scope written onto a card by a PM was invisible to the impl seat reading its own board: a peer install measured **240 cards visible with 0 readable descriptions**, one of them carrying a 7,805-byte scope statement the seat it was written for could not read. `include_description` is a per-CALL argument rather than config, because the property is call-shaped — a seat wants scope when STARTING a card, not on every poll. Passed, every projected card carries `description` cut to `board_tools.description_max_bytes` (default 16384) plus `description_truncated`, because **a seat must never be able to mistake a truncated scope for the whole scope**; a non-boolean argument is refused **422**, never coerced. **The cut uses `mb_strcut`, not `substr`** — the cap is a byte budget and a raw byte cut splits a multi-byte character, whose invalid UTF-8 fails `json_encode` for the WHOLE response, so one emoji at the cut point would blank the caller's entire board window (proven by mutation: `substr` ⇒ HTTP 500, *Malformed UTF-8*). Costs no extra API call — the kanban search row already carried `description` and the projection discarded it. **Read isolation is untouched:** `filterSwimlane` runs fail-closed BEFORE the projection, so a field cannot widen who sees which card, and the allowlist widening is scoped to THIS field. 10 positive-control arms, each red on its own named test.
- **card#5564, DL-244** — **A prose coverage claim in a source comment is now GATED: `bin/check-doc-refs.php` gains a third rule, and CI rejects a comment shape it previously accepted.** `docs/check-golden-coverage.md` is generated from and bounded to `CheckCommand::handle()`, so a comment claiming a migrated check's predicate is one of its disclosed gaps is **false by construction** — and nothing gated it: phpstan does not analyse `bin/` or `tests/`, pint excludes `bin/`, and a false comment is valid PHP that passes every assertion. **Fifteen instances went false in a single merge — stage 7 completing — with no character changing in the files holding them**, which is why a periodic re-read cannot be the control. Under the four check-surface roots, a comment block naming the gap list must also state what naming it does not buy: the bound in the same sentence, or the consequence drawn, or a two-way denial, or an explicit negative membership. **Whitelist and fail-closed on purpose** — an unrecognised wrong sentence passes a blacklist silently, where an unrecognised right one fails loudly and is fixed by naming the bound. **The first implementation was wrong and a planted claim found it:** it licensed a mention whenever the bound sat within a measured distance window, but every migrated check opens *"migrated out of `CheckCommand::handle()`"*, so the boilerplate opener sat inside the window of anything beneath it — proximity was a proxy for a grammatical link, and the sentence IS that link; that regression is pinned as a test. Three in-tree blocks are corrected to green the tree (`ChannelTokenPathCheckTest` stated absence without its sibling's consequence clause, `GoldenCapture` pointed at the file with no bound, `CheckGoldenTest` omitted that the file names nothing belonging to a migrated check); all six sentences DL-243 named as correct pass unmodified. Zero runtime change — no `app/` logic, no golden fixture regenerated, no predicate added or removed, nothing altered in what the receiver accepts or rejects. Evidence: 6-arm rule mutation matrix each red on its own named vector, plus a real-surface plant → `rc=1` naming the file / restore → `rc=0`. **Neither pint nor phpstan covers `bin/`, so those three gates are the rule's only ones.**
- **card#5463, DL-242** — *(amended: the one open question this plan recorded — whether the opt-in probes report `unvalidated` when their flag is absent — is now RESOLVED; see the DL-242 amendment and `docs/CHECK-REGISTRY-PLAN.md` § Resolved design decision. Ruled: neither `unvalidated` nor `ok` — "not requested" is a third DISPOSITION in the inventory/renderer split, no new `Severity` case, exit contract untouched.)* **`docs/CHECK-REGISTRY-PLAN.md`: the reasoning owner for the `bridge:check` consolidation program, and the ruling that the receiver core is NOT the source of this repo's card pressure.** Documentation only — **no code change, no migration, no new `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change.** Measured over 448 commits since 2026-06-01, the receiver/dispatch/adapter core is small and static (`WebhookController` 77 L / 2 commits, `VerifyHmacSignature` 130 L / 2, `KanbanAdapter` 42 L / 1, `GitHubAdapter` 89 L / 2 — DL-001 is holding) and is placed **explicitly out of scope**; the churn is `CheckCommand.php`, **74 → 1767 lines in two months**, 50 commits (2× the next-highest file), `handle()` at **891 lines** nesting 11 deep, with **129 check-site emissions of which only 4 (~3%)** route through the `Finding`/`Severity` primitive DL-238 built for exactly that. The doc names the generator mechanically — a silent-failure card is closed by adding a `bridge:check` leg; the leg is a raw `warn()` because there is nothing else for it to be; raw `warn()` **cannot represent "did not run"**, so the leg's not-run state is indistinguishable from its pass state, minting the next card (`CheckCommand.php:961` already prints a five-line apology for this, *"a floor, not an inventory"*) — and specifies the target: a `Check`/`PerAgentCheck` registry over the existing `Finding` primitive, migrated as a **strangler under a byte-identical golden-output contract** so every stage is revertible. Three load-bearing constraints are recorded because each would break the refactor mid-flight: **registration must be unconditional** (a conditionally-unregistered check re-mints *green-because-never-looked* one level up, at the registry), a **per-agent check scope** is required (output is emitted *inside* the config loop from L240, so hoisting reorders it), and **inline derivation owns `CheckContext` during stages 1–7** (`$githubScopeConsumers` is built at L376 and consumed at L915, so producer and consumer necessarily migrate in different stages). **Bounds stated rather than implied:** the program closes **3 open cards** (card#5229, card#5291, card#5292) and explicitly does **not** close card#5170 (released) or card#5178 (shipped) — those are the *precedent* whose fixes created `Severity::Unvalidated` and `Finding` — nor card#5310 / card#5312, the same silent-degrade shape at a different address (`GitHubPrCardMoveClassifier`, `WritebackAlertNotifier`); the justification is recurrence prevention and the doc says outright that patching the three cards individually is a legitimate cheaper call. Stage 0 is the plan's own falsifier (if byte-identical capture is impossible the approach is re-planned, not half-executed), and the golden test's guarantee is bounded by fixture coverage, so the fixture set is derived from `handle()`'s branch predicates with any uncovered predicate disclosed as a named gap. A **§ Disproved claims** section records four figures/attributions that were falsified during the build so they are not restated. **Stages 8–10 are hard-gate and unstarted; no new `bridge:check` leg should be added until Stage 8 lands.**
- **card#5143, DL-237** — **A seat-side launch-assert, `bin/check-channel-snapshot.py`: the deployed channel server is certified by LAUNCHING it, as the OS user who will launch it.** `python3 bin/check-channel-snapshot.py <deployed dir>` spawns `node <dir>/agent-webhook-bridge-channel.mjs` with **stdin closed** and reads the **exit code** — **0** launch OK (the module graph resolved and the server came up), **1** launch FAILED conclusively (node's own stderr, e.g. `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, is printed and IS the diagnosis), **2** could not check — **never** a verdict in either direction, and DEFINED as *no verdict was reached* rather than as a list (`main()` carries a catch-all, so any unanticipated fault lands here whether or not anyone enumerated it; the exit is always 0, 1 or 2, asserted as a property). The named causes today: no `node` on PATH at all; `node` found but **not executable** (a shim that is not a valid executable/ENOEXEC, a `noexec` mount — `shutil.which` uses `os.access(X_OK)`, which does not model mount flags — or a fork refused under `RLIMIT_NPROC`/ENOMEM); the path is missing or untraversable; the throwaway-socket guard refused; the backstop timeout fired; and the child exited 0 without ever reporting a bind (an empty or truncated entry does exactly that), or was KILLED BY A SIGNAL — an OOM-kill or cgroup reap being the same physical condition as the refused fork, and a fact about the machine rather than the deployment (named readably as `SIGKILL (9)`, never `-9`). The bind case is deliberately not a failure, because a failure verdict there would rest on matching a log string. **Once a verdict IS computed it is never downgraded:** output is rendered to memory before any I/O is attempted, so a reader closing the pipe (`| head`, `grep -q 'LAUNCH OK'` — the documented consumption pattern) cannot turn a conclusive 0 or 1 into a 2, and an in-band flush keeps a broken pipe from escaping to the interpreter's shutdown flush (which exits 120, outside the contract entirely). Delivery is **total** — it cannot raise — so it holds even with **fd 1 closed at startup** (`>&-`, or Windows `pythonw.exe`), where `sys.stdout` is `None`. A **dangling symlink** is a conclusive **exit 1** and is reported as one rather than as a missing path — but only once the TARGET's own ancestor chain is proven traversable, since `exists()` is false for EACCES exactly as for ENOENT; otherwise it is the exit-2 not-visible case, which is what `ChannelSnapshotProbe` also reports for that shape. It is **stdlib-only, self-contained and reference-free** — no version, no file list, no bridge config, no bridge checkout — so it is copied to a seat that has none of those, which is what makes it usable in the cross-host topology of [`multi-host.md`](multi-host.md) where host A cannot even `stat` the host-B deployment. **Two build requirements, both about not breaking the thing being diagnosed:** (1) the socket is a **throwaway, set EXPLICITLY** in the child environment — `BRIDGE_CHANNEL_SOCKET` wins over the name-derived default and every seat exports it, so setting only a throwaway channel NAME leaves the server reaching for the **live** socket, which it declines to bind only while that socket file happens to exist; run before a session starts (exactly when the assert is useful) nothing holds it, and the server **unlinks the socket it bound on exit** — a diagnostic that breaks live-wake. The path is minted inside a private `mkdtemp()` so it cannot collide by construction, the run **refuses** rather than launching if that path already exists (a path this run did not create is never unlinked) and refuses if it equals an operator-declared `--live-socket`; `BRIDGE_CHANNEL_TRANSPORT` is pinned to `http` and is load-bearing twice over — the entry defaults it to `unix` when unset, and on a Windows seat the real value is exported by the launcher's process and/or `.mcp.json`'s `env` block, **neither of which a freshly-opened PowerShell inherits**, so an unpinned child there binds a filesystem socket, Win32 rejects it with EACCES, and the tool would print LAUNCH FAILED about a *healthy* deployment; and an ephemeral loopback bind ELIMINATES the live-unix-socket hazard class outright rather than defending against it, demoting the socket override and both refusals to belt-and-braces. `BRIDGE_CHANNEL_PORT=0` and `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` get the same treatment so the `http` transport's port and the `.FAILED` marker are equally contained. Standing rule: enumerate the entry's addressing inputs at source and assign a throwaway for each before shipping a new transport. **⚠ The pin has a cost, disclosed rather than buried:** it MOVES the invalid-transport refusal into the blind spot rather than out of it — `.mcp.json`'s `env` block carries `BRIDGE_CHANNEL_TRANSPORT` on every deployment (the shipped `.mcp.json.example` sets it, and `bin/provision-board-tools.py` writes it), so the transport the session will actually use is the one value this assert guarantees not to exercise. Accepted because a false LAUNCH FAILED on every Windows seat is the worse failure and the module graph resolves identically under either transport; the pass message says outright that the bind proven is *neither your configured endpoint nor your configured transport*. (2) The verdict is the **exit code, never "survived the timeout"**: the lifecycle is stdin-driven (`process.stdin.on('end', () => shutdown(0))`), so with stdin closed a healthy server binds and exits 0 *immediately* while a survival-based assert would false-FAIL it; the timeout is a backstop only and is **inconclusive**, never a pass. **Bounded claim, stated in the output:** a pass proves the module graph resolves and the bind succeeds — not steady state, and nothing about **staleness** (that remains `bridge:check`'s version leg, and a seat the bridge cannot traverse still gets no staleness verdict from anywhere; unchanged by this card).

### Changed
- **card#5291 / card#5292, DL-251** — **`bridge:check`'s `unvalidated` severity gets a RULE, and 21 findings move onto it: *the leg did not answer its own question*.** **⚠ OPERATOR-VISIBLE OUTPUT CHANGE (the last gated row of the DL-242 check-registry plan, stage 10).** **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change — and NO exit-code change by construction** (`emitFinding()` returns false only for `fail`, asserted as a property, so a `warn` → `unvalidated` re-assignment cannot move an exit code). DL-238 left the `warn` ↔ `unvalidated` boundary deliberately unsettled because the discriminator drafted for it failed on a live site; the rule now lives in `App\Bridge\Support\Severity`'s docblock and applies **per LEG, not per check** — answered ⇒ `ok`/`warn`/`fail`; unanswered because of an install fact ⇒ `unvalidated`; unanswered because the operator never asked ⇒ no finding (the `not-requested` disposition). A leg is unanswered when the measurement did not complete, when it completed but may have measured the **wrong subject** (an `authorized_keys` at an assumed path sshd may not consult), or when a comparison's comparand does not resolve to exactly one comparable value. **What you will SEE:** 21 lines that were yellow now render plain and join the run's closing tally — every *CANNOT VERIFY* / *could not read board* / *skipped board-visibility probe* / *UNVERIFIED pinned line* / *snapshot not visible to this user* / *hit the page ceiling* line among them. **Salience loss, stated rather than discovered:** five of those name an actionable coordination-config defect and lose their yellow, and — a separate, disjoint set — **seven spell an explicit operator action** (*re-run as root* on the two ssh pinned-line legs, *point `$COORD_CONFIG` at coordination.config.json* on two cross-config arms, and three channel-snapshot arms whose remedy is to grant read access, repair a manifest, or re-run as the agent's own user). Accepted because what they report is that the MEASUREMENT could not be made, not that anything is proven wrong; the line still prints and now enters the tally. **THREE NEW LINES APPEAR**, which is an addition rather than a re-assignment. Two are comparison legs: the writeback mapped-stage-id compare and the board-tools `create_stage_id` compare both fell **silent** when the board returned no stages — and both are also silent when they succeed, so silence meant *every id exists* AND *the ids were never compared to anything*, at the two legs whose whole job is catching a silent 422. The third is the per-repo GitHub read-token probe: an **unreachable api.github.com** shared its `switch` arm with a token that probed clean, so *"this token is valid"* and *"we never found out"* were the same output — it now reports `unvalidated`, names the repo, and explicitly does **not** accuse the token (a blip is still not a token-validity signal). A sweep keyed on the sites that REPORT `warn` is blind to that class by construction — and so is every derivation in the repo, which all key on something a leg emitted, so **three is a floor**: that set was found by reading, not derived. **Also:** `bridge:check`'s closing tally is reworded (it now discloses that the count covers legs which REPORTED being unable to measure — a leg that never noticed is **not counted there, and is not reliably silent either**: it may say nothing, or say what it would have concluded), the DL-225 ssh flipped-default advisory's predicate widened in the same change so it does not go silent on the install shape it exists for (DL-238(g) is recorded **narrowed, not closed**), and `Finding`'s constructor is now **private** with a new severity-preserving `Finding::scoped()` for the three checks that re-prefix another probe's output. **`bridge:check --format=json` keeps `schema: 1`** — no tagged release has ever carried the renderer, and `docs/check-json-contract.md` §4 pre-disclosed exactly this correction; both grounds are now written into that doc's bump table as checkable conditions. `severity: "unvalidated"` is now sound evidence that a leg could not measure; its **absence** is still not evidence that everything was. **card#5292** rides along: `BoardToolAgentResolver::problems()` loses a `type` key written at three sites and read at zero.
- **card#5464, card#5468, card#5483, card#5500 (×2 — PRs #429 + #430), card#5515, card#5521, card#5531, card#5543, card#5550, card#5554, card#5557, card#5585 / DL-248** — **The DL-242 check registry, stages 0–8: `bridge:check`'s inline `handle()` becomes a registry of `Check` / `PerAgentCheck` objects, migrated under a byte-identical-output contract.** **This is ONE program recorded as ONE entry, not thirteen near-identical ones** — the decision record is **DL-242** (in *Added*, above), stage 9 is the `--format=json` document (card#5229, DL-249) and stage 10 is the `unvalidated` rule (card#5291 / card#5292, DL-251), each with its own entry. **Operator-visible only at stage 8** (below); stages 0–7 hold output byte-identical by contract — the golden corpus is the proof, not a regeneration. **The stages, and what each one forced:** **0** (card#5464, #426) the golden-output harness plus `Check`/`CheckContext`/`CheckRunner` scaffolding, with the falsifier result that byte-identical capture works but the harness must pin the ambient host; **1** (card#5468, #427) the two `Finding`-shaped probes, where `CheckSlot` proved necessary and the ssh fixtures closed the gap; **2** (card#5483, #428) the retention-posture cluster, whose proof was a plain green run and whose `catch` arms needed their own unit test; **3a/3b** (card#5500, #429 + #430) the writeback config plane then the probe plane — split into two PRs, and the stage where the disclosed-gap count's largest drop yet **closed no gap at all**, because the MEASURED REGION shrank; **4** (card#5515, #431) the database / install-suffix cluster, the first pure-assertion cluster; **5a/5b/5c** (card#5521 / card#5531 / card#5543, #433–#435) the per-agent classifier plane (which needed its own ABORT slot), the secret/token + channel-transport legs (**the first stage to COLLAPSE a slot rather than add one**), and the post-loop roster/identity legs (the first slot whose ordinal is forced by what its checks READ); **6** (card#5550, #436) the pre-loop install plane, which needed **three** slots because the two that migrated first already sat inside the region; **7a/7b** (card#5554 / card#5557, #437 + #438) the event-follows-consumer plane — where the predicate total went UP for the first time without that being a regression, and phpstan's purity inference was exposed as reading only the DIRECTLY called method — then the board-tools plane, needing five slots and **the first `CheckContext` field derived from another check's FINDINGS rather than from the install**. Each stage's full result, including the disproved claims, is owned by [`docs/CHECK-REGISTRY-PLAN.md`](CHECK-REGISTRY-PLAN.md); this entry deliberately does not restate that table. **Stage 8 (card#5585, DL-248) is the gate stage — and its own row's invariant was FALSE, corrected in the open rather than quietly built around.** The row read *"every registered check emits ≥1 finding"*. Instrumenting `CheckRunner` and re-running the suite plus all 33 golden fixtures **before writing any stage-8 code** measured, on the healthy `minimal` baseline that exits 0 in 11 lines: **37 registered, 13 NEVER INVOKED** (the writeback plane's 9 and the board-tools plane's 4, whose slots sit behind conditional envelopes), 15 invoked-but-silent, 9 speaking — and across the corpus 26 of 37 yield nothing on at least one run, with no fixture invoking all 37 (max 33, min 16, median 24). **Enforcing the row literally would have dissolved the envelopes stages 3a and 7b preserved AS BEHAVIOR and taken a healthy run from 11 output lines to ≥37**, contradicting the row's own operator-visible column; a stage row is a hypothesis until the stage measures it. What is enforced instead is that **every registered check is ACCOUNTED FOR** on every run — it either ran (findings recorded, possibly none) or was deliberately skipped with a recorded reason. **`NotRun` is DERIVED from the registration list, never declared by the caller:** the rejected alternative would have made the mechanism's correctness depend on remembering to call it — *the same shape as the bug it closes* — so a forgotten reason degrades a message and cannot open a hole. **`not requested` is DECLARED via `OptInCheck::wasRequested()`, never inferred from an empty yield** (a check that looked and found nothing and a check nobody asked to look are different facts). No new `Severity` case, so the exhaustive-match property and the exit contract are untouched by construction. **⚠ Operator-visible:** every run gains one inventory line and the `unvalidated` tally is reworded (33 golden fixtures regenerated, one inventory line each). **A later adversarial pass weakened every stage-8 claim to what its evidence supports** — one defect class in ten places, which on a diagnostic IS the product defect, since the whole value of `bridge:check` is that an operator can trust what it says: the not-run population is labelled `N did not run` rather than *"N not applicable here"* (the committed `agent-yaml-malformed` fixture claimed the latter on an exit-1 install where the agent plane is fully applicable and the YAML is merely broken); a `! is_readable($configDir)` arm was added to the per-agent skip reason, because a dir that exists but the bridge user cannot read passes `is_dir()` and globs empty, so the next arm asserted *"this install has no agent config files"* about an install that may hold a dozen (reproduced before fixing); and the two writeback not-run reasons are pitched at their ENVELOPE's strength instead of at one thrower inside it, which had printed the real cause in the warn and a different, confident one in the inventory. **Bounds stated rather than implied:** stage 8 does not make the severity vocabulary precise (that is card#5291 / DL-251), it cannot see a check nobody wrote, `not applicable` is the envelope's claim about itself, and a `Silent` disposition stayed indistinguishable from falling off the end of a generator until card#5596 / DL-266. Evidence: five SINGLE-ARM mutation proofs (mutate → run → restore → `cmp`, so each is exclusively attributable), phpstan's green positive-controlled on the new files specifically rather than inferred from `paths`, inventory conservation asserted across all 33 committed golden files and itself controlled by corrupting one, and a sibling audit that corrected the stale *"stage 8 turns this into a returned disposition"* claim across **17 inline comments in 15 check classes plus 6 docblocks** — where a uniform grep found 12 of the 17 and **would have read as complete**.
- **card#5288, DL-241** — **The writeback's `getCard` permanent-refusal reason splits 404 (no such card) from 403 (the card exists and is not visible to this token); `getcard_4xx` stays the catch-all.** The `getcard_4xx` alert `reason` was one opaque value over two unrelated operator hypotheses, and it sits on the branch that returns **before** the DL-009 belongs-to-mapped-board guard — that guard reads `board_id` **out of** the card, so it can only fire for a card the token could READ, and a card on a board the token cannot see never reaches it. The reason string is therefore the operator's only signal for exactly the case the security guard exists to refuse, and it named neither hypothesis. Now: **`getcard_404_no_such_card`** (deleted, or an id that never existed), **`getcard_403_not_visible_to_this_token`** (a foreign install's card id correlated onto this bridge, **or** this token's scope missing the card's board — both named in the operator-facing log line), and **`getcard_4xx`** unchanged for every other 4xx. Additive on `WritebackAlertNotifier`'s single `writeback_move_failed` event kind — no new event type, no body-shape change. **No status-code or exit-behaviour change:** all three still swallow the event (permanent → log + no-op, never a 5xx retry). Because dedup keys on `sha1(repo, outcome, reason)`, the split also *fixes* a silent loss the collapse caused: a 404 and a 403 on the same `(repo, outcome)` deduped against each other, so whichever arrived second alerted **zero** times — asserted directly (two deliveries, two distinct reasons, two pushes) and mutation-proven red against the collapsed reason.
- **card#5308, DL-240** — **The DL token gets a public grammar owner, `App\Bridge\Support\DlTokenGrammar` — the second pinned-not-tied accept-set the card#5267 audit found — and the vector loop that consolidation enabled immediately surfaced a FOURTH CI-lint divergence nobody had measured.** The pattern was a `private const` on `GitHubPrCardMoveClassifier` with four call sites, so the characterization test that had to compare it against the CI lint reached it by **reflection**, and its one operator-facing restatement was NARROWER than the code: `pr-title-lint.yml` said `DL-NNNN` and bounded the token at `[0-9]{1,4}` against an unbounded `\d+`. `parse()` (the first token, normalized) and `sole()` (the *exactly one* predicate the card#4852 stamp guard keys on — previously re-spelled as `preg_match_all(...) === 1` at two sites) replace all four; the reflection read is gone. **A sibling class, not a rename of `CardTokenGrammar` into a combined owner:** the two grammars are coupled by ratification (the card token's boundary was widened to match the DL token's, DL-201) but share no code, and a merged class would have prefixed every member — so the coupling is expressed as a TEST instead, driving one fixture through both grammars, which is a stronger tie than co-location. **No `describe()`, stated rather than silently omitted:** the card token has one because a runtime near-miss warning renders it; the DL token has no PHP surface that names its spellings, so a renderer with only a test caller would be a decoration. **The new divergence, measured both ways:** driving the whole vector set through the real gate (the card half has had this since DL-239; the DL half had three hand-picked shapes) showed that `[0-9]` inside bash's `[[ =~ ]]` is a **collation** range, not an ASCII one — under `en_US.UTF-8` a Unicode-digit `DL-٣` **passes the gate** while the classifier correlates it to nothing, the same FALSE-GREEN shape as `card4` and a direct breach of the fleet-wide ASCII ratification (DL-231). Under `C` / `C.UTF-8` / `POSIX` the gate reds it and agrees; GNU `grep -E` never does it in any locale, which is what identifies bash's engine as the cause and leaves the warn step unaffected. **PINNED, not fixed** — repairing it changes what a CI gate accepts (**card#5300**, hard gate) — and pinned as a *measurement*: both locale answers are asserted, with the grep control beside them. **The prose sweep is tied, not just corrected:** `DL-NNNN` is gone from the workflow header and both operator error lines (replaced by the delimited `DL-<number>`, which asserts no width), and a test now collects every `DL-<alnum>` spelling in the step and requires the grammar to accept it — a letter-run standing in for the digits necessarily encodes a count — with a positive control proving the collector catches the historical spelling, since a collector returning nothing would otherwise report a defect-free step as health. The header's "the lint must accept everything the classifier correlates" invariant now names its one measured exception instead of overstating. No behaviour change: the pattern is byte-identical, `stampRefs`/`dlToken`/the branch-create path return exactly what they did, and **the lint's regexes are untouched**.
- **card#5267, DL-239** — **The operator-facing card-token accept-set stops being a RESTATEMENT: `App\Bridge\Support\CardTokenGrammar` owns the pattern, and the sentence humans read is RENDERED by running it.** The near-miss WARN string told operators the accepted set was `card-<id>` or `card#<id>` for two releases after DL-233 made glued `card123` correlate, and its docblock listed `card123` as a *near-miss* — live, operator-facing, and read by the next agent building an emulator (one asserted from a downstream copy that `card5086` does not parse). `describe()` buckets a shared `VECTORS` example set by RUNNING `PATTERN`, so the next grammar move rewrites the operator text by construction; `card4` sits beside `card-3` in that set so the ≥2-digit glued floor is SHOWN rather than asserted. `CARD_TOKEN_PATTERN` is gone from `GitHubPrCardMoveClassifier` — both parse sites call the owner (a `private const` accept-set, the design brief's own first shape, was rejected during the build: the tie that makes this stick is a test running the authority, and reflection is a weaker tie than a public one). **The discriminator is NOT "has a test":** `PrTitleLintTest` executed the real lint bash across all three grammar edits and never read the constant, so it stayed green through every one — it is tied-to-the-authority vs pinned-to-a-past-value. That file now extracts the workflow's real `good=`/`looks=` regexes out of the YAML (failing LOUDLY if the assignment is gone) and compares COMPUTED ANSWER SETS against the grammar's in three renderings (bare, mid-prose, inside a branch ref — `(^|[^0-9a-z_])` and `\b` must agree in all three); the hand-written near-miss tables are now derived from the vector set, shrunk rather than deleted. **The require step is PINNED, not tied, and is labelled as such:** it disagrees with the grammar on three measured shapes — `card4` passes the gate but never correlates (FALSE GREEN: the gate certifies a silent no-op), `card#0123` and `DL-12345` red it though the classifier parses both (FALSE RED) — and repairing either direction changes what a CI gate accepts or rejects, so it is characterized in a named test and left alone (**card#5300**, hard gate). **Eight stale prose sites corrected**, found only because the sweep was run TWICE and then self-reviewed: a *what-breaks-if-the-accept-set-changes* pass finds every executable site and under-counts inert prose, so a second pass grepping the accept-set's WORDING surfaced the branch-create docblock, `docs/writeback.md`'s `started` paragraph and `pr-title-lint.yml`'s header, with two more (the class docblock, `cardToken()`'s own) found by re-reading rather than by either grep — the exact mirror of the prose-grep-misses-executable-sites failure on the other leg of roundtable #177. A self-review pass then found **four more of a different shape** in `docs/writeback.md` — the card-token channel omitted ENTIRELY, the doc claiming a branch push correlates by `DL-NNN` and that a ref *with no `DL-NNN`* is ignored, when the code's guard is `! $hasDl && $cardToken === null`; false since DL-177 introduced that channel, and invisible to both sweep passes because it contains none of the accept-set's wording. All now point at the owner; `docs/writeback.md`'s grammar blockquote is retitled to say it is the one place the spellings are written for operators. **The audit this card owed is closed:** nine other bridge accept-sets opened individually, one genuine second instance found (`DL_TOKEN_PATTERN` — private, unowned, and restated NARROWER than the code as `DL-NNNN`/`[0-9]{1,4}` against `\d+`), filed as **card#5308**; three already render their own const into the operator message, three are prose-only and verified correct member-by-member, three are not restated anywhere. **Bound stated rather than implied:** no mechanism here can tie a markdown doc or a YAML string to a PHP const, so the warning TEXT is tied only as far as it can be — a test asserts every ASCII shape the grammar rejects is NAMED in it. No migration, no new `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no change to what the classifier correlates or what CI accepts — the pattern is byte-identical and the lint's regexes are untouched.
- **card#5143, DL-237** — **The version-gated snapshot COMPLETENESS leg (DL-230, shipped in v0.71.0) is retired; `bridge:check` now DISCLOSES that it did not measure loadability instead of approximating it.** Measured on a real artifact, the file-set compare is **less precise in both directions** than launching: a retired copy missing **6 of 10** reference files (`.gitignore`, `.mcp.json.example`, `README.md`, three `tests/*.mjs` — exactly the enumerated-but-not-load-bearing set) FAILs completeness while `node` binds and exits **0**, and the same copy with `channel-lib.mjs` removed exits **1** on `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. That unearned-RED population is the one DL-230 (f) recorded as *hypothesized, not observed*, with an explicit instruction to re-weigh "if either population ever does show up" — it showed up. A launch also catches the DL-230 incident itself, since the entry imports `channel-lib.mjs` (verified at source, and pinned by a test). **`bridge:check` must NOT run the launch, and that is the ruling rather than an omission:** the bridge commonly runs as a **different OS user** than the agent, so launching from there would prove the entry loads for the *bridge's* PATH, node and read access — a proxy for the question again, which is the exact thing the launch was adopted to stop doing. `bridge:check` therefore emits **one `unvalidated` finding per agent, on every run that reached the legs** (*was NOT launch-tested … a green run here is not evidence the entry will LOAD at the next session start*) naming `bin/check-channel-snapshot.py` and the user to run it as — stale, current, newer and the repo-direct symlink alike, and beside a presence FAIL. **Uniform, not on the version-matched branch alone:** an earlier shape of this change put it there (where the retired leg lived) and left the repo-direct topology — the one the channel-server README recommends — fully green with nothing said, which is the *green check, dark seat* shape this card family exists to end. The four paths that end before the legs (undeclared `server_path`, the dangling and names-a-file FAILs, the not-visible WARN) do not carry it: each already says nothing was measured and names its one action. `unvalidated` renders plain and joins the run's closing tally; **the exit code is untouched by construction** (`emitFinding()` returns false only for `fail` — DL-236 (c)), asserted end to end. **⚠ Behavior change: a version-matched deployment missing a reference file no longer FAILs `bridge:check` (exit 1 → exit 0).** No migration, no new `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change; a `channel.server_path` left undeclared is byte-identical. Gone with the leg: `completenessLeg()`, `referenceFileSet()`, the per-subdirectory visibility walk (`deployedState()`), the missing-list cap, the *NEWER … completeness SKIPPED* line (it named machinery that no longer exists), and the cross-language enumeration lockstep `SnapshotFileSetLockstep` — the same disposition DL-229 gave it the first time this leg was cut. The `VersionComparatorLockstep` contract is unaffected and stays. Docs: `docs/config-schema.md`, `CLAUDE_DEPLOYMENT.md`, `docs/multi-host.md` and `examples/channel-servers/README.md` corrected (all four described a check with a completeness leg — the README grep-invisibly, since it never used the word), and `CLAUDE.md`'s quick-start block gains the seat-side invocation; channel-server snapshot `0.8.2` → `0.8.3` (README-only change to a shipped file, per the DL-038 bump guard); DL-230 amended in place with `[SUPERSEDED by DL-237 …]` notes on the paragraphs the measurement falsified, (f) above all.

- **card#5178, DL-238** — **One shared finding primitive: `App\Bridge\Support\Severity` (backed enum) + `Finding` (readonly VO) replace the `{severity, message}` string vocabulary that was implemented twice.** The four severities were two private helper triples — one per probe — plus a raw array literal in the consumer, all typed as `array{severity: string, message: string}` re-declared at every producer and consumer docblock.
