Nothing expressed which values were legal, so a typo'd or newly-invented severity fell through `emitFinding()`'s `else` and rendered ANSI-**green**: an unknown severity reported as CERTIFIED CLEAN. DL-236 had just made the two copies disagree by adding `unvalidated` to one probe only. All **8** construction sites in `app/` migrate (re-derived by shape-grep `'severity' =>`, never by class name — the card body's "both probes" inventory was already stale by one site, DL-236's own fail-soft catch in `CheckCommand`), producer return types move to `list<Finding>` / `?Finding`, and **both** consumers — `emitFinding()` and `severityMeansSetupIncomplete()` — become exhaustive `match`es, the **return** arm included (`return $severity !== Severity::Fail` would have silently classed a fifth case as non-failing: the same fall-through shape, relocated onto the exit contract). **Pure refactor, and UNGATED for a reason worth stating:** the card asked whether the else-branch should fail loud on an unknown severity and correctly flagged that as hard-gate — with the enum inside `Finding` that state is unrepresentable, so there is no else-branch left to make loud and no reachable input produces different output or a different exit. The rendering map is arm-for-arm identical. **PHPStan `paths` gains exactly one `app/Console` file** (`CheckCommand.php`, measured clean at level 7, no baseline): the card's premise that the enum makes exhaustiveness "real rather than advisory" was true for the producers and **false for the consumers**, which sat outside the analysed paths where an unhandled case would surface only as a runtime `UnhandledMatchError`. The rest of `app/Console` stays unanalysed. **The `warn` ↔ `unvalidated` SEMANTICS are deliberately left open** — the candidate discriminator fails on a live site (`CheckCommand`'s fail-soft catch is a genuine anomaly that DL-236 assigned `unvalidated`), so `Severity`'s docblock documents the **rendering contract only** and names the question instead of freezing an unargued split into the type every future producer reads; tracked as **card#5291**, hard-gate (it changes operator-facing output). The severity-contract tests' `'some-future-severity'` rows became unrepresentable and were **replaced, not deleted** — each now iterates `Severity::cases()`, which is strictly stronger than a stand-in for one hypothetical value: the fifth-case mutation reds **four** tests and **three** phpstan `match.unhandled` errors, where the old row would have reddened at most one. No migration, no new `.env`, no exit-code change, no change to what `bridge:check` accepts, rejects, or prints.
- **card#5538** — **`ChannelTransportCheck`'s socket-liveness gate drops an inert `! is_link()` clause: two of its four conjuncts excluded the same set.** Output is identical either way — `filetype()` is lstat-based, so it returns `'link'` for a symlink and never the target's type; any path where `is_link()` is true therefore already fails the type test, and `! is_link()` could not change the conjunction's value for **any** input. **The cost was not a wrong answer — it was that the two clauses masked each other's mutation, so neither could be mutation-tested alone**, which is the exact property the DL-242 coverage instrument exists to expose. It was recorded during stage 5b rather than fixed there, because stages 0–7 hold a byte-identical migration contract. Verified: golden corpus byte-identical (assert mode, fixtures unchanged), and the two exclusion tests were watched go **RED** with the surviving `filetype()` test deleted — so the remaining clause is the one carrying the behavior, not a second unmeasured passenger. **`! is_link()` stays in `SocketEndpoint::assertValid`, where it IS load-bearing:** a dangling symlink has `file_exists()` false but `is_link()` true, and the two checks are separate statements throwing different messages.
- **Dependencies** — routine dependabot bumps landed on the integration branch this cycle: `laravel/framework` 13.20.0 → 13.23.0 (#415), `phpunit/phpunit` 13.2.4 → 13.2.6 (#414), `actions/checkout` 7.0.0 → 7.0.1 (#416), and the `PupFuzz/agent-board-toolkit/promote` action pin (#417). The channel-server dependency work is separate and is recorded under *Security*, below.

### Fixed
- **card#5310, DL-273** — **A `DL-NNN` near-miss was completely silent: there was no DL near-miss probe at all, and the probe that finds one is now a shared primitive instead of a second grammar inside a second grammar.** A branch ref or PR title that APPEARED to name a DL but did not parse published, moved nothing and told nobody — `feat/DL_239-slug` (underscore, not the separator), `feat/DL239-slug` (glued; unlike the card token the DL token has **no** glued arm and its separator is mandatory), and every **plural** spelling (`DLs-239`, `DLs239`, …), silent by the identical mechanism DL-250 measured on the card stem: `\bDL` matches inside `DLs`, the optional separator group cannot consume the `s`, and `\d` then fails against it with no second word boundary to retry from. `warnCardTokenNearMiss()` only ever fired on card-ish text. **Measured on this repo's own history rather than argued from examples: of the 400 most recent PRs, 12 head branches use the glued `dlNNN` spelling** (`feature/dl207-…`, `docs/dl204-…`, `feat/dl189-…`, down to `dl028`) and carry no card token, so every one of those branch-create pushes correlated to nothing and said nothing — and in **all 12 the PR title carried a properly-spelled `DL-NNN`**, so the PR-path move worked and the board looked right while the DL-160 `started` move was silently lost: the card never advanced when work BEGAN, only when the PR opened. (Stated bound: a static evaluation of branch NAMES against today's grammar, not a replay of delivered events — about half carry DL numbers predating the branch-create leg, so 12 is an exact count of the spelling and an upper bound on events actually missed.) **DL-234 shipped exactly this warning for the card token on the argument that an unparsed token is "as high-value a miss as an unresolvable DL" (roundtable #48) — an argument made about the DL token BY NAME, applied only to the other one.** **Severity matches DL-234(c) exactly: a WARNING, never a failure. No accept/reject change** — which text correlates to which card is untouched, and nothing that moved a card before moves differently now; only which NON-correlating text gets an operator log line. **The mechanism moved to `NearMissProbe` rather than being copied** (canon #5, second real caller): DL-250 made the separators DATA so that the pattern and the corpus covering it are two consumers of one definition, and none of that reasoning was ever about cards — duplicating ~15 lines of rule-shaped logic into `DlTokenGrammar` would have re-created, one level up, the restatement shape DL-250 removed one level down. **This supersedes DL-250(1) as a code-state claim** (`NEAR_MISS_SEPARATORS` no longer lives on `CardTokenGrammar`; the public API of that class is unchanged) and reverses none of its reasoning. **One separator list now serves every stem, which is a claim about AUTHORS, not about tokens** — a card-side edit deliberately moves the DL probe too, and can only ever ADD warnings, since the probe is consulted solely where `parse()` returned null. `DlTokenGrammar` gains the **`describe()`** its own docblock said to add *"the day a PHP surface needs it"*, plus one plural sentence row; a subject that misses on both stems warns **once per grammar**, each line naming its own accept-set (pinned, so a later "de-duplicate the warnings" edit reds rather than silently picking one). **⚠ TWO THINGS ARE DELIBERATELY STILL SILENT and are filed as card#5961 — do not read their silence as coverage.** (1) A **Unicode-digit** DL (`DL-<U+0663>239`, one of the three shapes the card named) warns on **neither** surface: the probe's digit class is ASCII by ratification (DL-231), and unlike the card token there is **no DL arm on the CI PR-title lint** to cover the class by name — so scoping this to the runtime surface shipped the half DL-234's own alternative (d) called insufficient (*"the author-facing gate is the PR check"*), on scope and named rather than left to be discovered as an absence. (2) A subject whose **card token parses** beside a malformed DL (`feat/card-3410-slug-DL_272`) moves its card and silently fails to stamp `dl_number` — DL-234(e)'s whole-subject limit, kept because the warning's own *"no move"* clause would be a false statement about exactly the subjects a wider guard would newly cover; a lost STAMP is a different signal from a lost MOVE and needs its own site. **Evidence — 13 mutations, each applied-and-asserted, run, read, restored from a snapshot (baseline 127/127, 902 assertions):** losing `s?` reds 6 legs across 4 files including the silent-set pin, which names `DLs-239` as a NEW SILENT SHAPE rather than absorbing it; unquoting the separators (the `.` becomes the any-character metacharacter) reds the prose legs on BOTH stems; `/u` reds the ASCII-bound leg on both stems AND reds the silent-set pin by LOSING the Unicode row, pinning the bound in both directions; **dropping one separator from the now-SHARED data still reds `PrTitleLintTest`'s bidirectional union tie, and the sharpest of DL-250's rows — the ACCEPTED `-` dropped, once caught by nothing at all — reds 5 legs**, so the hoist did not break DL-250(4)'s removal direction; stopping the CARD probe reds 3 legs including the pre-existing ones, which is what shows the hoist did not blind the side that already worked; **and the mutation this hoist's own risk demanded — `DlTokenGrammar` binding the WRONG stem, the copy-paste bug a hoist actually produces — reds 5 legs across 2 files.** phpstan L7 0, pint clean. **[Superseded — see the Unreleased entry for card#5961: BOTH deliberate silences described above are closed one release later. (1) `pr-title-lint.yml` grows a `looks_dl=`/`good_dl=` arm beside its card one, so "there is **no DL arm on the CI PR-title lint**" is no longer true and a Unicode-digit DL is named by class to the PR author — the RUNTIME half of the bound stands, the probe is still ASCII and DL-231 is untouched. (2) The lost `dl_number` stamp is now warned at the STAMP site; DL-234(e)'s whole-subject limit itself is unchanged and that subject still draws no near-miss line from the runtime probe (the CI lint's DL arm is per-stem and does warn on its malformed `DL_272`). Read this entry as the state v0.72.0 shipped.]**
- **card#5287, DL-270** — **The head branch now outranks the PR title for `card#` authority: a foreign title token could silently move somebody else's card.** **⚠ This changes which card the writeback correlates to when the title and the head ref disagree — previously the title won.** The token was matched over `$title.' '.$head` with a single non-global `preg_match`, so the **LEFTMOST** match won, and the title sits first in that concatenation. The head branch ref is minted by this install's own tooling and is the authoritative referent for what a PR is work ON; the title is prose, where a `card#NNNN` is as often a descriptive citation of another card as a claim about this PR. **The reachable input is a same-board citation:** on the mapped board the `getCard` 4xx arm and the belongs-to-mapped-board guard both pass, and somebody else's card moves. Same shape as the foreign-DL hijack DL-218 closed, one layer up — a boundary its predicate does not reach across. The DL-218 conflict SHAPE is applied to `(title token, head token)`, defined once in `cardTokenResolution` and shared by the move path and the draft overlay: **branch wins; a disagreeing title token is refused and warned.** The residual — a title token the ref does not back — is settled at the handler against the card's own `pr_number` (unset or already this PR, else refused), using the read `getCard` already makes, and gated BEFORE the already-in-stage self-heal because that branch still stamps. **Corroboration is deliberately wider than selection:** a bare id in the ref backs a title token but can never select a card. Measured against merged PRs — the dominant branch convention is `<type>/<id>-slug`, which carries no card token, so requiring one would have refused every second PR against a card. On a conflict the sole-DL stamp derives from the head ref alone. Also corrects the false delegation claim (*"the durable handler rejects a card not on the mapped board"*) at 6 sites, including the Security-notes bound.
- **DL-269** — **The node channel-server suite could write a false deafness marker beside a perfectly healthy socket, because it inherited the seat's LIVE addressing environment.** The suite spawns the real channel server, whose `markerPath()` falls back to the ambient environment. A seat exports `BRIDGE_CHANNEL_SOCKET` pointing at its **live** socket, so a child spawned with a plain `{...process.env}` binds it, gets `EADDRINUSE`, and writes a `.FAILED` deafness marker next to a socket that is working. **Both existing suites already scrubbed their child env — one by allowlist, one by denylist — and both measured clean; neither could tell you when it STOPPED being clean.** `live-state-guard.mjs` neutralises all four addressing inputs at import and **fails the run if the seat's real runtime dir moved**. DL-269 records the audit across all three languages and why PHP is accepted without a guard. Test-only — no runtime code, no behaviour change.
- **card#5232, DL-268** — **The channel-server snapshot version drifted across roughly a dozen green CI runs, because the guard that enforces it reads one of the two files that carry it.** **⚠ CHANGES WHAT CI ACCEPTS: a PR whose `examples/channel-servers/package-lock.json` version disagrees with `package.json` now fails.** No app code, no migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change. DL-038 made `package.json` `version` the staleness signal a copied snapshot compares against canonical and guarded it with `version-bump-guard` — which reads the manifest only. Measured on `dev`: the lockfile's root `version` sat at **`0.7.1`** while the manifest went `0.7.2 → 0.8.3`, every run green, because a guard cannot fail on a field it never reads. **The drift is not live and was never fixed** — it closed at `6c4f504` when an `npm` run resynced the lock as a side effect, so its agreement had been luck. **Not an outage at any point:** `npm ci` validates the dependency tree, not the root `version`. A new step asserts the lockfile's `.version` **and** `.packages[""].version` both equal the manifest's — both, because the defect class is precisely a guard reading one of N fields that must agree, and covering only the root re-mints it one level down. It is **not diff-gated** the way the bump step is (agreement is a property of the tree, so no shape of diff earns an exemption) — though not unconditional either: the workflow's `paths:` filter means a PR touching neither the directory nor the workflow never runs the job at all, which is still complete coverage of the entry path, since drift can only be introduced by a PR that edits those files. An **absent** manifest `version` FAILS rather than comparing jq's `null` to `null`. **The trigger set is deliberately NOT weakened:** a lockfile change keeps demanding a bump, because a transitive dependency bump changes what `npm ci` installs and so genuinely moves the deployed artifact. A lockfile-only dependency PR is therefore red by construction (dependabot cannot bump the manifest) and takes a **one-command maintainer commit**, documented in the guard's own error output — the surface the maintainer actually reaches — rather than in a runbook that has to be found first. The new assertion is driven by a test that extracts the step's real `run:` block from the YAML and executes it under `bash`, so it cannot drift from what CI runs; its positive control is **real history rather than invented JSON** (the pair as it stood at `4abe8e3`, the last commit at which the defect was live), and it is mutation-proven 4/4 against a 5-test baseline with both control axes asserted. **Deleted in passing:** the bump step's comment claimed the filter excludes *"the CI workflow itself"*, which it never did — the workflow was never inside the pathspec, so the clause described a protection that was not performed and read as an invitation to add the lockfile to that same exclusion list. **Sibling audit → card#5910 (one confirmed, one refuted):** `VERSION` vs `docs/CHANGELOG.md` has the same shape — `auto-tag-version.yml` is the only automation reading both and merely WARNs on a missing section while publishing a placeholder release note; left unfixed deliberately, as adding a PR-rejecting check is a hard gate. `composer.json`+`composer.lock` **looked** identical in shape in both this repo and kanban-board (no `composer validate` in either CI) and is **refuted** — `composer install` in the shared `setup-app` composite refuses an out-of-sync pair itself (measured two-sided: rc 4 mismatched, rc 0 restored).
- **card#5548, DL-267** — **A destroyed run is not a measurement: `bin/check-golden-mutate.php` laundered a FAILED WRITE into the strongest verdict the program can produce.** When `file_put_contents` could not apply the mutant, the golden run aborted for that unrelated reason and every predicate scored **observed-via-abort at exit 0** — the best-looking outcome available, from a run that measured nothing. All writes now route through one `$writeOrThrow()`, which **throws rather than exits, because `exit()` does not run `finally`** — exiting from the mutation loop would leave a mutant on disk, the poisoning the file's own header warns about. The restore-failure path **reads the file back instead of inferring**, so *"a mutant is live"* is printed only when one is. Degenerate whole-run result sets (all observed-via-abort, all UNOBSERVED) and empty ones are **refused rather than rendered**; all-observed is deliberately not refused, being a legitimate strong result. A narrowed `--only`/`--limit` run no longer overwrites the artifacts, whose header claims to cover every predicate in `handle()`. Test-tooling only — no `app/` change, no runtime change.
- **card#5796, DL-265** — **`bridge:check` reported a NON-DIRECTORY's file mode as the secret dir's, and told the operator to `chmod 700` it.** **⚠ OPERATOR-VISIBLE on a split layout** (`secret_dir` ≠ `config_dir`); a single-dir install is unaffected (the config-dir check gates on `is_dir()` first). **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change;** `--format=json` `schema` stays **1**. DL-264 (above) gated the shared permission verdict's stat on `file_exists()`, which is true for a regular file, a socket, a fifo — anything that stats. So `BRIDGE_SECRET_DIR=/etc/passwd` reached `fileperms()`, succeeded, and printed *"secret dir /etc/passwd is group/world-accessible (mode 0644) — chmod 700 (it holds secrets)"*: a **real mode for the wrong subject**, whose remedy would be actively harmful if followed, while the operator's actual fault — *this setting does not point at a directory* — was never named. Now a **nested** `! is_dir()` arm INSIDE the existence gate names that fault directly. **Nested, because a single `is_dir()` gate is what DL-264 already rejected:** it answers false for an absent path AND for a present non-directory, so it would report a regular file as *"does not exist"* — trading one false claim for another in the class DL-262/263/264 exist to close. **⚠ It is this primitive's first `fail`, so it flips the exit code where the run previously exited 0.** DL-014's warn-not-fail rationale covers a loose **mode**, where the bridge still works and is merely less safe; it does not reach a path that is not a directory, because every secret and token resolves *underneath* it (`<dir>/<provider>/…`), so not one can be opened and no inbound webhook can be verified — a fault proven about that install, which is `Severity` limb 1 and the same ground the sibling config-dir leg stands on. **No healthy install newly reds:** the only state that flips already rejects every webhook. `DirectoryPermissions::warnIfInsecure()` is renamed **`verdictFor()`** — the old name had already stopped being true when DL-264 gave it an `unvalidated` arm, and a `fail` behind a name promising a warn is a trap for the third caller the primitive exists to protect. **[card#6025:** there is no `DirectoryPermissions::warnIfInsecure()` in the tree today — this entry is the release that renamed it.**]**

- **card#5774, DL-264** — **`bridge:check` ABORTED — stack trace, exit 1, no report for any subsystem — when `BRIDGE_SECRET_DIR` pointed at a directory it could not stat.** **⚠ OPERATOR-VISIBLE on a split layout** (`secret_dir` ≠ `config_dir`); a single-dir install is unaffected (the config-dir check gates first). **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change;** `--format=json` `schema` stays **1**. The shared DL-014 permission verdict ran `$perms = fileperms($dir)` ungated: `fileperms()` on an unstattable path **RAISES** rather than returning `false`, and under Laravel's error handler that warning is an uncaught `ErrorException` — so the whole preflight died on a directory it merely could not measure. **The card filed against this predicted the opposite mechanism** (a silent green line + a silent perms leg) and specified a fix on `$perms === false` that is **unreachable**; it was caught by running the command against the state before writing the fix, not by review. Now gated with `file_exists()` and split by cause: an absent directory is a **`warn`** naming it, one behind an untraversable ancestor is **`unvalidated`** via the shared `PathVisibility` guard (the checking process commonly runs as a different OS user, so *"absent"* is not a conclusion it is entitled to draw). Neither arm flips an exit code — the exit only changes in that a run which previously died with no output now exits on the merits of its findings. **`file_exists()` rather than `is_dir()` is deliberate:** a `secret_dir` pointing at a regular file keeps its pre-existing (wrong-subject but loud) mode warn instead of being quietly reclassified as *"does not exist"*, which would be a new false claim in the very class this fixes. **That wrong-subject report is closed by DL-265 below, which ships in this same release** — it is a nested second gate, not the `is_dir()` swap rejected here. **Sibling audit by reading, not grepping:** every stat-family call in `app/` (`fileperms`, `filetype`, `readlink`) was checked for a pre-gate — `DirectoryPermissions` was the only unguarded one.
- **card#5789, DL-263** — **Five `is_file()`-gated reads threw an undocumented `ErrorException` on a present-but-unreadable file — the inbox reader among them, where the degraded answer is *"no intents staged"* for a file the process simply cannot see into.** **⚠ OPERATOR-VISIBLE on the same ordinary topology as DL-262** (the bridge runs as a different OS user than the agent, DL-227). **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change;** `--format=json` `schema` stays **1**. **No exit code moves in the fixed direction** — four of the five paths already aborted, and the fifth can only turn a 5xx into a served request. DL-262 fixed one member of this class and filed the rest; this lands the consolidation. **The audit found FIVE, not the four on the card, and how matters:** `BridgePaths::readJsonl` uses `file()` rather than `file_get_contents()`, so the grep that produced the card's list could not have surfaced it — it came from re-reading the subsystem (canon #7). Its `?: []` fallback was **already dead code** under the console and HTTP kernels. **Hoisted, not re-derived:** new `FileContents::read($path, $subject)` returns the bytes / `null` when absent / throws `UnreadableFileException` when present-but-unreadable, and `TokenFile::readTrimmed` — which held the only correct copy — now layers trimming and the secret subtype ON it. **`UnreadableSecretException` is retained as a SUBCLASS** so the six callers that catch it do not silently widen to non-secret faults. **Per-site disposition turns on what the benign answer CLAIMS:** `BridgePaths::readSeen` + `readJsonl` **propagate** (fail-soft is strictly worse — `bridge:inbox` filters on both, so `[]` re-surfaces every seen intent or hides the inbox, and `updateSeenLocked` then opens the same file `c+` and throws anyway: a duplicate inbox followed by a crash); `WritebackConfig::load` raises **`ConfigException`** (`null` means *"writeback is off"*, so answering it for a permissions fault would silently disable every card move on the dispatch path); `AgentRegistry::loadSharedIdentities` **degrades to `[]` with a warning** — the one exception, because its other caller is the receiver, which must not 5xx over an optional policy file. **That last one makes existing docs TRUE rather than needing a doc edit:** `SharedIdentitiesCheck`'s docblock, DL-259 and `UnvalidatedCallSiteTest`'s comment all already claimed the loader answers `[]` for a file it could not read. It did not. **A pre-existing defect fixed in passing:** `ProvisionResult::cannotReconcile` hardcoded *"secret **missing** at …"*, but one of its two existing callers already passed a perms message — so a present, `0644` secret was reported as missing; the parameter is now a reason rendered verbatim. **An `@`-suppression sweep was the rejected fix** (it converts every one of these into the "absent" answer — card#5698's defect minted five more times). **Every new assertion was watched go RED** against a pre-fix tree reconstructed by stashing only the tracked `app/` changes — 5 reds, four raw `ErrorException`s and the provisioner's literal `API error: file_get_contents(…)` — each unreadable-state test carries a **skip-if-still-readable** guard, and each propagating site asserts the outcome directly rather than only the exception type. **Test-harness trap recorded in DL-263:** `expectsOutputToContain` registers one Mockery `doWrite` matcher per substring and Mockery dispatches to the FIRST match, so two substrings of one line cannot both be satisfied and a following `doesntExpectOutputToContain` silently cannot fail.
- **card#5778, DL-262** — **A secret file that exists but the bridge cannot read threw an undocumented `ErrorException`: `POST /agent-tools/call` answered **500** to an unauthenticated caller, and `bridge:check` ABORTED on a healthy install.** **⚠ OPERATOR-VISIBLE, and the trigger is ordinary rather than exotic:** a `0600` secret owned by the agent's OS user is exactly what correct provisioning produces on the same-box topology (DL-227), while `bridge:check` reads it as the operator and the receiver reads it as the web user. **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change;** `--format=json` `schema` stays **1**. **No exit code moves in the fixed direction** — the new severity is `unvalidated`, which never flips it, and the run that previously aborted had no exit contract at all. `SecretFile::read()` documented two outcomes and had three: it delegates to `TokenFile::readTrimmed()`, which gates on `! is_file()` and then calls `file_get_contents()` with nothing between them, so a present-but-unreadable file fell through to an uncaught `ErrorException` that **four of seven callers** never expected. **Both pre-gates a caller would reach for PASS in that state** (`is_file` true, `isInsecure` false), so guarding with them was no protection. The **500 broke the controller's own stated contract**, written eight lines below the failing construction — *"Do not distinguish 'unknown token' from 'collided/unreadable token' to the caller"* — by telling an unauthenticated caller that another agent's bearer exists and is unreadable, on the one door reachable with no credential; it now answers **401** like every other unresolvable bearer. **Fixed at the primitive, not with seven guards:** the failed open classifies itself and throws the new `UnreadableSecretException`, with **the open's return value as the authority rather than `is_readable()`** (which answers for the real uid and cannot see an ACL). **The one-line cheap fix was rejected as the defect itself** — an `is_readable()` gate returning `null` converts an unreadable secret into a confident *"no secret here"*, which is card#5698's assert-absence-off-a-permission-denial defect minted into the primitive every secret reader shares. Per-caller dispositions turn on one question — *is the read that failed the read that matters?* — so `BoardToolAgentResolver` reports **`unvalidated`** (it is built both as the operator and as the web user; this **narrows** DL-222's and DL-251(e)'s *"an unreadable bearer FAILs"*, both annotated in place), `WritebackClientFactory` raises the `ConfigException` its callers already expect, and the two `bridge:provision*` commands **`fail`**, because there the operator's own read IS the one that matters. **The measurement trap is worth recording: `tinker` would have DISPROVED this** — psysh installs its own error handler and the same call returns `null` there, so the outcome was decided by ambient state, which is a second defect inside the first and is why the fix depends on no handler. **Six new assertions were each watched go RED** against the un-fixed code (the door test returning 500 with a stack trace through `AgentToolsController:41`), each unreadable-state test carries a **skip-if-still-readable** guard rather than a silent pass under root, and discriminating controls pair every arm so no state could be satisfied by collapsing it onto another. **Sibling audit filed, not folded (card#5789):** four more `is_file()`-gated reads were **measured** to throw the same way — `BridgePaths::readSeen` (*"missing/garbage → []"*), `AgentRegistry::loadSharedIdentities` (**fail-soft by design**, and `SharedIdentitiesCheck` already models this exact state as `unvalidated`, so the check and the loader it certifies disagree), `WritebackConfig::load` (receiver dispatch path), and `WebhookProvisioner`'s reconcile read (rendered as *"API error"*, blaming the upstream for a local permission fault). **Also corrected:** `WritebackClientFactory`'s docblock claimed `ConfigException` for an *insecure* token, which it has never thrown.
- **card#5698 (CLOSES the class), DL-261** — **`bridge:check` reported *"this PHP install has no `fastcgi_finish_request()`"* off a PATH read, so a healthy PHP-FPM install whose `php-fpm` sits in `/usr/sbin` was told to adopt the thing it was already running.** **⚠ OPERATOR-VISIBLE on any install where a `php-fpm` binary is not findable on the PATH the invoking shell exports — which is most non-root logins on several distributions. NO exit code moves in either direction** (`emitFinding()` returns false only for `fail`, and this leg has never emitted one); no migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change; `--format=json` `schema` stays **1** (no key added, and `message` strings are explicitly not part of that contract). The retention preflight asked `ExecutableFinder` whether a `php-fpm` binary was findable, and reported the answer as a fact about the receiver's SAPI. Those are different questions: `bridge:check` is a **console command**, so the CLI SAPI never defines `fastcgi_finish_request()` however the receiver is served, and the PATH it sees belongs to the operator's shell rather than to the receiver's process — the same subject discriminator as DL-260 and DL-254. **Measured, not argued:** the reference host keeps `/usr/sbin/php-fpm8.5` and serves the receiver under Apache + PHP-FPM; from a shell whose PATH includes `/usr/sbin` the leg is silent, and from one without it the same box produces the definite claim. The leg now reports **`unvalidated`**, names both reasons it could not measure, states that a healthy FPM install can keep the binary off a non-root PATH, and **keeps the full latency consequence and both remedies**, conditioned on *if the receiver is NOT served under PHP-FPM*. **The sibling state stays silent rather than becoming a green line** — finding a binary does not establish the receiver's SAPI either, and a disclosure fired on *both* states would print on every run of every install and carry no information. **The method is renamed** `receiverSapiFinishesEarly()` -> `earlyFinishIndicated()`: its docblock claimed it probed *"the fpm binary's own module list"*, which no code has ever read, and **DL-199 carried the same inversion** (*"probing the receiver's SAPI rather than the CLI's"*) — corrected in place at that entry. **34 of 37 golden fixtures change**, and what they witness is the message plus the closing `unvalidated` tally — **a golden capture is plain text and sees no severity at all**, which is why the leg also gained a severity assertion watched go RED (`Warn` vs `Unvalidated`) against the un-fixed code, plus a control proving the disclosure is not unconditional. The `minimal` / `minimal-fpm-present` pin-potency pair is re-pointed and now asserts the retired claim survives in neither half. **Card#5698 is CLOSED** — this was the last of its four sub-shapes (stat-conflation DL-254, partial-map / swallowed-throw DL-255/DL-257, bundled-cause catch DL-260, PATH-proxy here). **Named and not built, recorded on the card:** only the receiver can answer this authoritatively, and `RetentionGate` could record it from inside the request — rejected here because that marker exists only after a webhook has arrived, i.e. never on the fresh install a preflight is read on.
- **card#5698 (partial), DL-259** — **`bridge:check` had never audited its `fail` and `ok` findings against its own severity rule, and four of them asserted things the run had not measured — one of them in GREEN.** **⚠ OPERATOR-VISIBLE on four narrow install shapes; NO exit code moves in either direction. No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change;** `--format=json` `schema` stays **1** on the pre-disclosed-precision row (no key added, no severity invented). DL-251 (stage 10) settled the `warn` ↔ `unvalidated` boundary and said outright that it had swept only legs already REPORTING `warn`, leaving the `fail`/`ok` populations unexamined; card#5698 held that as a bound and required it be run **whole**, since applying a rule site-by-site is how the class re-minted itself. Populations re-derived live — **45 `fail` · 33 `ok`**, up from the recorded 41/32 as DL-254…258 added sites — and all 78 read. **The rule needed no amendment; it needed two corollaries written down**, both of which were being decided ad hoc in per-site prose in contradictory directions, and both now live in `Severity`'s docblock: **(A)** an `ok` may disclose ambiguity about what a measured fact IMPLIES but never its own blindness, so a disclosure splits by its **cause**, not its presence; **(B)** a vacuous universal claim is SILENT when its emptiness is the operator's own config and `ok` when only this run could establish it. **(B) closed a live divergence and changed no behaviour** — the mapped-stage-ids leg's silence and `WritebackSourceCoverageCheck`'s `ok` are both correct, and converging them to look productive would have destroyed a correct output; recording the discriminator was the fix. **Four sites disagreed.** `EventFollowsConsumerCheck`'s action inventory rendered **green** while disclosing that an unreadable declaration may have made it wrong → now `unvalidated` when the run could not obtain a declaration by EITHER shortener (a classifier asked that threw, or an agent never finished reading; gating only the first would have left the same site still asserting past its evidence), while the `undeclared` caveat on the same line **keeps its `ok`** (an `instanceof` returning false is measured — DL-257's own ruling — so only its consequence is open). `SharedIdentitiesCheck` certified `0 shared account(s)` for a file it could not read **and** for one that is not JSON: the loader is fail-soft **by design** (its other caller is the runtime, which must not 5xx over a bad optional file), so the count alone could never carry the signal its docblock claimed — unreadable is now `unvalidated`, malformed is now a **`warn`** (those bytes were read, so it is a measured fault, and a real one: the file is ignored and every agent sharing an account loses attribution silently). `SshTransportProbe` hard-failed *"does not resolve to an OS account on this host"* on any PHP build without **`posix_getpwnam`**, where no account database was consulted at all — fixed at the primitive (`SshProbeEnvironment::homeForUser()` is `?string`: `null` = cannot look up, `''` = looked up and no such account), not at the call site. `BoardToolsHttpProbeCheck` accused *"no bearer at <path>"* off an `is_file()` that is false for `EACCES` exactly as for `ENOENT` — a member DL-254's sweep missed, reading the **same file** its sibling `BoardToolAgentResolver` guards. **Two of the four keep their exit-code-bearing `fail`, and that is the main result rather than a shortfall:** the `InstallConfigDirCheck` precedent governs both — when THIS RUN certainly certified nothing, the exit code is earned and only the CLAIM was wrong, so both get honest two-cause messages instead of a downgrade. The audit moved a severity **only** where the run had genuinely measured nothing; where `BoardToolAgentResolver` *was* downgraded in DL-254, the difference is subject, not shape — it asserts about the RUNTIME's enablement while running as the wrong OS user. **The golden corpus witnessed none of it** — no fixture carries an unreadable shared-identities file, a posix-less host, or an unreadable declaration beside an unlisted action — so **all 33 fixtures are unchanged**, which is both the evidence the re-assignments are inert on covered install shapes and the reason every new arm carries a unit test watched go RED against the un-fixed code. `UnvalidatedCallSiteTest` fired as designed. **Also corrected:** `Severity`'s docblock cited the `EventFollowsConsumerCheck` swallowed throw as a LIVE example two slices after DL-257 closed it. **`docs/CHECK-REGISTRY-PLAN.md`'s three `Still open on the class` enumerations are RETIRED** in favour of a pointer to card#5698, which owns that list — closing the audit invalidates all three on landing, so re-syncing a third copy would have been the defect. **card#5698 remains OPEN**: the bundled-cause catch (`ChannelTokenPathCheck`) and the PATH-proxy leg (`RetentionPostureCheck`) are still on it, and the card is now the only place that list lives.
- **card#5701, DL-258** — **A board this token cannot SEE stops being certified as a board whose cards are all fine.** `readBoardCards()` returns no rows on a **200** for a board whose cards the writeback token's user cannot see — identically to a genuinely empty board. With no cards the per-card loop never ran, `flagged` stayed 0, `truncated` was false, and the leg certified **GREEN**: *"dl_number cards on board N all have a mapped source"*. It examined nothing and reported a pass, which is the invariant `Severity::Ok` says it must never carry — and the same run already printed the DL-029 probe's *"token sees 0 cards … EITHER the board is empty … OR the token's user isn't a member"* beside it. **Nothing available to the leg can disambiguate an empty read:** `visibility()` reports `total === 0` for both worlds and says so itself, and `byRefAvailable()` folds *"not accessible"* together with *"kanban predates by-ref"* while probing only the first mapped board. So the leg **WITHHOLDS** — an empty read yields `unvalidated`. **The discriminator is the EMPTY READ, never "no DL cards found"**: a fully-read board holding no DL card was measured and keeps its `ok`. **No exit-code change** (`unvalidated` is not `fail`), no JSON key moved, `schema` stays 1. No golden file changed — the corpus cannot witness this leg at all (every fixture with a writeback client runs `scan`), re-verified by positive-controlled grep rather than inherited from the docblock.
- **card#5698 (partial), DL-257** — **A classifier `bridge:check` could not ASK was reported as a classifier that declares nothing — and then its consumed events were reported as silently dropped.** **⚠ OPERATOR-VISIBLE on installs running a classifier that throws from `consumedEventTypes()`, and on those only. No exit-code change** (the lines that moved were `warn`; the lines replacing them are `warn` + `unvalidated`, and `unvalidated` never flips the exit); **no migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change.** `--format=json` gains `event_consumers.scopes[].unreadable` and `schema` stays `1` — an added key is not a bump. The per-agent loop probes `DeclaresConsumedEvents` **out of process** (DL-025) and then asks the resolved instance what it consumes; that call's `catch (Throwable)` wrote `declared: false`, **the same value a classifier that does not implement the interface gets**, so *"it declares and we could not ask it"* and *"it does not declare"* were one state. Two false claims followed: the operator was told the classifier **does not declare its consumed events** (false — the probe had just said it does), and the emptied declaration set made `unconsumed()` over-inclusive, so the **silently dropped on arrival** warn could accuse a scope whose arrivals that very classifier consumes. Fixed with a THIRD STATE — `declared` is `?bool`, `null` written only in the `catch` and routed to a new `unreadable` bucket — the same shape as DL-255's null scope set and DL-256's null id list. The throw is DISCLOSED as a `warn` (it is measured, and it is the operator's real defect: `consumedEventTypes()` must be a pure `$cfg` → event-types map) while the dropped-arrival **verdict** is withheld as `unvalidated`, mirroring the incomplete-roster arm directly above it. **Path (C) deliberately still reports `false`** — a probe/booted-process disagreement is a measured in-process fact, not an inability to measure. **One trap this change had to avoid rather than discover:** the action inventory's *possible false inventory* caveat was gated on the `undeclared` set that a throwing classifier used to be in, so moving it out would have silently deleted a line that used to print — invisible to every test (the finding is `ok`) and to all 36 golden fixtures (none reached it); the caveat now carries a second clause in its own wording, which preserves reach and adjudicates nothing about the `ok` population. **The corpus could not witness this**: no fixture ran a classifier that throws from `consumedEventTypes()`, so one was added as the discriminating PAIR for `event-consumer-unconsumed-type` — same scope, same arrival, same unconsumed type, differing only in whether the declaration could be read — and **no existing golden file changed**. **card#5698 remains OPEN**: this closes its swallowed-throw slice only; the bundled-cause catch, the PATH-proxy leg, the two `WritebackBoardStateCheck` round-3 members and the 41-fail / 32-ok severity audit are still on it.
- **card#5698 (partial), DL-255** — **An agent whose config `bridge:check` could not read was reported as an agent that does not exist: one unreadable YAML turned every scope-keyed question into a confident "the operator did not enable it".** **⚠ OPERATOR-VISIBLE on runs that already FAIL, and on those only. No exit-code change is possible here by construction** (both abort sites set the run's verdict to failed *before* recording, and the new findings are `unvalidated`, which never flips the exit); **no migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change.** `--format=json` gains a top-level **`agent_scope_coverage`** key and `schema` stays `1` — an added key is not a bump, and the three re-assigned severities are the precision correction §4 of `docs/check-json-contract.md` pre-disclosed. `handle()`'s per-agent loop accumulates three scope maps — which github scopes have a writeback-emitting classifier, which enable the coord-card-move family, and which classifiers consume which event types — and it accumulates all three *after* both of its aborts, so an agent whose YAML did not parse (or whose classifier did not resolve) is in none of them. Every downstream consumer then read absence as an answer: the mapping for that scope was **ORPHANED** ("add an agent" — for an agent the operator already has, whose real fault a line above already named), no agent **enables the coord-card-move family** ("edit the families list" — possibly already correct in the config that failed to load), the arrived events were **silently dropped** ("add a consuming family"), and the DL-200 coord-terminal preflight — MANDATORY, the read that makes the bridge owning a terminal stage id legitimate at all — was skipped **in silence**, which is the one arm where a missing input was standing in for agreement. Fixed as ONE fact rather than four guards: `CheckContext::$agentScopeCoverage` records the agents this run did not finish reading, **and the github scopes each is known to subscribe to** — a classifier-gate abort has a parsed config, so its subscriptions are a real answer and an unrelated mapping's finding must survive it untouched (a bare abort *count* would have softened every writeback finding on the install for one broken YAML; a `null` scope set, meaning "the load itself is what would have told us", is kept distinct from an empty one, which rules that agent out of every scope). **One map-fed arm deliberately gets no disclosure and the omission is a ruling:** the `family-on, terminal-missing` warn asserts nothing when the scope is absent — it is scoped to family-enabled scopes precisely so a pure PR-writeback mapping stays quiet — so an unread agent costs it no false claim, and disclosing there would print on every mapping of every run with an unreadable agent config. **The golden corpus could not witness ANY of this**: not one of its 33 fixtures combined an aborted agent with a writeback mapping, so two were added — one where all three disclosures fire, and one where the ORPHANED accusation must SURVIVE an unrelated agent's abort. **card#5698 remains OPEN**, and one of its own filings is corrected in passing: the swallowed-throw half of its partial-map pair is **not** closed by this (its mechanism is a `catch` turning *"the classifier was never asked"* into `declared: false`, which no coverage ledger reaches), and two of its four "round-3" members are not map-fed at all — the `boardCustomFieldKeys()` legs belong to the `idList()` primitive shape. The bundled-cause catch, the PATH-proxy leg and the `idList()` collapse are likewise still on it.
- **card#5698 (partial), DL-254** — **`bridge:check` reported a path it could not SEE as a path that was not THERE — accusing healthy installs of missing secrets, tokens and directories, and in one case flipping the exit code.** **⚠ ONE EXIT-CODE CHANGE, in the safe direction: an install where `bridge:check` cannot traverse to a board-tools bearer previously exited non-zero on a false accusation and now reports `unvalidated` instead. No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change; `--format=json` `schema` stays 1 (`message` strings are explicitly not part of that contract).** `is_dir()`/`is_file()` return false for a permission denial exactly as for a removed path, so a leg reading that bare `false` as ABSENCE states a definite fact its evidence cannot support — and prescribes the wrong cure: *run bridge:provision* for an already-provisioned secret, *Repoint channel.socket* for a correct config, *place a least-privilege token* for a token already in place. This is ordinary rather than exotic — the bridge commonly runs as a different OS user than the agent (DL-227) and an agent's own directories are commonly `0700`. **The guard that solves it already existed and had been PRIVATE to one class since DL-230**, which is why every new stat re-minted the defect; it is now `App\Bridge\Support\PathVisibility`, adopted by `ChannelSnapshotProbe` (its author, now a consumer), `ChannelTransportCheck`, `WritebackAlertChannelCheck`, `AgentWebhookSecretCheck`, `WritebackTokenCheck`, and `BoardToolAgentResolver` — the last being the exit-code one, because `bridge:check` runs as the operator while the resolver actually serving board tools is built by the receiver as the **web** user. **Two members of the filed class were re-derived away rather than fixed:** `AgentApiTokenCheck` was never a member (its message already says *"not readable"*, true under `EACCES` and `ENOENT` alike), and **`InstallConfigDirCheck` KEEPS its `fail`** — `CheckCommand` gates the whole agent loop on the same `is_dir()`, so this run certainly loaded no agent config either way; downgrading it would have been loosening a check to make a failure go away. Only its message was wrong, and it now names both causes plus the fact that every per-agent check below it is MISSING rather than clean. `BoardToolAgentResolver::problems()` now carries `Finding`s, restoring the discrimination card#5292 removed **with** its explicit invitation to restore it — DL-251's ruling is untouched, since both faults it settled are measured and both still `fail`. **card#5698 remains OPEN**: this closes its stat-conflation sub-shape only; the bundled-cause catch, the PATH-proxy leg, the swallowed-throw pair, the three-`CheckContext`-maps root cause and the `idList()` collapse are still on it.
- **card#5738, DL-253** — **A counterparty's action on a thread YOU opened was dropped as your own echo, and the seat never saw it — including every bare reply, the exact population DL-235's grant was built to wake.** **⚠ DELIVERED VOLUME GOES UP on affected channels — see the discriminator below. No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no exit-code change, no token-scope change, no correlation change, and no wire-shape change** (`Intent`, `payload` and `docs/event-schema.json` are untouched — this changes only WHICH events are dispatched, never how one reads). Under a shared upstream identity (DL-002) `CoordinationClassifier` handed the dispatcher a UNION of *who acted* and *whose thread it is*; the dispatcher DROPS on that value (DL-005). The `from:<agent>` label is frozen at thread-open (DL-035), so on a **bodyless** action — `reopened`, `closed`, `ready_for_review`, anything an install adds via `coord_extra_actions` — a counterparty's action on a thread the serving agent had opened carried that agent's own name into the echo recheck and was suppressed as `echo: own write (reattributed author)`. Same for a **bare reply with no `FROM:` line**, whose only recoverable name is that same label — the population one operator measured at **112 dropped coordination comments per impl agent**, and the grant DL-235 shipped to close it was being undone one step downstream. The echo input is now the **actor** DL-252 already derives, and `echo_author` is **deleted** rather than narrowed (one fact, one place — no union left for a future caller to re-read). **`null` now DELIVERS**, because the real choice is which error to make when the event evidences nobody: **a wrong delivery costs one read; a wrong drop costs the message.** **Own-write suppression is fully preserved wherever the actor is genuinely evidenced** — `scope_author_map`, the `from:` label on the action that OPENED the subject, the body `FROM:` line on the action that WROTE it — and a different shared-id agent's write still surfaces. **Residual cost, stated not discovered:** an agent's own *evidence-free* action on its own thread (its own `reopened`/`closed`, or its own `FROM:`-less comment) now wakes it — bounded, since a protocol-conformant post names its author and stays suppressed, and not a loop (a coordination intent is a wake, never a write). **A config knob was rejected**: one of the two settings drops other people's mail, and that is not a preference. **Who is affected is one grep:** a channel inherits this iff it **inherits the `wake_membership` default** AND **opens its own threads** — an explicitly-configured `wake_membership` cannot be reached by it. On the reporting operator's install: 2 explicitly-configured PM channels unaffected, 3 inheriting impl seats affected, 5 utility channels inheriting but opening no threads (no population); they audited for any baseline keyed on delivered comment volume and found none. **Test debt closed, and it generalises:** every assertion that a coordination event WAKES an agent had stopped at the `ClassifyResult` — DL-235's own grant test passed on the day it shipped while the dispatcher dropped the event it certified — so a wake claim now gets an end-to-end `DispatchServiceTest` counterpart driving the real classifier through dispatch and asserting the OUTCOME plus the wake leaving the box. **Doc-sync — the shipped guides taught the defect:** `docs/customization.md` and `docs/multi-agent.md` both instructed classifier authors to recover "the true **author**" and return it (customization.md citing this repo's own union as a pattern to copy), so both now state the contract as *this agent performed this event* with an explicit return-null-when-unevidenced; `docs/consumer-guide.md` § Echo-suppression semantics gains the bound its flat "consumers will never see the agent's own writes" no longer supports under a shared identity. Notice given to the affected operator on roundtable #209 before ship, on the same terms as the card#5734 agreement. Suite 1890/1890 (5584 assertions), phpstan L7 0, pint clean, `check-doc-refs.php` clean.
- **card#5721, DL-252** — **A bodyless coordination event reported the thread's OPENER as the agent that acted, and a seat that had never heard of the defect posted that belief as fact.** **⚠ CONSUMER-VISIBLE on the coord intent + its rendered summary; no migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no exit-code change, no token-scope change, and no correlation change** (the card/DL token grammars are untouched). `CoordinationClassifier` recovered ONE name (`scope_author_map`, else the `from:<agent>` label, else the body `FROM:` line) and put it in the Intent's **`Actor`** and the summary's `from <name>` slot. That is right on `issue_comment.created` and `issues.opened` — where the actor wrote the text — and wrong on every other handled action: the `from:` label is the **frozen thread-opener** (DL-035), so `issues.reopened` / `closed` / `pull_request.ready_for_review` / anything added via `coord_extra_actions` reported the opener as the actor, confidently and in the same shape as the correct cases. Under a shared upstream identity (DL-002) the webhook `sender` is one account for every seat, so the true actor is **unrecoverable, not unimplemented** — the fix is to stop implying one. `attribute()` replaces `reattribute()` and returns the facts separately: the Intent's `actor` is now null on those actions, `payload` gains **`actor_attribution`** (always present — `resolved` / `unresolved` / `unattributable`, three states because "nobody wrote it down" and "no evidence can exist" are different) and **`thread_author`** (the datum, correctly named), `payload.from` is null exactly on `unattributable`, and the summary renders `by an unattributable actor (thread opened by <name>)` where it read `from <name>`. The marker is the fix, not polish: `actor.name` was already nullable with null meaning "the registry could not resolve it", and to a *reading agent* an absent field renders as nothing at all — indistinguishable from a summary that just didn't say who, which is the gap the misled seat filled from context. The affected set is **derived**, not hand-listed: `AUTHORING_ACTIONS` names the one action per prefix that CREATES the text read, `subject()` computes the flag from the same prefix/action pair that selects the subject, and it is an allow-list, so a future or operator-added action is non-authoring by default — a widening can lose a name, never invent one. A **sibling** in the same primitive is fixed with it, and it moves the wire on an **authoring** action — so the consumer-visible change has TWO shapes, not one: a comment *preferred* its body `FROM:` line but **fell back** to the frozen label, so a bare reply by anyone on someone else's thread reported the opener as its author. On that shape (an `issue_comment.created` with no `FROM:` line, on a `from:<opener>`-labelled thread) `payload.from` now carries the actor's raw upstream id instead of the opener's name, `actor_attribution` is `unresolved`, and the summary goes from `coord comment #9 from <opener>: T` to `coord comment #9 from 99: T`. **Explicitly NOT fixed by THIS card and pinned by test** *(— and fixed in this same release by **card#5738 / DL-253**, below; the two pins did their job and went red the moment it was narrowed)*: `ClassifyResult::reattributedActor` still carried the pre-change union, because the dispatcher DROPS on it (DL-005) — so a counterparty's reopen of a thread you opened was still suppressed as your own write, and so was a bare `FROM:`-less reply (the case DL-235's grant exists to wake). Narrowing that changes what is delivered and was gated separately; DL-252 (f) states the bound as it stood. Doc-sync: `docs/consumer-guide.md` gains the per-`kind` attribution contract; `docs/config-schema.md` (`scope_author_map`, `coord_extra_actions`); and `docs/customization.md` + `ClassifyResult`'s docblock, which both claimed *every shipped classifier leaves `reattributedActor` null* — false since `CoordinationClassifier` shipped. **Two doc corrections worth calling out on their own**, both found by re-reading rather than by grep: `docs/customization.md`'s shared-identity worked example told a classifier author to display `$reattributed ?? $actor` — i.e. to commit the exact conflation this release removes, ten lines above the new bullet saying that value is not a display value — and now says what the recovered name is for (the echo check) and points at the consumer guide for the display question; and the consumer guide's `resolved` state enumerated two evidence paths where the code has **three** — the registry resolving the actor's upstream account (declared in some agent's `identity`, not in `shared-identities.json`) reports `resolved` on **any** action, including the non-authoring ones, which is the default topology for an install that shares no account, and is the one path that is *not* operator-writable text.
- **card#5691, DL-250** — **The FR-7 near-miss warning was silent on every PLURAL spelling of the card token, and every guard over that grammar was blind to it at once because all of them ask the same hand-authored corpus.** `\bcard` matches inside `cards`, the probe's optional separator group cannot consume the `s`, and `\d` then fails against it with no second word boundary to retry from — so `cards #123`, `cards#123`, `cards-123`, `cards123`, `cards_123`, `cards:123` and `cards.123` all reached the probe's null-parse-only call sites and warned **nothing**. None of them correlate either, so the whole family was the silent no-op the probe exists to convert into a loud one: the branch publishes, the card never moves, nobody is told. The **finding** is one layer up and is what DL-250 records — the DL-239 ties are sound, but each is keyed to `CardTokenGrammar::VECTORS` — eight legs across two files ask that one list — so a shape absent from it is invisible to all of them **at once** and presents as eight independent greens. Fixed by making the coverage derived rather than listed: the probe moves onto `CardTokenGrammar` with its separators as **data** (`NEAR_MISS_SEPARATORS`), the pattern and the corpus that covers it (`probeVectors()`) are two consumers of that one definition, and `VECTORS` — the *sentence* corpus that renders the operator-facing log line — gains exactly one plural row rather than seven. Two guards were added because neither existed: the `looks=` CI predicate is now tied to the **runtime** probe answer-set to answer-set (previously only to the vector set, so the two could diverge on any shape outside it) over the **union of both engines' separator domains**, so a narrowing on either side reds it and not only a widening; and `GitHubPrCardMoveClassifierTest`'s hand-written three-element near-miss list — the only leg that exercises the runtime surface, and the one that stayed green through the corpus edit that reddened three lint legs — is retired for a grammar-driven loop covering both call sites. **No accept/reject change:** which text correlates to which card is untouched; only which non-correlating text gets a warning. **Disclosed alongside that:** widening the CI lint's `looks=` predicate to `cards?` also adds **warn-noise** on branches that are legitimately plural — a `cards` + separator + digit shape (`feat/cards-2-column-layout`, `board cards-12 layout`, `refactor the cards.2 view`, `cards_2_grid`, `cards2go`) now draws a `::warning::` where it drew none. Bounded to that shape (`reorder the cards later` still warns nowhere) and never a merge block — the leg emits `::warning::` and its exit code is pinned to 0 — and it is the price of catching `cards-123`, which is the same string an author who *did* mean a card writes. One deliberate narrowing: the probe's space separator is now a literal ` #` rather than `\s#`, matching the bash predicate it is now tied to.
- **card#5553, DL-247** — **Three of the 33 `bridge:check` golden fixtures did not exercise the subject they were named for — and nothing asserted that a golden fixture MEASURES anything.** A golden file is captured once and thereafter only ever compared against itself, so **a fixture that never reached its install shape is indistinguishable from one that works**. The three failed by three different mechanisms (card#5552): `board-tools-http-enabled` carried `channel.auth.token_path` with no `channel.url`, so `AgentConfig::load` threw, `$configs` was empty and the whole board-tools plane was unreachable — the bearer moves to `board_tools.auth.token_path`, a first-class shape the config's own error text recommends, which keeps the fixture channel-free and so the http TWIN of the ssh fixtures; `writeback-board-unreadable` registered its 500 **after** `moveLegInstall`'s `'*'` catch-all, and **Laravel matches stub callbacks in registration order**, so the catch-all answered everything and the fixture captured the fully HEALTHY output — byte-identical to `writeback-move-leg-coord-config-unset` — with the stubs now passing THROUGH `moveLegInstall` ahead of the defaults and scoped to the kanban board reads, so the fixture carries one diagnosis rather than also failing the unrelated github token probe; and `retention-last-pass-failed` set the cache marker in its builder while `PinnedHost::apply()` cleared it one step later, because `apply()` necessarily runs AFTER the builder — the ambient-cache reset moves to `resetAmbientState()`, called BEFORE it. **Two guards close the root cause:** every fixture declares ≥1 substring its render must contain (`subjects()`), asserted on the capture in the same data-provider run and **AFTER** the golden compare, so an `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` regen cannot mint a fixture that measures nothing — the three deliberately-silent fixtures also declare what must stay ABSENT, **never instead**, because a `notContains` alone is satisfied by an empty capture; and byte-identical golden files red unless declared in `CONTROL_PAIRS`, where a declared pair must STILL be identical, so a pairing that stops holding is caught in both directions. **The subject lookup is STRICT rather than `?? []`** — a missing declaration fails at the fixture itself instead of relying on `test_every_fixture_declares_a_subject` continuing to exist, since a guard that silently asserts nothing when its declaration is absent is exactly the shape this change removes.
- **card#5575, DL-246** — **The channel-snapshot tripwire was guarding a file that no longer held its subject, and its CI job could never run on the PRs that break it.** Two assertions in `bin/test_check_channel_snapshot.py` guard that path — that `ChannelSnapshotProbe` cannot execute a process, and that the call into it passes only strings — but the DL-242 stage-7 migration moved that call from `CheckCommand::handle()` into `ChannelSnapshotCheck`, and the guard went on scanning `CheckCommand.php`. **The near-miss IS the finding: it survived as a RED only because it was an exact equality against one literal argument string.** Written the way such a guard is usually written it would have been **GREEN over an empty result set, guarding nothing, indefinitely.** The call-site guard now walks all of `app/`, **asserts at least one call site EXISTS** (naming the lost-its-subject failure by name), and judges arguments against a grammar rather than pinned text — anything containing `(` fails by construction. The no-exec guard gains an allow-list compared by exact equality **in both directions** (so a permission nothing uses fails too) plus a transitive exec scan over every reachable collaborator — `Finding` AND the `Severity` it reaches — with the bound stated rather than implied: one hop over a declared list, not a call-graph proof. The glob `app/**` joins the workflow path filter, and this is the half that made the guard unreachable: the tests assert about `app/` while the filter listed only `bin/` plus the channel server, so **no `app/`-only PR could ever run them** — the migration that broke the guard could not be caught by it, and `dev` stayed red unseen until an unrelated PR touched `examples/channel-servers/**`. **`CLAUDE.md` rule 5 stops enumerating CI workflows** — it named four, this job was added later and never joined the list, so a literal reader self-merges over this red. Sibling audit: honest **NEGATIVE** — only two workflows have path filters and the other runs entirely under `working-directory: examples/channel-servers`, reading nothing outside it; one instance, closed, no second. Zero `app/` change, zero runtime change; CI now runs one more job on `app/` PRs. 5 positive-control arms, each mutating the SUBJECT rather than the assertion, including one that recreates this defect exactly.

### Docs
- **DL-271** — **Comments that DELEGATE a check to a callee which cannot run it for the cited case — the canon-#7 sweep DL-270 flagged and did not run.** Population **re-derived, not recalled**: 4,216 comment blocks → 1,324 vocabulary candidates, all read and classified. **The first extractor was wrong, and that is recorded rather than quietly fixed** — a regex comment-opener reads `'/*.yml'` (a real glob in `CheckCommand`) as a block comment, which injected code into the candidate set AND masked real comments inside the false span, and its negative control could not fail for that shape. It was replaced with each language's own tokenizer, negative control re-pinned to the exact line that broke v1 and shown to discriminate. **Findings, all comment-only: ONE class, TWO instances.** `BoardToolsRegistry` and `Tool` justify the board-tools surface by naming the HTTP door's guards (loopback route + controller bearer). **Neither runs for `bridge:tools-call`** — the ssh door DL-223 added later, which reaches the same registry via `BoardToolDispatcher` with no route and no bearer. Both files have exactly one commit (DL-217); the ssh door landed afterwards. The conclusions still hold via `ToolsCallCommand`'s own gate, **so nothing was insecure — what was wrong is that an auditor reads two named guards and stops.** Fixed by **deleting the restatement and pointing at the dispatcher**, which already owns the door set, rather than re-syncing two copies of the call graph. Separately, `ValidatorsTest` claimed its patterns must stay identical to a Python provisioner at `lib/validators.py` — **a path that has never existed in this repository** (the v0.12.0 rewrite is the root commit) and no Python source carries either pattern; the method name carried the same claim and was renamed with it, corrected to the true rationale — a receiver-boundary accept-set whose `..` rejection is the path-traversal defense for the per-scope secret filename. Eight further delegations were verified truthful (delegate opened, guard reachable on the cited path) and are **recorded as checked rather than left implicitly clean**; the sweep stopped on its stated bound, not on convergence, and every member whose delegate was not opened is named individually on the card. No behavior change, no migration, no `.env`, no token-scope change, no change to what the receiver or either board-tools door accepts.
- **DL-243** (card#5551) — **Fifteen Check-package comments stated a FIXTURE-scope measurement at whole-suite strength.** The shape was *"no golden fixture reaches X, so `XCheckTest` is its whole measurement"*. A 12-arm mutation run over the full suite (1,735 baseline / 1,681 pass, 54 red) settled every one: **six false, five hold** — the false ones now name the command-level test that reaches the arm, and the five that hold now say they were **mutation-measured**, so a reader can tell a proven claim from a guessed one. It also deletes every claim of membership in `docs/check-golden-coverage.md`'s disclosed-gap list: that file is generated from and bounded to `CheckCommand::handle()`, so a migrated check is no longer in it and no regeneration can put it back — **those claims are false by construction rather than stale** (its gap table then held exactly one row). The two rules live in `CLAUDE_TESTING.md` alone and the sites point at them rather than restating them (card#5547). **A follow-up pass then said which mutation FORM was run:** the five *"reds `YTest` and nothing else in the suite"* claims came from a 12-arm BATCH run, not five single-arm runs — sound, since the mutants were severity-preserving so exit codes could not move and a cross-arm message-overlap check found none, but a reader six months out cannot tell a batch verdict from a single-arm one, and the difference is real (a test observing two mutated arms reds once and is attributed to one of them). Caught by the adversarial pass on that PR's own diff: the batch's subject is claims stated past their instrument, so its own evidence got the same bar. Comments and docs only — no app logic, no golden fixture, no predicate change. The follow-on **gate** is card#5564 / DL-244, filed there rather than bundled here because it changes what the pipeline rejects.
- **card#5739** — **Three doc sites still shipped the pre-DL-025 positional `classify()` signature, so a class written from either worked example does not satisfy the `Classifier` interface.** An operator following the guide gets an **uncatchable `E_COMPILE_ERROR` on the next live delivery** — the exact failure DL-025's own callout warns about. The sites: `docs/multi-agent.md` (Path C), a positional signature on one line; `docs/multi-host.md`, **the same signature spread over seven lines, which no single-line grep for `function classify(` could see**; and `CLAUDE_ARCHITECTURE.md`, which described the contract positionally AND pre-DL-022 (no serving `AgentConfig` at all) — the stalest of the three. **Both worked examples are ported in full, not just at the signature:** the bodies read the retired parameters as `$ctx->` field accesses, and the `use` blocks are re-derived (`AgentConfig` drops out of both, `Actor` drops out of multi-host, `ClassifyContext` is added). The same DL-025 sweep had also missed `CLAUDE_ARCHITECTURE.md`'s core-data-shapes file list, which omitted `ClassifyContext.php`. **Verified by LOADING every classifier example in the doc corpus against the real interface:** all 5 declarable examples now satisfy it, and the pre-fix copies of the two edited ones fatal with *"Declaration … must be compatible with"* — the carded defect reproduced. `examples/channel-servers/README.md`'s `classify(...)` is a prose elision of a shipped class and is signature-agnostic, deliberately untouched; `CLAUDE_DECISIONS.md` DL-022 and this changelog are append-only history of the then-current signature and are correct as written. Docs only — no runtime code, no behaviour change.
- **card#5466** — **The bash-collation paragraph counted locale NAMES, not distinct locales.** It said *"Measured across four locales"* and then enumerated four **names** — but `POSIX` is glibc's alias for `C`, so only **three** distinct locales were ever measured. The count overstated the independence of the data points, and **this exact figure has now been miscounted twice in opposite directions**: four in PR #421's body, then a false "correction" to two, since retracted. Re-measured on the box before the edit rather than recalled — `LC_ALL=C` vs `LC_ALL=POSIX` give an identical `locale -k LC_COLLATE` keyword set, identical charmap (`ANSI_X3.4-1968`), both single-byte; `C.UTF-8` vs `C` are genuinely distinct (collate-codeset UTF-8, multibyte-aware). **An `LC_ALL=bogus.INVALID` control reads 2, so the len-1 result proves `C.UTF-8` was honored rather than silently falling back — which is what makes the count three and not two.** The finding itself reproduces unchanged: `dl-<U+0663>` against `(^|[^0-9a-z_])dl-[0-9]{1,4}([^0-9]|$)` matches under bash `[[ =~ ]]` ONLY in `en_US.UTF-8`, while GNU `grep -E` rejects it under all four names. **Scope is the count claim alone** — the divergence, the FALSE-GREEN harm, the pinned-not-fixed disposition and card#5300's ownership of the repair are untouched, as are the lint's regexes and every test. Sibling-audited by shape rather than by the search term: `docs/CHANGELOG.md` and `tests/Feature/Workflows/PrTitleLintTest.php` enumerate the same locale names but assert no count, so this was the only live site.
- **Solo-orientation** — the managed solo-orientation block was re-synced to coord v0.25.0 (#413), v0.26.0 (#432), v0.27.0 (#444) and v0.28.0 (#466). Managed block only — no bridge code, no behaviour change.

### Security
- **card#5915** — **The reference channel server shipped two HIGH CVEs on both `main` and `dev`, and the audit that would have caught them structurally cannot fire on `dev`.** Lockfile-only dependency bump plus the DL-038 snapshot version bump it requires — **no app code, no migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change.** Measured on the pinned tree, not read off an advisory feed: `npm audit --audit-level=high` → **5 vulnerabilities (3 moderate, 2 high), exit 1**. **HIGH** `ip-address` 10.2.0 — GHSA-mwp4-54f8-5fhr (leading-zero octets decoded as decimal where resolvers decode octal), GHSA-4xrf-jv44-h6hh (a CIDR suffix suppressing special-use classification), GHSA-22jq-vg5j-6vgg (IPv4-mapped/NAT64 misclassification), each an SSRF / trust-boundary bypass; **HIGH** `fast-uri` 3.1.4 — GHSA-7p8r-x3mc-p8w7, host confusion via a backslash authority introducer (note the advisory range is INCLUSIVE of 3.1.4, which is the vulnerable version, not the fixed one). Moderates: `hono` 4.12.31 (GHSA-8j4g-w8fx-2239, CORS-middleware ReDoS) and `@hono/node-server` 1.19.14 (GHSA-frvp-7c67-39w9, Windows `serve-static` path traversal), the latter reached through `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.29.0. This is not a dev-only tree: the component reads a bearer token and accepts loopback POSTs **as the agent's OS user**, and `main` pinned identically. **Resolved as ONE maintainer PR rather than three dependabot ones** (precedent #356): every single-advisory PR is red on BOTH legs by construction — the audit still finds the other two highs, so no such PR can ever green it, and dependabot cannot bump the manifest the DL-038 guard requires. New pins: `ip-address` 10.4.0 · `fast-uri` 3.1.5 · `hono` 4.13.0 · `@hono/node-server` 1.19.14 → **2.1.0** · `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.29.0 → 1.30.0; snapshot version 0.9.0 → 0.9.1 (DL-038, and both `package-lock.json` version sites move with it per DL-268). **The `@hono/node-server` MAJOR is sanctioned by the direct dependency, not forced by the resolver** — sdk 1.30.0 declares `"@hono/node-server": "^1.19.9 || ^2.0.5"`, read from the lock rather than assumed. **The sdk is a DIRECT dependency (`^1.0.0`), so a lockfile-only audit says nothing about whether the server still runs** — validated by running the tree, not by reading it: `npm ci --ignore-scripts` rc 0, `node --test tests/` **27/27** (the suite spawns the REAL server and drives it over the MCP SDK stdio client, so the sdk/node-server move is exercised end to end), `npm audit --audit-level=high` rc 0, and the DL-237 seat-side launch assert `python3 bin/check-channel-snapshot.py examples/channel-servers` → **LAUNCH OK**. That assert was given a way to fail before it was trusted: the same check against a copy of the tree with no `node_modules` returns **LAUNCH FAILED, exit 1** — a launch assert that cannot fail is a decoration.
- **The structural half is deliberately NOT fixed here and stays open on card#5915.** The audit job is `paths:`-scoped to `examples/channel-servers/**` plus its own workflow file, with a weekly cron — and a scheduled run executes on the **default** branch (`main`). So a newly-disclosed CVE in the pinned tree is invisible on `dev` until some PR happens to touch that directory: this batch surfaced only because card#5232's PR edited the workflow file. **The timing is measured, not inferred:** the last cron on `main` ran **2026-08-03 08:44Z** and was green because five of these six advisories published **later that same day** — GHSA-7p8r-x3mc-p8w7 at 19:16Z through GHSA-8j4g-w8fx-2239 at 20:23Z, ten hours after that run — so the next cron would have caught them on `main` up to six days late, and on `dev` never. **The sixth passed two crons legitimately and is worth stating so it does not read as a hole:** `@hono/node-server`'s GHSA-frvp-7c67-39w9 published 2026-07-21 and was visible to the 07-27 and 08-03 runs, which were green because it is **moderate** — below the `--audit-level=high` gate those runs assert. Widening the trigger changes when CI can reject a PR, which is gated.

## [0.71.0] - 2026-07-27

**Minor — the coordination wake path gains a narrow bare-reply grant and an actor deny-list, the card-token grammar converges with the toolkit, and `bridge:check` stops rendering never-ran checks green.** 10 PRs since v0.70.0 (#400–#409). The receiver's HMAC/dedup/dispatch core is untouched; the changes land in the coordination classifier's membership gate, the PR-correlation token grammar, and the `bridge:check` reporting vocabulary.

**⚠ UPGRADING — three changes alter behavior on an install that configured nothing:**

1. **`bare_reply_to_own_thread` (DL-235) ships IN the `wake_membership` default**, which becomes `[to_me, to_all, comment_to, bare_reply_to_own_thread]`. An install with **no explicit** `wake_membership` starts live-waking on **bare** replies (no `TO:` line) to threads it opened. It is echo-guarded — a comment whose body `FROM:` line is the agent itself never wakes it — and an explicit `TO: someone-else` still narrows. To keep them dark, set `wake_membership` explicitly and omit it. An install that already sets `wake_membership` explicitly is unchanged.
2. **The classifier now correlates the glued `card<id>` token (DL-233)** with a **≥2-digit floor** on the glued arm. A PR whose title or head branch carries `card4524` — accepted by the toolkit's `board-card-start` since v0.17.0 and silently non-correlating here — now moves card 4524. Separated `card-3` / `card#3` are unchanged; `card4`, `discard4524`, and `wildcard-2` still correlate to nothing.
