3. **`bridge:check` emits a new `unvalidated` severity** for an undeclared `channel.server_path` and for a fail-soft-skipped event-consumer check, rendered plain rather than green. **Exit codes are unchanged by construction** (only `fail` returns false), and nothing changed about what `bridge:check` accepts or rejects.

**No migration, no new required `.env`, no token-scope change, no receiver accept/reject change.** PHP 1426/1426 (3063 assertions), phpstan L7 0, pint clean.

### Added
- **card#5108, DL-229** — **`bridge:check` now certifies the DEPLOYED channel-server snapshot, not just the running socket.** The channel legs read the live process; nothing read the FILES the next session respawns from, so a stale or unlaunchable `examples/channel-servers` copy reported `channel socket live` and exited **0** — the failure only materialized at the next session start, as live-wake silently never coming back. New **optional** per-agent key **`channel.server_path`** (absolute path to the deployed channel-server directory, or its entry `.mjs`, normalized to the directory at load; validated like `channel.socket` — absolute, no `..`, no null byte, else throws). It is **never inferred** — the bridge may run as a different OS user and cannot read the agent's `.mcp.json`, so inference would produce a confident wrong answer; the precedent is `board_tools.ssh_account` (DL-224). **Absent ⇒ the snapshot legs SKIP with a notice**, never silently and never a fail (byte-identical exit for every existing install). Present, `bridge:check` resolves it **non-strictly** and branches, in order: **(1)** does not resolve ⇒ **FAIL** (*dangling symlink or removed directory — the link target moved, repoint the symlink*), or, when it resolves to something that is not a directory, a separate **FAIL** (*names a file, not the channel-server directory* — only the `.mjs` entry form is normalized for you, so `server.js`/a wrapper lands here and must not be sent after a symlink that isn't there); tested BEFORE classification because a dangling link resolves to a stale path that differs from the checkout and would otherwise version-compare against a directory that is not there; **(2)** resolves to **this checkout's** `examples/channel-servers` (the common symlink topology, which looks external but resolves internal) ⇒ the version compare would be a self-compare, so it is skipped; **(3)** differs ⇒ the version leg runs too. **Presence leg (FAIL ×2):** the deployed directory must contain the entry `.mjs` (absent ⇒ *`channel.server_path` does not point at a channel-server deployment; the MCP server will not launch at next session start* — this is what stops a `server_path` naming an empty directory from certifying clean) and a **`node_modules`** (absent ⇒ *dependencies are not installed — run `npm ci` in it*: bare specifiers *resolving* is npm's business, but their directory being absent is not — a `cp -R` without the follow-up `npm ci` dies on `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` for the MCP SDK at the next session start). Every FAIL message names **one distinct operator action**. The check never reads any file's CONTENT, and **extra** files you keep in the deployment (your own modules, scratch files) never produce a finding — the **comparison** runs in one direction only (reference → deployment), and only for a version-EQUAL snapshot (see card#5142, DL-230 below); the deployed directory is never enumerated, which is precisely why extra files there are invisible. The ok verdict says only what was stat'ed — *has its entry file and node_modules — a presence check, not a load test* — never that the entry **loads**: nothing here runs node. **Version leg (WARN):** the deployed `package.json` `version` vs this checkout's; a lower one warns, naming both versions, the re-copy command, **and the permanent cure** — deploying as a *symlink* instead of a copy makes the compare a self-compare, so staleness cannot recur (a copy is still required for cross-host and separate-OS-user topologies, where [`multi-host.md`](multi-host.md) says to leave `server_path` unset). An unusable deployed manifest warns rather than crashing, and names WHICH of its three causes applies (*is not present* / *exists but is not readable by this user* / *does not parse as a JSON object*) — only absence carries the re-copy advice, since a copy neither fixes a permission denial nor needs to replace a whole directory to repair one corrupt file. Classification is on the **resolved realpath**, never a string/prefix test. **⚠ Every leg is `stat`-derived and therefore conclusive only while the bridge process can traverse to the deployed directory:** when the directory itself — or anything above it — denies this process `+x` (ordinary when the bridge runs as a different OS user and the deployment is `0700`), the legs are replaced by one **WARN** naming that directory (*not visible to this user … could NOT be validated*), since `is_dir()`/`is_file()` cannot tell EACCES from ENOENT; in that state a genuinely dangling path is also reported unverified rather than FAIL, which is unavoidable and accepted. The comparator conforms to the declared authority — `_version_tuple` in `bin/provision-board-tools.py` (leading digits per dot-chunk, pre-release/build tags dropped) — **for ASCII-digit versions within PHP's integer range** — a bound stated outright rather than assumed, with divergence confined to two classes that an npm `version` field cannot reach (non-ASCII digits, which python's Unicode-aware `\d` matches and PHP's `[0-9]` does not; and integers above `PHP_INT_MAX`, where PHP saturates and python is arbitrary-precision), each pinned by an assertion. PHP's `version_compare()` is **not** used (it disagrees on 3 of the 7 pinned vectors, which would make `bridge:check` say "stale" while the provisioner says "up to date"), and the 7-vector table is asserted in **both** suites in lockstep. **Deliberately NOT included at DL-229: any comparison of the deployment's file set against this checkout's.** A leg for it was built and cut — a snapshot taken at an older tag is legitimately missing files the checkout has since added, so it reddened stale-but-complete deployments, and for a stale install the verdict is not actionable anyway (the STALE warn's remediation already fixes incompleteness). DL-229 (f) records the full decision path. **card#5142 / DL-230 (below) reinstates it in the one form that has no such population — version-GATED** — after this shape was found to pass GREEN on the incident that motivated the whole feature. Docs: new `channel.server_path` row in `docs/config-schema.md`; `examples/channel-servers/README.md` corrects "a symlink never drifts and needs no check" — a symlink *trades* drift risk for dangling risk rather than removing a failure mode — and the v0.30.0 entry's identical parenthetical carries a bracketed correction pointing at DL-229. **[Superseded — see the Unreleased entry for card#5143 / DL-237: the version-gated completeness leg described here is RETIRED one release later. Measured on a real artifact, the file-set compare is less precise than LAUNCHING the entry in both directions — a pruned-but-working copy missing 6 of 10 reference files FAILs completeness while `node` binds and exits 0, and the DL-230 incident it existed for is caught by the launch anyway. `bridge:check` now discloses that loadability was not measured and names the seat-side `bin/check-channel-snapshot.py`; a version-matched deployment missing a reference file no longer FAILs (exit 1 → exit 0). Read this entry as the capability v0.71.0 shipped.]**
- **card#5142, DL-230** — **The snapshot check no longer passes GREEN on the incident that motivated it: a deployment claiming to be CURRENT must hold every file the current reference ships.** The DL-229 shape was reproduced returning **exit 0** on the real failure — an entry `.mjs` + `package.json` cherry-picked from the *current* reference (so the version stamp came with them), a `node_modules` left over from a prior install, and the sibling module `channel-lib.mjs` never copied: `snapshot is current (deployed 0.8.1 >= bundled 0.8.1)` and `deployment has its entry file and node_modules`, while `node` on that same directory gives `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. Every leg was individually right; the set of them had a hole exactly where the motivating failure lives. **New completeness leg, GATED ON VERSION EQUALITY** — the gate is the whole design: deployed **older** than this checkout ⇒ the STALE warn stands alone and completeness is **skipped entirely** (an older whole-directory copy is legitimately missing files the checkout has since added — the false-red population that got this comparison cut in DL-229, and one the STALE warn's remediation already covers); deployed **equal** ⇒ the comparison **runs**, and any missing reference file is a **FAIL** naming the missing paths (capped) and the re-copy; deployed **newer** ⇒ **skipped, and said so** in its own message, because an older reference cannot say what a newer snapshot should contain. Equal versions mean the same **tracked** file set by a repo-**enforced** invariant, not by assumption: the DL-038 `version-bump-guard` fails any PR that touches a shipped `examples/channel-servers/` file without bumping `package.json` `version`. **The FAIL states what was measured and never why** — the two counts, the missing paths, the `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` consequence, the re-copy — because that invariant governs the tracked set while the reference set is the **working tree**: an untracked stray in the checkout (a `.orig` from a conflicted `git apply --3way`; nothing gitignores those here) joins the reference set with no version bump behind it, so a *faithful* whole-directory copy taken before it landed lands in the same FAIL, and telling that operator they "assembled the deployment file-by-file" would be a diagnostic confidently wrong about what they did (DL-230 (f) states both residual populations and records that each is hypothesized rather than observed). **One direction only** — EXTRA files in your deployment (local modules, scratch files, your own edits) are never a finding, and no file's CONTENT is ever read; the DL-229 (f) import text scan stays permanently rejected. The reference set is not a rule of our choosing: it is exactly what `bin/provision-board-tools.py`'s `shutil.copytree(..., ignore=ignore_patterns("node_modules"))` delivers — **recursive, dotfiles included, `node_modules` excluded at any depth** — pinned by a shared synthetic-tree lockstep asserted in BOTH suites (PHP + python), plus a source-text guard that `_deploy_snapshot` still copies that way. **Visibility discipline extends with it:** the walk proves `+x` on each deployed SUBDIRECTORY before statting inside it, so a `0700` `tests/` reports the same *not visible to this user* WARN naming that subdirectory instead of claiming its files are missing and handing out a destructive `cp -R`; an unreadable reference directory can never collapse to "nothing is missing". A blocked path leaves the **accounting**, not the verdict: a file proven absent through a traversable parent still FAILs (exit **1**) with the visibility WARN emitted alongside and the blocked count folded in, so an unseeable subdirectory cannot turn a real gap back into a green exit. `channel.server_path` **undeclared still exits 0 with the same notice** — no existing install changes behavior unless it declares the key. Channel-server snapshot `0.8.1` → `0.8.2` (README-only change to a shipped file, per the DL-038 bump guard). Docs: the `server_path` row in `docs/config-schema.md`, `examples/channel-servers/README.md`, and `CLAUDE_DEPLOYMENT.md` all corrected (they described a check with no completeness leg); DL-229 (c′)/(e)/(f)/(g) and its per-language-lockstep paragraph annotated additively so the log neither reads as rejecting what later shipped nor keeps stating counts this leg moved (two legs → three, four FAILs → five, one cross-language contract → two). **[Superseded — see the Unreleased entry for card#5143 / DL-237: the version-gated completeness leg described here is RETIRED one release later. Measured on a real artifact, the file-set compare is less precise than LAUNCHING the entry in both directions — a pruned-but-working copy missing 6 of 10 reference files FAILs completeness while `node` binds and exits 0, and the DL-230 incident it existed for is caught by the launch anyway. `bridge:check` now discloses that loadability was not measured and names the seat-side `bin/check-channel-snapshot.py`; a version-matched deployment missing a reference file no longer FAILs (exit 1 → exit 0). Read this entry as the capability v0.71.0 shipped.]**
- **#403, card#5156, DL-232** — **`impl_wake_deny_actors`: an actor deny-list on `impl-ci-wake`.** Every install running `impl-ci-wake` live-woke its impl seats on the weekly bot batch; sola-pm measured one seat against one Dependabot batch at **24 `workflow_run` wakes + 6 `dependabot/*` push events**, 23 success + 1 cancelled, every one resolving to "no action" — impl seats don't act on bot PRs, the PM batch-merges them at a release rollup. The family's complete existing knob set (`benign_conclusions`, `ci_failure_workflow_patterns`, `coord_non_addressed_disposition`, `impl_non_wake_disposition`, `impl_repos`, `provenance_patterns`, `release_branch`, `wake_membership`) was verified to key on **nothing** actor- or branch-related, and the nearest precedent `ci_failure_workflow_patterns` is structurally unable to express it — bot CI runs the same workflow names as human PRs. **Keys on WHO, never on the conclusion:** a bot's run is non-actionable for an impl seat green **or** red, so the knob silences a *failing* run too, pinned by the mirror of the existing `test_workflow_run_failure_wakes` on the same payload and config (a conclusion-keyed filter would have suppressed exactly the wrong half). **A NON-WAKE, not a pre-gate drop** — a denied event skips only the wake-signal computation and falls through to `impl_non_wake_disposition`, so an `inbox_stage` install still records it and only the default `drop` loses it; returning early would make the knob lossy by default and un-opt-out-able. **Actor-keyed, not branch-keyed** — the original `actor OR dependabot/* branch` proposal over-reaches, since an impl agent pushing a fixup onto a bot branch would have its own CI silently suppressed. A **general** deny-list, not a Dependabot special case: entries match the `login` (case-insensitive) or the numeric `id` (exact), ids being immutable where logins rename. Field precedence is `triggering_actor` → `actor` → `sender` for `workflow_run` and `sender` for `push`; the two diverge only on a **re-run**, and that divergence is deliberate — a human re-running a bot's workflow is asking for that result and must still wake. **That precedence is GitHub's documented semantics and is NOT verified against a real divergent payload:** 200 recent `workflow_run` payloads on this install gave `actor !== triggering_actor` in **0 of 200**, with `run_attempt` `{1: 198, 2: 2}` and 0 bot-authored runs — the 2 re-runs present were re-run by the *same* account, so the divergent case was never in the sample. That is a measurement that never contained the condition, not evidence the fields agree, so it is pinned by a **synthetic** payload where the two deliberately differ.
- **#406, card#5150, DL-234** — **an unconditional near-miss WARNING on the PR-title lint.** DL-231 closed a *wrong* correlation and opened a *silent* one: `card#٣` now correlates to nothing, correct and completely unannounced. **The cause is deeper than reported** — it is not that `#` sits outside the near-miss probe's separator set, because the probe carries its **own ASCII `\d`**; `card_٣` is the proof (`_` **is** in that set and `card_3` *does* warn, yet `card_٣` doesn't). Every Unicode-digit token was invisible to the correlation path *and* the warning path, on every separator. The one pre-merge gate that could have told the author was **branch-gated**, skipping unless the head branch matched `^[a-z-]+/[0-9]+-`, so it exited early on this repo's own `card-<id>-…` convention. **The predicate asserts the accepted grammar did NOT match, and never enumerates bad shapes** (`warn iff looks && ! good`) — that inversion is why it needs **no Unicode awareness at all**, a Unicode-aware `grep` being precisely the machinery DL-231 declined and one that would put engine-dependence straight back on the warning path. It also stays correct as the grammar moves, proven within the same session: DL-233 made the separator optional hours earlier, creating a brand-new near-miss (single-digit glued `card4`), which the predicate catches with **no edit** because it re-derives "bad" from "not good". **Warning, not error** — the ASCII near-misses are legal-but-lossy today, and blocking them is a separate decision with a different blast radius. **The lint's bash is now executed by a test — it never was:** its grammar has been edited three times (DL-201, DL-233, this one) with each round's matrix driven ad-hoc during review and then thrown away, so every later edit re-derived correctness by reading; `PrTitleLintTest` extracts the step's `run:` block from the YAML and drives it under real `bash`, holding it in lockstep with `CARD_TOKEN_PATTERN`.
- **#407, card#5153, DL-235** — **`bare_reply_to_own_thread`: a narrow, echo-guarded `reply-dir B` grant, default-ON.** A **bare** reply (no `TO:` line) to a thread an agent opened was dropped under the shipped default `[to_me, to_all, comment_to]`: `to:<me>` absent, `to:all` absent, `from_me` not in the default, and `comment_to` with no `TO:` line to key on. Three operators measured it independently without comparing configs — sola 3 of 4 seats non-conformant, aimla 3 impl seats + 5 auxiliary, this seat conformant only because its yaml sets `from_me` explicitly — and `coord_non_addressed_disposition` was **unset on every seat of both other installs**, so the drops were *silent*, not deferred; aimla attests **112** dropped coordination comments per impl agent. **Not `from_me`**, the knob that already existed and looks like the answer: it grants on **all** activity on an authored thread, strictly wider than `reply-dir B` needs, so operators faced *violate the row* or *accept wake-on-everything* and six-plus seats chose the former — nobody chose the bug, they declined the volume. Flipping `from_me` into the default would also have reverted **DL-190**, a deliberate 3-way-agreed wide→narrow flip this agent had signed off on. **The echo guard is what makes an authorship-keyed grant safe at all:** DL-213 rejected `from_me` on a mechanism, not a precedent — `comment_to` keys on the comment's directed `TO:` line, *not authorship*, so under the shared account a comment the agent itself authored does not wake it. A narrow bare-reply grant still keys on thread authorship and inherits that hazard in miniature, so it grants **iff** the labels contain `from:{me}` **AND** the body carries no `TO:` line (`=== null`, never `false` — an explicit `TO: someone-else` still narrows) **AND** the body's `FROM:` line is not the agent, via the already-shipped `RecipientAddressing::author()`. Each conjunct is load-bearing; drop any one and it becomes the knob the fleet already rejected. Converged 3/3 on roundtable #160.

### Changed
- **#405, card#5137, DL-233** — **the classifier accepts the glued `card<id>` token, converging with the toolkit.** `board-card-start` has accepted the glued `card4524` branch spelling since **v0.17.0**, deliberately, as *"the natural, most-typed glued spelling"*; this classifier required a separator and rejected it — producing the worst available failure mode, where the card **moves locally** when the toolkit hook fires on checkout and the PR writeback **never correlates**, so the board looks right until the merge that should have advanced it does nothing. sola-pm measured **14 of 80 PRs** silently no-op'd; the FR-7 near-miss probe did warn on the shape, but into the bridge log, which the PR author never reads. The pattern becomes `/\bcard(?|[-#](\d+)|(\d{2,}))/i`. **The ≥2-digit floor on the glued arm is the load-bearing part, and it is adopted rather than invented** — it is exactly `board-card-start`'s `0*[0-9]{2,}`; without it an optional separator imports a false-correlation class, because DL-201 deliberately carries **no trailing `\b`**. Branch-reset `(?|…)` gives both arms capture group 1, so all three resolution sites read `$m[1]` unchanged, and **DL-231's ASCII-only digit class is untouched** — this widens the *separator* only and the pattern still carries no `/u`. Two things moved with it: **`pr-title-lint.yml` was widened in the same change** (it asserted `card[-#]${card_id}` and would have redded a title carrying the now-correlating glued token, inverting the rule the lint exists to serve), and an existing near-miss assertion **changed subject rather than being weakened** — `test_near_miss_card_token_in_branch_warns_and_noops` pinned `feat/card3054` as warn-and-no-op, precisely the behavior this DL reverses, so that shape moved into `test_glued_card_token_correlates` and `card.3054` took its place, keeping the near-miss list asserted non-empty. Operator-approved before implementation (a permissive change to what the classifier accepts).

### Fixed
- **#409, card#5170, DL-236** — **`bridge:check` reports an undeclared `channel.server_path` as `unvalidated`, not `ok`.** `ChannelSnapshotProbe::probe()` returned `ok` carrying the message *"channel.server_path not declared — snapshot not validated"* — the severity that means measured-and-clean, on a check that never ran. `bridge:check` rendered it green and exited 0, so **"green because checked" and "green because nobody looked" were the same output.** This is card#5108's own shape one level up: #5108 existed because `bridge:check` certified the channel *socket* while never examining the deployed *snapshot*; DL-229/DL-230 fixed the snapshot legs, and this was the same shape in the leg deciding whether to run them at all. **Population: 0 of 9 per-agent configs on this seat declare the key — and 0 of 9 on a second, independent install** — opt-in with no writer, so the default population is every install that has not deliberately opted in. **A distinct severity rather than a `warn`, because `docs/multi-host.md` *instructs* operators to leave the key unset cross-host** (the probe `stat`s the path from the bridge process, so a host-B path declared on host A reports a dangling FAIL for a healthy deployment); the undeclared population therefore includes a documented-correct configuration this repo's own docs mandate, and a `warn` would nag every multi-host install for following the instructions, permanently, with no action available to silence it — the cry-wolf shape rejected twice already (card#5148, card#5150 v1) and aggravated here by the docs having created the population. The severity names what was **measured**, not what someone decided about it. Rendered **plain** — not green (reads as certified by a check that never ran), not yellow (the nag). **`emitFinding()` returns false ONLY for `fail`**, so exit codes are preserved *by construction*, asserted as a property over the whole vocabulary plus an unknown future severity; a per-run tally is counted at the `emitFinding()` chokepoint and reset at the top of `handle()` (the container can hand the same instance to a second `Artisan::call`, and a leaked count over-reports), silent at zero. **Two fixes beyond the card's stated scope:** `checkSshTransport()` carried `if ($finding['severity'] !== 'ok')` — a **negative membership proxy** for "warn or fail" that a fourth value silently extends to sweep `unvalidated`, flagging an agent's ssh setup *incomplete* over a check nobody ran (now a positive predicate; latent, not live, since `SshTransportProbe` is three-valued); and **a sibling of this card's own defect in the same command**, the DL-196 fail-soft catch rendering a check that did not run as green via `$this->info('event-consumer: check skipped — …')`, exiting 0 and uncounted, now routed through the same `unvalidated` vocabulary with fail-soft preserved. That sibling also exposed an **over-claim in the tally's own first draft**: `emitFinding()` is the chokepoint for **probe findings** (3 call sites), not for the command's output — 26 other direct `info()` calls, ~75 `warn()`, and 27 `error()` bypass it — so the summary now states its real mechanism and bounds itself as *a floor, not an inventory*. **Known gap, stated rather than smoothed:** `ChannelSnapshotProbe:236` had **zero** test coverage, which is how it drifted from its matched pair at `:315`, one spelling its remediation and the other not. Every new test was proven failable and **by which method is recorded per test** — four red-when-reverted, two failable only by **mutation** (the IFF-fail property test is green against pre-change code and the silent-at-zero test survives every revert), labelled as such rather than filed under a blanket header. The `{severity, message}` shape now has **three** producers feeding one renderer, where an unknown severity falls through the else-branch and renders green — this card's shape one level further out, filed as **card#5178** rather than fixed here.
- **#404, card#5156** — **DL-232's "digest, not drop" was false on a `route_intents` channel — corrected at the doc surface, and its siblings with it.** `DispatchService.php` appends a `channel_push` target for **every** staged intent when `channel.route_intents` is true, unconditionally, with no re-check of whether the classifier intended a wake. So `impl_wake_deny_actors` fully suppresses under the default `drop` and under `inbox_stage` + `route_intents: false`, but under **`inbox_stage` + `route_intents: true` it still wakes — no suppression at all** — and that third row is **the requester's exact configuration**: all three of sola's impl seats run it, so adopting the knob as documented would have delivered **zero** wake reduction on the very install that asked for it. The runtime is unchanged and the knob is not defective — an operator setting `route_intents: true` has said "push me every staged intent", and the classifier docblock already called that shape *"a broad-wake install"*; what was wrong was **DL-232's own wording**, which read as an unconditional quiet digest. DL-232 (h) records the correction with the full matrix and the explicit instruction that such installs must use `impl_non_wake_disposition: drop` to benefit; the `docs/config-schema.md` row carries the caveat; and a deliberate **counter-example test** asserts the push *does* reach the channel, so the universal reading cannot be re-derived by the next reader — it documents a limit rather than a behavior worth wanting. **Sibling audit (canon #7) found the same promise twice more, and the second one is what actually bit:** `impl_non_wake_disposition` and `coord_non_addressed_disposition` both described themselves as staging "with **no** `channel_push`" with no `route_intents` caveat anywhere — pre-existing, and the **coord** one nearly took sola's three impl seats live on *all* unaddressed coordination traffic before they caught it by exercising rather than reasoning. Both rows now state that on a routing channel **staging is waking**, and that "no `channel_push`" describes the classifier's own emission rather than the delivered outcome. That is the root cause: three doc surfaces each promised a quiet digest, and the one mechanism overriding all three was documented only in `DispatchService`.
- **`wake_membership`'s stated default was self-contradicting in two places** (found while cutting this release). DL-235 added `bare_reply_to_own_thread` to the shipped default and updated the `docs/config-schema.md` Default column, but left that row's own mid-prose **`DEFAULT [to_me, to_all, comment_to]`** at the pre-DL-235 three values, and left `CoordinationClassifier.php`'s inline comment stating the same three **13 lines above the four-value default it documents**. Both now name all four. The historical DL-213 entries in this changelog and in `CLAUDE_DECISIONS.md` correctly describe that decision's own flip and are unchanged.

### Docs
- **#402, card#5147, DL-231** — **the card-token digit class is ratified as ASCII-only across the fleet, and guarded against the one-character edit that would re-widen it.** The DL-201 token is implemented in at least three regex engines across the fleet, and python's `\d` matches **Unicode decimal digits** while `int()` folds them — so `card#٣` (U+0663) correlates to card **3** through python and is **invisible** to a bash `[0-9]` path: one token, two engines, two answers, no error on either side. Both of this fleet's engines were verified already conformant (bridge `CARD_TOKEN_PATTERN` and `DL_TOKEN_PATTERN` carry no `/u`; the toolkit's `board-card-start` uses `[0-9]{2,}`; the kanban repo has no card-token engine), so the divergence was one-sided, and sola-pm deliberately did **not** narrow their copy unilaterally — narrowing a ratified cross-repo grammar without ratification is the divergence ratification exists to prevent. **The decision is ASCII `[0-9]` in every engine:** a card id is a numeric primary key and the token's job is to name exactly one card unambiguously, so an engine-dependent answer is a correlation-integrity defect, with the added hazard that it correlates to a card *the author never named in ASCII*. Widening instead would require every engine to agree on the numeric **conversion**, not just the match — python's `int()` folds Unicode digits and bash has no equivalent without new machinery — where narrowing costs one character class in one python constant. **No production code changed; the guard is the substance.** A ratified grammar with no executable check is one edit from silent re-divergence, and that edit is **one character** — adding `/u`, plausible as a well-meant "handle unicode input" change, which would pass every existing test. `test_card_and_dl_tokens_accept_ascii_digits_only` asserts `card#٣`, `card-٣`, and `DL-٣` all correlate to nothing, with an ASCII positive control (`card#3` → card 3) so the negative assertions are attributable to the digit class and not the fixture. **Verified by revert-and-restore:** adding `/u` to both constants reds it, so the widening this DL forbids is empirically reachable in *this* engine, not merely a python quirk.
- **#408** — the managed `coord:solo-orientation` block re-synced to coord plugin **v0.24.0** (from v0.23.0) via `orientation-sync.py --apply --role solo --integration dev`. Block-only change; this repo's own addendum outside the markers is untouched. The content delta adds `MIRROR-BEGIN/END` markers around the context-budget chat-gate prose, which are load-bearing: the framework's `chat-gate-mirror-drift.selftest.py` extracts exactly what they enclose and reds if any of the five lockstep copies diverges.


## [0.70.0] - 2026-07-24

**Minor — the board-tools SSH transport hardens and gains self-management.** 4 substantive PRs since v0.69.1 (#393, #395, #396, #397) + a solo-orientation sync (#394). The operator-lockout sshd drop-in is retired (the forced-command key is the sole boundary), a key-scoped forced-command timeout bounds a hung session, a `clear_context` self-management tool lands on the reference channel server, and the same-box provisioning wrapper ships. **All changes are operator-side provisioning tooling (`bin/provision-board-tools.py` + wrapper) or the reference channel server (`examples/channel-servers/`); the webhook receiver + dispatch runtime is untouched — no migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change.**

### Security
- **#395 (card#5091)** — **retire the account-level sshd hardening `--role a` used to write; the forced-command `authorized_keys` key is the sole board-tools boundary.** The old `Match User <acct>` drop-in (`PasswordAuthentication no` + `ClientAlive*`/`MaxSessions`) locked out an operator whose interactive login shares the ssh-account (the deployment reality). Removed the drop-in write + the now-orphaned `sshd -t`/reload, and dropped the `bridge:check` sshd-posture probe leg that asserted the retired contract — its certify path (`--probe-tools-ssh`) would otherwise be a guaranteed red demanding re-creation of the lockout. `bridge:check` now certifies via the pinned forced-command key + the live round-trip only. Docs reconciled (`multi-host.md` §3 owns the model). Full PHP suite 1334/1334, Python 122/122; red-when-reverted guards.
- **#397 (card#5092)** — **a key-scoped `timeout -k 10 <n>` (default 300s) wraps the forced command** so a hung/stalling key-holder is reaped (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL 10s later) at the command level — the bound that survives on a shared login account (an account-level `ClientAlive` would disrupt the operator, which is why #395 retired it). New pure `build_forced_command` single-source + `--forced-command-timeout` knob; existing pinned keys keep their command until re-provisioned (documented). red-when-reverted tests.

### Added
- **#396 (card#5089, roundtable #146)** — **a `clear_context` local-exec MCP tool on the reference channel server.** A model can clear its own screen/tmux context to save tokens: advertised iff `$STY` is set AND `clear-agent.sh` resolves on PATH (orthogonal to the board-tools `TOOLS_ENABLED` gate); the handler spawns `clear-agent.sh` detached (`{detached:true, stdio:'ignore'}` + `unref()`) and returns immediately — never proxied over the bridge ssh/http path; `tools/call` branches it before the proxy with a call-time arming re-check (a not-armed call returns a structured `isError`, no spawn). Description carries the guardrails (final action of turn, after commit+post, never mid-task/uncommitted/queued-human-message). New `examples/channel-servers/tests/clear-context.test.mjs` (5 cases incl. absent-without-`$STY` red-when-reverted + call-spawns-detached with zero bridge hits); channel-server suite 27/27; `package.json` 0.7.3→0.8.0; DL-228.
- **#393 (card#5090, DL-227, FR #5010 follow-on)** — **a same-box one-shot wrapper (`bin/provision-board-tools-samebox.py`) collapses the 6-step cross-user SSH board-tools enablement into one root-run command.** On a box where the agent's Claude seat and the bridge share the machine (each its own OS user), `sudo bin/provision-board-tools-samebox.py --agent <name> --ssh-account <host-A user>` orchestrates, on `127.0.0.1`: a fail-closed preflight (root, both OS users exist via `getent passwd`, the agent's `.mcp.json` resolves unambiguously → `--project-dir`/`--channel-name`, both checkouts' `provision-board-tools.py` + host-A `artisan` present, `php` on PATH) → `--role b` as the agent user from the **agent's own checkout** (captures + validates the printed pubkey path; no `--self-cert`) → `--role a` as root from the host-A checkout (`--pubkey-from`) → prints the one manual step (restart the agent's Claude session) → `php <artisan> bridge:check` → `chown -R <ssh-account> <host-A storage>`. **No global binary staging (version isolation):** each leg runs *its own checkout's* provisioner — the wrapper never copies the tool into a shared path like `/usr/local/bin` (two agents on one host may run different bridge versions; a shared path lets one clobber the other), and fails with an actionable "give the agent its own checkout" message if the agent's copy is absent or unreadable by the agent user. `--dry-run` prints the exact per-leg argv without changes; idempotent (the underlying legs are, and the wrapper adds no non-idempotent state). Python (matches the repo `bin/` convention, reuses `provision-board-tools.py`'s `_AGENT_RE`/`_SSH_ACCOUNT_RE` validators). **Operator-side provisioning tooling only** — no `app/` PHP, no migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change. 25 red-when-reverted unit tests (`bin/test_provision_board_tools_samebox.py`), CI-wired via `provision-tools-python.yml`.

## [0.69.1] - 2026-07-24

**Patch — the Windows `--role b` deploy leg's `npm ci` step is now actually launchable (`npm.cmd` is unreachable via bare-name `CreateProcess`) and a not-found npm is no longer mislabeled as connectivity, plus an internal channel-server helper extraction with direct unit tests.** 2 PRs since v0.69.0 (#389, #390). **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change.** Both changes are **operator-side provisioning tooling** (`bin/provision-board-tools.py`, run on the agent's own host-B machine) or the **reference channel server** (`examples/channel-servers/`); the webhook receiver + dispatch runtime is untouched. Success/failure semantics are unchanged — this corrects Windows launchability + diagnostic text only.

### Changed
- **#389 (card#5072)** — **the reference channel server's genuinely-pure helpers are extracted into a sibling `channel-lib.mjs` with direct `node --test` unit coverage.** `agent-webhook-bridge-channel.mjs` self-executes on import (top-level `process.exit`/socket bind) and exported nothing, so `scrubSnippet`, `relayBridgeResponse` (the relay/`isError` contract) and `deriveMeta` (envelope→meta parse) had no direct unit coverage. They move byte-identical into `channel-lib.mjs` — which has **no import-time side effects** (decls/exports only, no top-level await/IO/exit/env, no import cycle) — and are imported back, so startup ordering / marker races / self-execute semantics are untouched. The I/O- or startup-state-coupled helpers (`resolveToolsToken`, `shouldAdvertiseTools`, `markerPath`) are left in place (conservative partial extraction over a risky full one). New `tests/channel-lib.test.mjs` adds 18 direct units (full suite 22/22, 0 skipped; red-when-reverted proven); channel-server snapshot `0.7.2 → 0.7.3` (DL-038). Internal refactor — no runtime behavior change.

### Fixed
- **#390 (card#5074, roundtable #145)** — **the Windows `--role b` deploy leg's `npm ci` step is now actually launchable, and a not-found npm is no longer mislabeled as connectivity.** On Windows `npm` is `npm.cmd`, a batch script `subprocess.run(..., shell=False)` (via `CreateProcess`) cannot launch by bare name (PATHEXT is a shell concept, not a `CreateProcess` one) nor by full path (`CreateProcess` execs PE binaries only) — so `_npm_ci()` failed on every Windows host-B, and the single `except (OSError, CalledProcessError)` then reported it as "Fix connectivity/proxy" (a mislabel: the deploy never reached the network). `npm ci` now routes through `cmd.exe` (a real `.exe`, which resolves `npm.cmd` via its own PATHEXT) on Windows via a pure `_npm_argv(args, os_name=os.name)` helper (POSIX runs `npm` directly, unchanged); `shell=True` stays forbidden. The `except` is split so a not-found npm (`FileNotFoundError`) is reported as an invocation problem naming the Node-20-on-PATH fix, and only a genuine non-zero `npm ci` (or other OS error) keeps the lockfile/network guidance. Linux-side unit-tested (both `_npm_argv` branches + both error paths, red-when-reverted); real Windows validation routes to sola's device agent (roundtable #145). No `shell=True`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change.

**Verification:** no `app/` PHP touched (phpunit unchanged at 1341/1341); provisioner python 95/95 + reference channel-server `node --test` 22/22. Linux-side unit coverage only — real-surface Windows validation of the `--role b` deploy leg routes to sola's device agent (roundtable #145; there is no Windows CI runner in-repo).

## [0.69.0] - 2026-07-24

**Minor — `bridge:provision-tools` reaches cross-platform host-B feature-completeness (Linux legs + a validated Windows host-B leg), plus a channel-server fail-loud refuse primitive and its first CI test harness.** 7 PRs since v0.68.1 (#379, #380, #381, #382, #383, #384, #385). **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change.** Everything here is **operator-side provisioning tooling** (`bin/provision-board-tools.py`, run on the agent's own host-B machine) or the **reference channel server** (`examples/channel-servers/`); the webhook receiver + dispatch runtime is untouched. The Windows legs were validated on a real Windows 11 seat by sola's device agent (roundtable #145); live cross-device end-to-end certification of the whole enablement remains gated on that seat (FR #5010 stays open until certified).

### Added
- **#379 (card#5010, roundtable #141)** — **`provision-board-tools` Linux legs.** One python program (`bin/provision-board-tools.py`, `--role a|b`, single-sourced so the key-shape validator + `.mcp.json` merge contract cannot drift). `--role a` (host-A, Linux root): forced-command `authorized_keys` line + sshd `Match User` drop-in, idempotent (append-or-verify), `sshd -t`-before-reload, euid-0 fail-closed. Same-box collapse: `--role a` reads the `--role b` pubkey by path (no paste). The Windows host-B path shipped stubbed (three `NotImplementedError`s), held for sola validation.
- **#381 (card#5010)** — **the Windows host-B leg, un-gated.** The icacls/SID/keygen implementation validated on a real en-US Windows 11 seat (Win32-OpenSSH 9.5p2, Python 3.13) by sola's device agent (roundtable #145, Round 2): `_host_b_home()` resolves `%USERPROFILE%`; `_harden_private_key_perms_windows()` uses `icacls /inheritance:r` + `/grant:r *<owner-SID>:R`; `_seed_known_hosts()` seeds under the Windows profile; FIPS ECDSA keygen. The ACL check is **fail-closed** (a principal it cannot resolve to a trusted well-known SID is treated as untrusted → REFUSE, never an unsafe accept).
- **#384 (card#5056)** — **the reference channel server's first `node --test` CI harness.** The `examples/channel-servers/` supply-chain workflow ran only `npm ci` + `npm audit`; it now also runs the `marker-refusal` regression suite (`node --test tests/`), so a refuse-and-exit path that stops writing its `.FAILED` marker fails the PR. The http-throw test's privileged-port strategy was hardened from a uid-based skip (which false-fails + hangs where a non-root process can bind a low port) to an empirical bind probe.

### Changed
- **#380 (card#5010)** — **the fresh-Windows channel-listener transport default is now `http`** (was `unix` unconditionally), via a pure `channel_transport_default()` helper (`http` on `os.name == 'nt'`, `unix` on POSIX — unchanged). Empirically: `unix` is unusable on Windows (Node on Win32 rejects filesystem socket paths with `EACCES` and the channel server `process.exit(2)`-refuses a socketless `unix` transport); `http` (a `127.0.0.1` loopback listener, the in-tunnel last hop) is the only working value.
- **#382 (card#5050, #5051; roundtable #145)** — **channel-server refuse-and-exit paths are now fail-loud.** Several refuse sites died with **no `.FAILED` marker**, so a misconfigured seat — especially on Windows, where #5010 made `http` the listener default — came up deaf to live-wake with zero signal (Claude Code swallows MCP-server startup stderr). A single `refuseDeaf(reason, {advice})` primitive (canon #5/#7) now writes the marker → logs → `exit(2)` on every Windows-reachable refusal, with platform-aware advice.

### Fixed
- **#385 (card#5053)** — **locale-independent Windows ACL principal→SID resolution.** The host-B `authorized_keys` ACL check matched principals via a hardcoded **en-US name→SID table**, so a localized Windows (de-DE `VORDEFINIERT\Administratoren`, `NT-AUTORITÄT\SYSTEM`) missed the table, kept the name raw, and **spuriously REFUSED** a legitimate ACL. New `_lookup_account_sid` (a `LookupAccountName`-equivalent via PowerShell `NTAccount.Translate`, no-op off Windows, principal passed via env var so injection-safe) resolves localized well-known names to their invariant SIDs; threaded into the shared `_make_name_to_sid` primitive so both the key-file and `.ssh`-dir decisions become locale-independent. **Fail-closed preserved** (unresolvable principal stays raw → untrusted → refuse); a localized broad principal (Users/Everyone) resolves to its true broad SID and is still refused. Adversarial security review to zero must-fix.
- **#383 (card#5066; roundtable #145)** — **`provision-board-tools --self-cert` certifies the round-trip result, not just JSON-parseability.** The self-cert parsed ssh stdout as JSON and printed OK without inspecting `proc.returncode` or the envelope's `ok`/`error`, so an error envelope (`{"ok":false,"error":"unknown agent"}`) at exit 2 **false-passed** — a check that could not fail. It now fails unless the exit is 0 **and** the body is a non-error dict envelope. `--self-cert` stays the host-B-side smoke test under `bridge:check --probe-tools-ssh` (the lane-asserting authoritative cert).
- **card#5033** — **the `authorized_keys` pubkey is validated as a single well-formed line** (`is_authorized_key_shape`: rejects any CR/LF, positive key-type allowlist, pure-base64 blob), landed within the #379–381 rewrite. This structurally closes the pre-rewrite v0.68.1 scaffold's prefix-only guard, where a multi-line paste starting `ecdsa-` could inject a second, unrestricted `authorized_keys` line with no forced command.

**Verification:** 1341/1341 phpunit, phpstan L7 0, pint clean; provisioner python 91/91 + reference channel-server `node --test` 5/5. Windows-specific behavior (icacls/SID/keygen, `%USERPROFILE%` paths) was validated on a real Windows 11 seat by sola's device agent (roundtable #145) — there is no Windows CI runner in-repo; full cross-device end-to-end certification of the enablement remains gated on that seat (FR #5010).

> **Precision correction (see [0.69.1] / bridge #5074):** the "cross-platform host-B feature-completeness" headline was premature for the `--role b` **deploy** path specifically. That leg's `npm ci` step was not Windows-validated at v0.69.0 — it was unresolvable on Windows (`npm.cmd` unreachable via bare-name `CreateProcess`) and, when it failed, mislabeled as a connectivity error. Fixed in [0.69.1] above.

## [0.68.1] - 2026-07-23

**Patch — `bridge:provision-tools`'s generated ssh setup-script pubkey guard now survives a naive `sed` injection (DL-226 follow-on).** 1 PR since v0.68.0 (#375). **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change; the change is confined to the ssh-path generated-script template.**

### Fixed
- **#375 (card#5009, roundtable #141)** — **the generated root-run ssh setup script's `HOST_B_PUBKEY` guard now survives a naive `sed` injection.** The DL-226 script carried the paste placeholder (`PASTE HOST-B PUBLIC KEY HERE`) on **both** the value-assignment line and an equality guard; a first-match-per-line `sed 's|PASTE...|<key>|'` rewrote both, so the guard compared the injected key against itself and always passed — the script died with `ERROR: set HOST_B_PUBKEY` even after a correct paste (cost aimla's operator a cycle during the first live same-box ssh enablement). The placeholder now appears exactly once (value line only); the guard is replaced with a positive key-shape test (`case … in ecdsa-*|ssh-*|sk-*`) that greens only once a real key is in place, regardless of how the value line was edited — covers FIPS ECDSA P-256, ed25519/rsa, and `sk-*` security keys. Considered-and-rejected: reading the pubkey from a keyfile at generation time (`provision-tools` is topology-agnostic — host B may be a different machine, so the path isn't config-derivable without baking in a same-box assumption; tracked separately as the one-command end-to-end enablement, FR #5010). Regression test extracts the real generated guard string and executes it under bash, red-when-reverted (exit 1 on the untouched placeholder / exit 0 after a naive sed injection). 1342/1342 phpunit, phpstan L7 0, pint clean.

## [0.68.0] - 2026-07-23

**Minor — SSH becomes the DEFAULT board-tools transport (DL-225) + a guided, idempotent ssh-setup script in `bridge:provision-tools` (DL-226).** The two-front-door work (DL-223/DL-224) shipped ssh as an opt-in; the fleet direction (roundtable #133/#138) is ssh-first, so an unset `board_tools.transport` now reads as `ssh` instead of `http`. Plus `bridge:provision-tools` graduates from print-only scaffolding to GENERATING a root-run setup script whose output PASSES the `bridge:check` sshd backstop.

**UPGRADING (DL-225) — read before upgrading:** the pre-0.68.0 default for `board_tools.transport` was **`http`**; it is now **`ssh`**. **Any `board_tools` block relying on the implicit `http` default must set `transport: http` explicitly BEFORE upgrading to keep the loopback path** — post-upgrade an unset `transport:` is read as `ssh`, so the bearer stops resolving over the HTTP loopback door and `POST /agent-tools/call` fails closed (401). The runtime break is loud/fail-closed (no silent permissive change), and `bridge:check` emits a **pre-upgrade advisory** for any agent that landed on ssh via the flipped default with no completed ssh setup (naming the `transport: http` pin as the fix). Explicit `transport: http` or `transport: ssh` blocks are unaffected. The caller-facing 401 is deliberately indistinguishable from an unknown-token 401 (naming a re-class would leak that a presented bearer is valid).

### Changed
- **DL-225 — SSH is the default board-tools transport.** `BoardToolsConfig` now defaults `transport` to `ssh` at both the parse site (absent `transport:` key) and the constructor default; a new readonly `transportExplicit` flag records whether the key was present. `bridge:check`'s ssh leg gains a pre-upgrade **advisory** (warn, never a hard fail): an agent on ssh by the flipped default (`transportExplicit === false`) whose ssh setup is incomplete (the pinned-line / sshd-posture probe already run in that leg reports any non-ok finding) is flagged to pin `transport: http` or complete ssh setup. The caller-facing 401 in `AgentToolsController` is unchanged (security property: it must not distinguish unknown-token from re-classed-to-ssh). Runtime fail-closed path is unchanged — an implicit-http block now reads as ssh, so `BoardToolAgentResolver`'s `transport !== 'http'` exclusion already drops it from the HTTP index.
- **DL-226 — guided ssh setup script in `bridge:provision-tools`.** The ssh path graduates from print-only scaffolding (which omitted the three DL-224 backstop directives, so following it then running `sudo bridge:check` FAILED the backstop leg) to GENERATING a root-run setup script (still operator-run — "guided" = generate + verify, not silent automation; root surfaces stay operator-run). The script wires idempotently (append-or-verify, never clobbers existing config): (a) the forced-command `authorized_keys` line (enumerated `no-pty,no-agent-forwarding,no-X11-forwarding,no-port-forwarding` FIPS-safe flags, artisan + agent substituted, a clear PASTE placeholder for host B's public key), (b) an sshd `Match User <account>` drop-in with `PasswordAuthentication no` AND `ClientAliveInterval 300` / `ClientAliveCountMax 2` / `MaxSessions 10` (all `>0` — matching what `probeSshdPosture` hard-asserts, so the generated config PASSES `bridge:check`), (c) a validate-then-reload (`sshd -t` before reload), and it documents (d) the FIPS ECDSA P-256 keygen to run first on host B (`ssh-keygen -t ecdsa -b 256`, never ed25519). The operator runs `sudo bash <script>`, then certifies with `bridge:check --probe-tools-ssh=<user@host>` (a real `board_my_cards` round-trip). Docs: `docs/config-schema.md` transport row + `docs/board-tools.md` updated for the flipped default and the guided script.

## [0.67.0] - 2026-07-23

**Minor — the board-tools SSH-forced-command transport (DL-223): a no-root, firewall-uniform alternative to the loopback HTTP endpoint, plus the DL-224 follow-on that certifies the forced-command account and verifies the sshd idle/concurrency backstop, and a release-CI promote-guard pin.** 3 PRs since v0.66.0 (#368, #369, #367). **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change. The SSH transport is opt-in (`board_tools.transport: ssh`, default `http`) and fail-closed end to end ⇒ absent it, behavior is byte-identical — see UPGRADING.**

### Added
- **#368 (DL-223, card#4952, roundtable #133)** — **board-tools SSH-forced-command transport: a no-root, firewall-uniform alternative to the loopback HTTP endpoint.** New per-agent **`board_tools.transport: http|ssh`** key (default `http` — absent-config byte-identical); `ssh` lifts the bearer requirement — identity comes from the pinned key's forced command, not a token (a contradictory `auth` block under `ssh` fails closed). **`bridge:tools-call --agent=X`** is the SSH front door: identity is un-spoofable (the pinned `command=` line; `SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND` is never read), stdout is strictly the result body (all diagnostics to stderr/log), exit codes `0` ok · `1` caller-fixable input · `2` bridge-side misconfiguration. A shared **`BoardToolDispatcher` + `DispatchOutcome`** core is extracted from the HTTP controller so both front doors serialize one body shape (HTTP maps `status`, ssh maps `exitCode()`). **`bridge:check`** gains an offline `authorized_keys` capability-model verification (`restrict`-based, last-writer-wins option parsing, quoted-field tokenizer, port-forwarding fail-closed) plus a `--probe-tools-ssh` live round-trip (non-root ⇒ UNVERIFIED warn, never a false OK); `bridge:provision-tools` scaffolds ssh agents by print (incl. FIPS keygen guidance — ed25519 rejected on a FIPS host ⇒ ecdsa). A `.mjs` channel-server ssh-spawn leg shares one relay helper with the HTTP leg (`ConnectTimeout=10` + a 60s overall deadline). Design ran 4 fresh-adversarial rounds to zero must-fix. **One shipped-surface error-reporting change (hard-gate class, standing dev authorization):** via the shared relay helper the existing HTTP leg now returns `isError: true` for a corrupt/unparseable bridge response body (was `isError: false` with raw text). Docs: `docs/multi-host.md` SSH section, `docs/board-tools.md` two-front-door model, `docs/config-schema.md` transport row, `CLAUDE_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `CLAUDE_DECISIONS.md` DL-223. Live-fire debt (`.mjs` leg, `--probe-tools-ssh`, `sshd -T` root path, FIPS `restrict`, stdin `timed_out` on a real ssh pipe) is recorded in DL-223 and lands with aimla's + sola's enablement.
- **#369 (DL-224, card#4977, roundtable #133)** — **`bridge:check` now asserts the sshd idle/concurrency backstop** DL-223 only recommended in an operator note. With the forced command as the sole entry point, sshd's `ClientAliveInterval`/`ClientAliveCountMax`/`MaxSessions` are the only real bound on a key-holder that holds stdin open (the in-process stdin deadline is best-effort + unverified on the pipe). The posture leg reads the SAME Match-resolved `sshd -T -C user=<forced-command-account>` output and **fails** if `ClientAliveInterval` is non-positive/unset (no idle timeout), if `ClientAliveCountMax` is non-positive/unset (`0` disables the client-alive disconnect; the `interval × countmax` idle window is otherwise unbounded), or if `MaxSessions` is non-positive/unset — naming the exact directive + a `Match User <account>` remedy; only an all-positive config reports the computed idle bound. Docs: `docs/multi-host.md § 3` gains the `ssh_account` sudo callout + the now-required backstop directives (the old "unverified" operator note corrected), `docs/config-schema.md` + `docs/board-tools.md` updated.

### Changed
- **#367** — **PupFuzz/agent-board-toolkit/promote 0.20.0 → 0.21.0** (release CI, `release-promote-cards.yml`; SHA-pinned `ddb7330158f6b98b0b62c3e32ab38bbc53f2b405`), opting in to the new optional `shipped-stage-ids` input with board 8's Shipped-to-Dev stage id (`52`). At release-promote time a DL/PR-matched card whose CURRENT stage is not Shipped-to-Dev is now skipped (with a log line) instead of moved — a stale or recycled DL/PR stamp can no longer resurrect a declined/backlog card. Blank input = prior behavior, so the guard is strictly opt-in; the pin bump alone is behavior-identical. **Runs only on the merge-to-`main` release event ⇒ takes effect only on `main`; this release activates it.**

### Fixed
- **#369 (DL-224, card#4977)** — **the SSH-transport `bridge:check` probe now certifies the FORCED-COMMAND account, not the invoking one.** DL-223's probe resolved the account running `bridge:check` (`posix_geteuid`) at two sites where it means the forced-command account, so under the documented `sudo bridge:check` (euid=0) it certified **root's** `sshd -T -C user=root` posture and read **`/root/.ssh/authorized_keys`** — false-negativing every seat whose forced command runs as a non-root account (the common case). New optional **`board_tools.ssh_account`** (string; SSH transport only, absent ⇒ the invoking run-user, byte-identical to pre-4977) names the account; the probe then resolves `sshd -T -C user=<account>`, expands `%h`/`%u` and the default `authorized_keys` path against that account's home (`posix_getpwnam`), and the posture leg is deduped per resolved account. Parsed into `BoardToolsConfig::$sshAccount` (new `optionalString` helper; present-but-empty/non-string fails closed at load, inheriting the DL-222 throw/suppress classification).
- **#369 (DL-224 follow-on, card#4977)** — two security-correctness gaps in the DL-224 backstop closed: (1) the idle/concurrency backstop guarded `ClientAliveCountMax`/`MaxSessions` on `=== null` only, but `sshd -T` always emits both (defaults 3/10) so the `null` branch was dead — a `ClientAliveCountMax 0` (which DISABLES sshd's client-alive idle disconnect) computed a `0s` idle bound and was certified **ok**; both are now guarded `=== null || <= 0` like the interval sibling, distinguishing unset from non-positive. (2) A **configured** `board_tools.ssh_account` that does not resolve to an OS account (`posix_getpwnam` ⇒ `''` home) flowed unchecked into the `authorized_keys` path, certifying against a phantom `/.ssh/authorized_keys`; the account-dependent legs (`probeSshdPosture`, `probePinnedLine`) now **fail** honestly via a single `configuredAccountUnresolved()` guard, gated strictly on a non-null `ssh_account` so the unset fallback (`runUserHome` may also be `''`) keeps its pre-4977 non-authoritative warn. RED-when-reverted tests for `clientalivecountmax 0`, `maxsessions 0`, and the unresolvable-configured-account case; the unset-empty-home fallback pinned green.

**UPGRADING (DL-223):** The SSH transport is entirely opt-in — absent `board_tools.transport: ssh` (default `http`) and absent a configured `board_tools.ssh_account`, this release is **byte-identical** to v0.66.0 for existing installs. Adopting `transport: ssh` requires a pinned forced-command key; under the documented `sudo bridge:check`, set `board_tools.ssh_account` to the forced-command OS account (unset ⇒ the invoking run-user, byte-identical to pre-DL-224), and sshd must set positive `ClientAliveInterval`/`ClientAliveCountMax`/`MaxSessions` or `bridge:check` FAILs the posture leg. One shipped-surface change applies regardless of transport: a corrupt/unparseable bridge response body now surfaces to the channel client as `isError: true` (was raw text with `isError: false`).

## [0.66.0] - 2026-07-23

**Minor — board-tools default-ON (DL-222): the channel token becomes the default bearer and a complete `board_tools` block turns on without an `enabled` key, with structural default-suppression instead of load throws. Plus the DL-move correlation-stamp fix (DL-221), two runbook additions from aimla's live enablement, and a solo-orientation doc sync.** 4 PRs since v0.65.0 (#362, #360, #361, #363). **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change. ⚠ One config shape flips behavior and the board-tools bearer's authority widens — see UPGRADING.**

### Added
- **#362 (DL-222, card#4907, roundtable #102)** — **board-tools default-ON.** The operator-ratified successor to DL-217's opt-in shape (supersedes-pointers into DL-217/DL-220): (1) **Bearer = the channel token.** `board_tools.auth.token_path` becomes a deprecation alias — honored first, so a pinned `BRIDGE_TOOLS_TOKEN_FILE` / `.mcp.json` never silently flips source — and the default path needs no separate credential and no mint. DL-222 names the blast-radius widening explicitly: the push-auth channel token gains own-lane board authority (own-lane reads + untriaged own-lane creates), bounded by the DL-217 isolation set (own-lane / forced scope / untriaged / loopback-gated), all preserved. (2) **Structural default-suppression — classification precedes parsing.** Throws exist only behind a strict-bool `enabled: true`; every present default-class block that cannot be satisfied (bare `board_tools:`, non-bool `enabled` incl. YAML `yes`, partial scope, no bearer, malformed optional) **suppresses** — `enabled=false` + a `suppressedReason` that `bridge:check` renders as **FAIL** — never a load throw, because a config throw would propagate through `SubscriptionRegistry`→`DispatchService` into an install-wide webhook 5xx storm (6-path fleet-outage regression pinned red-when-reverted). (3) **Channel side:** `BRIDGE_CHANNEL_TOOLS` is tri-state — `1` force-on · `0`/empty off · unset ⇒ advertise iff `BRIDGE_TOOLS_ENDPOINT` is set and a token resolves (with the unreadable-explicit-source short-circuit); snapshot 0.5.3 → 0.6.0 (DL-038). (4) **Stays explicit permanently:** the write-scope block `{board_id, swimlane_id, create_stage_id}` (the swimlane is the agent-keyed read-isolation boundary — deriving it from the repo-keyed DL-027 create-lane is a category error, per the roundtable #102 design arc) and the one `BRIDGE_TOOLS_ENDPOINT` line (deriving it re-opens the DL-220 default-vhost trap or the push-handshake bearer-exfil edge). UDS seats are inert by construction. Bearer **collision, suppression, and unreadable-bearer now FAIL `bridge:check`** (were WARN-class); board-STATE checks stay WARN. Design ran a 7-round fresh-adversarial loop to 0 must-fix, operator-gated twice; the v7 truth table (12 rows) ships as the test spec verbatim. The `.mjs` tri-state advertise + channel-token fallback have no in-repo harness — aimla's first live enablement is the committed live-fire witness (canon #9, on the record in roundtable #102).

### Changed
- **#361 (card#4902, roundtable #102)** — **runbook: multi-user actor callout + loopback-port vhost as a first-class same-box recipe** (docs-only), from aimla's live enablement of the v0.65.0 package: § Same-box enablement now maps steps to actors (root owns the endpoint step; the agent's OS user owns channel env + restart + bearer placement; the bridge operator runs config/mint/check), and a `Listen 127.0.0.1:<port>` loopback-port vhost is documented alongside the `/etc/hosts` pin — no box-global DNS side-effect, no default-vhost Host ambiguity on a multi-vhost box, loopback-only. The channel-server README's over-narrow "needs the /etc/hosts recipe" claim reworded to present both; snapshot 0.5.2 → 0.5.3 (DL-038).
- **docs: solo orientation synced to coord v0.20.0** (#363; `CLAUDE_AGENTBOARD.md` — subagent dispatch REQUIRED + STOP-and-retry on API/limit termination; context-backstop CLEAR auto-fire nudge; Compliance section compressed to owner-pointer).
- **docs: `VERSIONING.md` § Release flow reconciled with the retired PR-open checkpoint** (release-prep commit; the "ask before opening every PR" hybrid-policy line predated v0.62.1's CLAUDE.md rule #5 — PR-opening is pre-authorized; the sole hard gate remains user-merged `main`).

### Fixed
- **#360 (DL-221, card#4852)** — **the DL-resolution move path stamps `pr_number`+`pr_url`, sole-DL-gated.** A DL-only feature PR moved its card but stranded it without PR correlation refs (DL-187's deliberate stance, now superseded with guards), and `pr_url` — a registered payload key driving kanban's multi-repo by-ref `source` derivation — was stamped by **no** path. Now: `stampRefs` emits `stamp_pr_url` (from `pull_request.html_url`); the handler writes `pr_url` add-if-missing exactly like `pr_number` (flat body, DL-219); the DL-win branch threads stamp refs into every per-card target — resolved DL **excluded** (`dl_number` is never re-stamped; identity-poison protection stands) and **sole-DL-gated** (a 2+-DL title is release-shaped; its PR refs are foreign to the cards). The explicit-`card#` path stamps regardless; the `started` push path is byte-identical. Sibling audit: `GitHubPrCardMoveClassifier` is the only `kanban_move_card` emitter; no missed emitter, no double-stamp.

**UPGRADING (DL-222):** a present `board_tools` block with **no `enabled` key** previously no-op'd silently; it now **turns ON** (if its scope is complete and a bearer resolves — the channel token now qualifies by default) **or suppresses + FAILs `bridge:check`**. To stage a block silently, set `enabled: false` explicitly. Explicit installs (`enabled: true` + `auth.token_path` alias + `BRIDGE_CHANNEL_TOOLS=1`, aimla's live shape) upgrade **byte-identically** (test-pinned); to converge on the default path, drop the alias line + token file and let the channel token carry it. Channel servers: `BRIDGE_CHANNEL_TOOLS` unset now advertises tools when `BRIDGE_TOOLS_ENDPOINT` is set and a token resolves (`=1`/`=0` keep their explicit meanings). Also note the bearer-authority widening and the WARN→FAIL check promotions described in #362 above.

## [0.65.0] - 2026-07-22

**Minor — the board-tools same-box enablement package (DL-220): `bridge:provision-tools` mints the per-agent bearer, `bridge:check --probe-tools` live-certifies the loopback+bearer path, the Apache/FPM endpoint runbook lands, and the no-`TrustProxies` gate posture is test-pinned. Plus channel-server dependency bumps and a solo-orientation doc sync.** 3 PRs since v0.64.1 (#357, #356, #355). **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change. Both new commands/flags are additive; absent them, behavior is byte-identical.**

### Added
- **#357 (DL-220, card#4846, roundtable #102)** — **board-tools same-box enablement package**, closing the delivery gaps aimla-pm's first-validation pass found on the v0.63.0 surface. (1) **`bridge:provision-tools {--agent=} {--dry-run}`** mints each enabled agent's `board_tools` bearer at its configured `auth.token_path` — the bearer DL-217 specified as *provision-minted* but `bridge:provision` never delivered: 0600 umask-safe (perms pinned before the secret bytes land), idempotent (an existing secure file is a no-op), fail-loud on insecure perms (DL-010 posture), cross-agent collision detection at mint time (a collision fail-closes both agents at request time — surfaced at provision instead of on the first live call); never edits agent YAML (an explicitly-named agent without the block gets a paste-ready skeleton + non-zero exit). (2) **`bridge:check --probe-tools=<endpoint>`** — opt-in live probe: a real `board_my_cards` per enabled agent over the actual network path (TLS verify on), certifying reachability, bearer→agent resolution, and the swimlane-isolation scope header; failure modes name their cause (403 → the endpoint-recipe trap; 401 → bearer path/collision; connection refused → vhost); a missing/unreadable bearer FAILS the probe (an explicit certification run must not exit 0 over a broken enablement). (3) **`docs/board-tools.md` § Same-box enablement (Apache/FPM)** — the end-to-end runbook, leading with the endpoint trap: `https://<public-host>/…` on-box FAILS the loopback gate (the kernel's local-route source selection makes the TCP peer the box's public IP); the recipe is an `/etc/hosts` loopback pin of the bridge hostname + the real-hostname https endpoint (TLS verification stays ON). (4) **XFF posture pin** — the `LoopbackOnly` gate is sound only while no `TrustProxies` middleware exists (mod_proxy_fcgi forwards the true TCP peer as `REMOTE_ADDR`; a forged `X-Forwarded-For` is never consulted); that posture was documented but unpinned — two new feature tests (external peer + spoofed loopback XFF → 403 with no outbound call; loopback peer + external XFF → admitted) go red the moment a `trustProxies` registration lands, mutation-proven.

### Security / dependencies
- **#356 (card#4838)** — **channel-server transitive dep bumps** (`examples/channel-servers`, lockfile-only): body-parser 2.2.2 → 2.3.0, fast-uri 3.1.2 → 3.1.4, hono 4.12.26 → 4.12.31 — consolidating dependabot #343/#353/#354, which each failed the DL-038 version-bump-guard (dependabot cannot bump the snapshot version, and three separate bumps would conflict). Snapshot version 0.5.0 → 0.5.1; `npm audit --audit-level=high` clean (2 pre-existing moderates on `@hono/node-server` via the MCP SDK, below the gate). The #357 README correction bumps the snapshot again, 0.5.1 → 0.5.2.

### Changed
- **docs: solo orientation synced to coord v0.17.0** (#355; `CLAUDE_AGENTBOARD.md` — human-prompt bookends; repo-agnostic subagent dispatch).

The old pin 422s the wrapped `{"task":{…}}` promote body against a flat kanban (the DL-219 cutover — v0.64.0); v0.20.0 sends flat write bodies matching the flat v3 write API. **This pin runs only on the merge-to-`main` release event, so it takes effect only on `main` — this release activates it.**

## [0.64.0] - 2026-07-21

**Minor — the bridge's `KanbanClient` sends FLAT v3 write bodies (the missed kanban DL-219 companion), plus a PR-title correlation-conflict fix.** 2 PRs since v0.63.0 (#346, #345). **⚠ LOCKSTEP: deploy this simultaneously with the kanban-flat upgrade (v0.36.0) and toolkit v0.20.0 — a wrapped bridge 422s against a flat kanban (and vice-versa); there is no shim.**

### Fixed
- **#346 (DL-219, roundtable #118)** — **`KanbanClient` now sends task-write bodies FLAT (top-level), dropping the `{"task":{…}}` wrapper.** kanban v0.36.0 (its DL-219) unified the v3 write API to flat top-level fields and now STRICT-REJECTS an unknown top-level `task` key (422, no shim) — and the bridge writeback was a **missed consumer** of that cutover (kanban's DL-219 note said "no other known consumers"). All four write sites drop the wrapper: `moveCard` (`{workflow_stage_id}`), `stampCorrelationRefs` (`{payload}`), `setBlockReason` (`{block_reason}`), `createCard` (posts the assembled fields flat). `archiveCard`'s `{"_action":"archive"}` is a top-level control key, unaffected. Unconditional cutover (no version probe) matching the no-shim server change. Exhaustive `app/` audit confirmed these were the only wrapped task-writes (every other `/tasks` call is a GET; `KanbanProvisionClient` posts only to `/webhooks`). Tests updated across 9 files asserting the flat body shape; red-when-reverted (reverting to wrapped reds 56 assertions).
- **#345 (DL-218, card#4815, roundtable-triggered)** — **PR-title / branch card-correlation no longer hijacked by a foreign `DL-NNN`.** When a PR title or branch ref carried a `DL-NNN` that resolved to a card DIFFERENT from a co-present explicit `card#<id>` (e.g. "guard against DL-219 (card#4811)"), the DL silently won and the intended `card#` was dropped. `GitHubPrCardMoveClassifier` now detects the conflict via a shared `cardConflicts()` predicate at all THREE resolution sites (PR move, branch-create `started`, draft overlay), warns loudly, prefers the explicit `card#`, and excludes the foreign DL from being stamped onto it. 4 conflict tests (incl. the bundled-DL edge), all red-when-reverted.

## [0.63.0] - 2026-07-21

**Minor — two-way board tools (DL-217): an agent-initiated request/response surface over the existing channel, plus a release-CI dependency bump and a solo-orientation doc sync.** 2 PRs since v0.62.1 (#341 + dependabot #340). **Opt-in, fail-closed end to end ⇒ absent the new per-agent `board_tools` config AND the channel server's `BRIDGE_CHANNEL_TOOLS=1`, behavior is byte-identical. No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change.**

### Added
- **DL-217 (card #4719, roundtable #102) — two-way board tools.** The bridge gains an agent-initiated request/response surface over the existing channel, exposing two channel-identity-scoped MCP tools so an impl seat with **no kanban token and no toolkit** gets a board window: `board_my_cards` (read-proxy — your own product swimlane grouped by stage + a shared swimlane + coordination cards addressed to you; the kanban token never leaves the bridge) and `board_create_card` (create a card in **your own** swimlane — forced from config, args cannot name a lane; born untriaged). **Opt-in, fail-closed end to end: absent a per-agent `board_tools` block AND absent the channel server's `BRIDGE_CHANNEL_TOOLS=1`, behavior is byte-identical.** New pieces: the Node channel server advertises a `tools` capability + proxies `tools/call` to the bridge over HTTP loopback with a per-agent bearer (dumb pipe — no board logic, no token, no retry); a new loopback-gated `POST /agent-tools/call` (the `LoopbackOnly` middleware gates the TCP peer `127.0.0.0/8` + `::1`, sound under the no-`TrustProxies` posture; the per-agent bearer is defense-in-depth on top, resolved iterate-and-`hash_equals`, fail-closed on unreadable token per-agent and on token collision for both); own-swimlane forced-scope create with reserved-tag (`created-by:`/`idem:`/`id:`/`type:`/bare `triaged`) + idempotency-key charset sanitization and the full DL-198 correlate-before-create + `CardCollapse` idempotency; a fail-closed swimlane row filter on reads. `bridge:check` gains board-tools probes (token readability, token collision, swimlane/stage existence, service-user board membership). **Functional prerequisite:** kanban's `tasks/search.json` needs the `swimlane_id=` q-term (a sibling of `workflow_stage_id=`); an un-upgraded kanban returns empty (broken-but-safe). Docs: new `docs/board-tools.md`, the channel-server README section, the MCP `instructions` string (reworded — channel events stay one-way notifications, the tools are the new request/response surface), and corrections to standing one-way claims in `docs/writeback.md`, `docs/multi-host.md` (incl. the pre-existing FALSE `no-port-forwarding` "still permits `-R`" claim — `no-port-forwarding` forbids both `-L` and `-R`), and `CLAUDE_ARCHITECTURE.md`.

### Changed
- **docs: solo orientation synced to coord v0.10.0** (`CLAUDE_AGENTBOARD.md`; operator-responsiveness rules + the managed orientation block).

### Security / dependencies
- **#340** — **PupFuzz/agent-board-toolkit/promote 0.17.0 → 0.19.0** (release CI, `release-promote-cards.yml`). Interface verified compatible against `promote/action.yml` at the new pin (`1965ee3`) — all five inputs (`writeback-token`, `expected-host`, `api-base`, `dls`, `dry-run`) unchanged; the action runs only on the merge-to-main release event.

## [0.62.0] - 2026-07-19

**Minor — a new opt-in `coord_non_addressed_disposition` inbox-stage seam (DL-215) + a seen-cursor read-modify-write race fix (DL-216), plus a dedup/refactor batch (audit findings F7, F8, F10–F14) and two CI-hygiene fixes.** 11 PRs since v0.61.0 (#319–#329). **No migration, no new required `.env`, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change; the one new feature is opt-in ⇒ absent-config byte-identical.**

### Added
- **#319** — **`coord_non_addressed_disposition` (DL-215, card #4650, roundtable #90):** the `coord-message` family gains the durable-inbox sibling the `impl-ci-wake` family already had (`impl_non_wake_disposition`). A coordination event that fails the recipient gate was unconditionally gate-dropped; the new key (`drop` default | `inbox_stage`) lets it be staged to the durable inbox instead. Under `inbox_stage` the normal coord `Intent` (`coord_issue`/`coord_pr`/`coord_comment`) is built with **no** `channel_push` — a durable record, never a live wake. It softens **only** the recipient gate (`$forMe`): the `subject === null` (unhandled action) and `drop_title_all_of` (noise-title) drops still fire, an addressed event still live-wakes, and `route_intents:true` routes the staged intent with no hand-emit (no wake widening). Any value other than `inbox_stage` falls back to `drop`. Absent key ⇒ `drop` ⇒ byte-identical to pre-DL-215.

### Fixed
- **#320** — **seen-cursor locked read-modify-write closes the #4630 clobber race (DL-216, card #4630):** the DL-199 review deferred a race in the inbox seen-cursor. `BridgePaths::writeSeen` locked only the write, not the read→modify→write around it, and two callers open-coded an unlocked RMW on the same cursor file (`InboxCommand` advance; `RetentionService::pruneSeen`, the DL-212 sweep). A prune landing between inbox's read and its write could write an intersection computed from the pre-advance cursor, dropping a just-marked id back to unseen (consequence bounded — one re-delivered wake, never data loss). New `BridgePaths::updateSeenLocked(path, transform)` — the JSON-array sibling of `filterJsonlLocked` — holds one `flock(LOCK_EX)` across read→transform→write; both callers route through it. `writeSeen` is **retired** (the unlocked-write primitive that let the race exist is gone), leaving `updateSeenLocked` the sole cursor writer. Byte-identical on-disk format; the change is purely the concurrency guarantee. **[card#6025:** there is no `BridgePaths::writeSeen` in the tree today — the entry records the code as it stood.**]**

### Changed
- **#321** — **fold the writeback config-load resolve into `WritebackConfig::loadDefault()`** (card #4500, dedup audit F7). The `config('bridge.config_dir')` → `WritebackConfig::load()` resolve was copied byte-for-byte across 13 sites (6 handlers, `GitHubPrCardMoveClassifier` ×3, `CoordinationClassifier` ×2, plus `ReconcileCommand` and `WritebackAlertNotifier` — the two the audit under-counted, surfaced by canon #7 + review). One shallow `loadDefault(): ?self` folds **only** the resolve; every caller keeps its own fail branch. Zero behavior change.
- **#323** — **consolidate `KanbanClient::idList` (×3) + `preloadStages` (×2) within-file** (card #4503, F10). One `idList` numeric-`id` projection shared by `findCardsByRef`/`cardsByTag`/`boardSwimlaneIds`, and one `preloadStages` fetch+descent shared by `boardStageOrder`/`boardStageIdsByName`; each caller keeps its own fetch/field-guard. The `correlate*` scan loops are left inline (different predicate). No behavior change.
- **#324** — **consolidate the tolerant string-list coercion into `StringList::coerce`** (card #4501, F8). The *non-array → `[]`; scalar → `(string)`, else `''`; never-throws* idiom collapses three copies (`EchoSuppressionConfig`, `SubscriptionConfig`, `AgentRegistry`) into one primitive; the strict `ClassifierConfig` string-list family stays separate by design. No behavior change.
- **#326** — **reuse `SecretFile::permsMessage` for the reconcile insecure-secret refusal** (card #4506, F13). `WebhookProvisioner`'s reconcile-refusal carried the most-drifted copy of the file-perms message (no `mode`); it now reuses the canonical mode-bearing form. Operator-facing diagnostic enrichment only — no accept/reject or control-flow change.
- **#327** — **collapse three plain-text `Response` constructions into `PlainTextResponse::make`** (card #4504, F11). `WebhookController::plain()`, `VerifyHmacSignature::fail()`, and `EnvelopeSizeLimit::tooLarge()` shared a byte-identical `text/plain; charset=utf-8` Response build; one static primitive reproduces it exactly (status, content-type, body all byte-identical). No behavior change.
- **#328** — **consolidate the strict require-mapping config helper into `ConfigMapping::require`** (card #4505, F12). `AgentConfig::requireMapping` and `ClassifierConfig::section` differed only by their `ConfigException` label; one label-parameterized primitive folds both. The deliberately-tolerant `AgentConfig::section` twin is left as-is (a guard test proves it isn't routed through the strict helper). Byte-identical messages and accept/reject.
